# Session archive — 2026-10 (nana-pi)

*Newest first. Frontier in `HANDOFF.md`.*

## 2026-10-20 — nana's readers of nana-pack.json read the file saved as UTF-16 with its byte-order mark, as Windows PowerShell 5.1 writes it

Spend served the current priority's Windows line: nana working in terminal mode for both seats on macOS and Windows, installation and every hook included. Windows PowerShell 5.1's `>` and `Out-File` write UTF-16LE with a byte-order mark by default. Every nana reader of nana-pack.json decoded its bytes as UTF-8, so such a file never parsed; bom-pack-config (2026-10-19) carried that as a residual. The plan's probes on main, in a throwaway clone under a temp home: pi's gate stopped every gated tool, `ls` included, with "gate block is malformed … invalid JSON"; the Claude Code objective producer printed the objective plus "NANA-PACK CONFIG UNPARSEABLE" for `objective.enabled: false`, so the owner could not turn the objective off; `nana-roster --json` and `set` refused with "invalid JSON"; doctor read the pi nana-pack.json row ✗, the default projectFile and the objective file ✗; `project --check` lost the post-edit commands; the adoption settings fell back to the default journal and OBJECTIVE.md.

**The change.** `packages/nana-pack/lib/json-text.mjs` gains `parseJsonBytes`. A buffer that starts FF FE decodes whole, mark included, as UTF-16LE; one that starts FE FF is copied, `swap16`'d and decoded the same way; an odd byte count after either mark throws a SyntaxError before any swap; anything else decodes as UTF-8, as before. The text then goes through `parseJsonText`, which removes exactly one U+FEFF, so R-1586's two-mark rule holds in every encoding. The two marks are contract constants. Decoding and parsing happen in one call, so a bad UTF-16 file lands in each reader's existing catch with its existing fallback and message. Seven readers switch to it: `config.ts` `readConfigFile` (the pi gate and objective extensions and the Claude Code objective producer), `adoption.mjs` `adoptionSettings` (both adoption seats), `nana-roster.mjs` `readRosterFile` and `resolveRoster` (nana-roster, the roster notice, pi-worker and pi-review), `roster-set.mjs`, `doctor.mjs` `piPackConfigState`, `steps.mjs` `readPiPackConfig` (doctor and install's objective seed) and `project.mjs`'s `.pi/nana-pack.json` parse. The pi settings.json readers keep `parseJsonText`. No file that parses on main reads differently, since FE and FF never occur in UTF-8. `nana-roster set` re-serializes the file, so it writes UTF-8, and `.bak` keeps the original bytes. The pack README gains a Config encodings bullet, names marked UTF-16 in the roster's byte-order-mark sentence and says `set` rewrites the file as UTF-8. Rows R-2230 to R-2237 sit in FEAT-015 after R-1586, one per reader path plus the unparseable shapes (R-2236) and the Windows PowerShell proof (R-2237). CAP-04 moves from 489 to 497 rows and FEAT-015 from 128 to 136.

**Evidence.** CI run 39418143889 on draft pull request #128 at 2d2ba429 (build 2 plus the seat's merge of main 0305bc8d): macOS 185 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 187 files (7,943 checks pass); Ubuntu 184 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,882); Windows 170 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,874 pass, 111 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On native Windows pack-config-utf16 passes 5 of 5, including R-2237: a nana-pack.json written by Windows PowerShell's `Out-File` and by its `>` turns the objective off. That was the plan's claim most likely wrong, and it held; build 1's run 39414266878 at 961f76d1 passed it first. None of main's known failures showed. The seat's wrong-code pairs: 19 of 19 red, 0 survived, each exactly on its named titles, on 961f76d1, on build 2 (58e2da48) and on the merged tip 69f0a82e (records `seat-mutate-output-961f76d1.txt`, `-58e2da48.txt` and `-69f0a82e.txt`). Each reader reverted to main's decode turns its own row red, and twelve wrong decoders (odd count allowed, marks sliced, TextDecoder defaults, every mark stripped, no BE swap, no BE branch, NUL sniffing, the UTF-8 mark kept, the marks swapped) turn R-2236, R-2230 or the existing UTF-8 byte-order-mark checks red. The independent check on 961f76d1 HOLDS over nine probes with no MUST. The trace reads 1,619 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 353 modules, 0 problems; readme:check reads 723 claims, 0 problems; requirements-moved lists R-2230 to R-2237 new and implemented in FEAT-015. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/pack-config-utf16-2026-10-20/seat-verify-6cc66daf.md`, with the Windows log and the independent check beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. R-2232 narrowed from "treat it exactly as" to byte-identical `--json` output and a byte-identical file from `set`, which it probed. R-2236's test gained the BE shapes, six in all. Two likely builder mistakes joined the wrong-code list, slicing the mark bytes and TextDecoder's default ignoreBOM, both of which strip two marks. Every child process in the tests sets HOME, USERPROFILE and PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR to the temp home, and the `set` child drops PI_SESSION_ID, so the test neither fails under pi-worker nor rewrites a real file. R-2237 writes both files in one PowerShell run, with one-line JSON under 80 characters. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker (two builds): build 1 db2e4c1b, and build 2 58e2da48, tests and row only, which made R-2237 a real requirement once the Windows leg passed it and a real skip off Windows. Claude Opus 5.5 (the final reviewer, through review-ledger) and pi gpt-6-astra (the land reviewer, through pi-review; the owner moved the land review from Fable to astra on 2026-10-20) review it.

**Known limits.** A UTF-16 pi settings.json stays unreadable to pi and to doctor, because pi owns that file. The frozen desk reads nana-pack.json its own way and still cannot read UTF-16. `install-check.mjs` reads only the file it seeds and is unchanged. ANSI code-page text, PowerShell 5.1's `Set-Content` default, still turns non-ASCII characters into replacement marks. A refusal or gate stop for a still-unparseable UTF-16 file quotes parser text, as on main. The rows sit in FEAT-015; sharing them with other features is the owner's call. Nobody has seen the owner's file: the lane fixes PowerShell 5.1's documented default.

**Carried to HANDOFF.**
- pack-config-utf16: bom-pack-config's line "a nana config file saved as UTF-16 stays unreadable everywhere" drops its fixed half and keeps that nobody has seen the owner's file.
- pack-config-utf16: new lines for pi's own settings file in UTF-16, the desk, the ANSI code page and the 1 MiB room; the byte-order-mark rows question for the owner now names this lane's gate, objective and roster rows.

The frozen desk still reads a UTF-16 file as unreadable, but its save path warns and keeps a backup before it writes, so no data is lost (the plan's known limit). A UTF-16 file has half the room under the 1 MiB bounded-read cap, which no realistic config nears. It did not verify R-2237, which skips on macOS; the Windows CI leg did, on 2d2ba429.

The seat merged main 1207b69e at 69f0a82e, where git merged both sides' `steps.mjs` changes cleanly, and re-ran the pairs there.

- notify-lifecycle-pins (final review): the notify tests' platform helper restores the real platform as soon as its callback returns a promise, so the fake platform covers only the synchronous start of the settled handler. It weakens the test, not the product; a new HANDOFF line.

HANDOFF's existing review-output line now carries it.

HANDOFF's walk line is corrected now; the pack README's "as git also stops there" is a new known-bug line.

## 2026-10-20 — on macOS and Linux, install converges every link and the knowledge hook an earlier install wrote through another spelling of the checkout, and uninstall removes that entry

Spend served the current priority's installation line: nana working in terminal mode for both seats on macOS and Windows, installation and every hook included. The change is macOS and Linux only; Windows output is unchanged. setup-checkout-spelling left two gaps in its one-time convergence of a home an earlier install wrote through another spelling of the checkout, both HANDOFF residuals. The plan's probes on a temp clone of main aa5f25cd (macOS, temp homes), with every link text and hook command rewritten to a letter-case or a linked spelling of the checkout: install relinked 16 links, but spec, py-lint, py-review and py-test printed "unchanged" and kept the old text, because `linkSkill`'s foreign-link branch returned UNCHANGED whenever the real paths matched. Through the linked spelling, install called the knowledge hook "already wired" and kept the link spelling; the case spelling was already rewritten, since the healthy check's JS realpath keeps a typed case. And uninstall printed "settings hook variant skipped left" and left the knowledge entry through both spellings, because `removeInstallerHooks` removed only exact objects. A linked spelling arises when a checkout moves and leaves a link at its old path; install never writes one itself.

**The change.** In `packages/nana-setup/lib/steps.mjs`, `linkSkill` enters the foreign-link branch only when the link's text, resolved against its folder, differs from the source. There it compares `realPath(…, { mustExist: true })` of link and source: a dangling link or another real path stays the PROBLEM "foreign link … — left untouched", and a match falls through to the ordinary relink ("would relink (was …)" on a dry run). `lib/settings.mjs` gains one private predicate, `respelledKnowledgeEntry`: the entry has no `args`, both commands name an installer knowledge target, the commands differ, every other key equals the wanted entry, and both targets are one file under the native lookup, a throw reading false. On win32 the wanted entry has `args`, so the predicate is false there by construction. `removeInstallerHooks` removes an exact or a respelled entry, and `mergeRepoHooks` also replaces a healthy "posix"-form entry whose command differs and whose target is the same file; the win32 branch is unchanged. The healthy check keeps `fs.realpathSync`, now with a comment saying why. `lib/uninstall.mjs`'s `settingsVariants` reads the settings after removal. `scripts/test.mjs` declares the new file's skip on win32, since an all-skip file is a runner failure. The setup README rewrites the earlier-spelling bullet and the uninstall sentence and adds a tests-table row. Rows R-2200 and R-2201 sit in FEAT-013 SUB-012 after R-665, R-2202 in SUB-013 after R-1984, and R-2203 and R-2204 in FEAT-018 after R-1985. CAP-04 moves from 474 to 479 rows, FEAT-013 from 180 to 183 (SUB-012 39 to 41, SUB-013 45 to 46) and FEAT-018 from 22 to 24. The new `checkout-converge.test.mjs` runs each check through the link spelling on every POSIX host and the case spelling where it exists. Its earlier-home fixture fails when it rewrites no link or no knowledge entry.

**Evidence.** CI run 39409313594 on draft pull request #125 at 29b83bf4 (GitHub's merge commit 3f2cfc8a merges 29b83bf4 into its own ancestor c7f582ad, so the tree tested is 29b83bf4's): macOS 179 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 181 files (7,919 checks pass); Ubuntu 178 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,858); Windows 163 PASS, 0 FAIL, 18 SKIP (6,845 pass, 110 skip); install-check passes on macOS and Windows. Windows skips main's declared 17 files and this lane's file, with its declared reason; settings-merge passes 214 with 7 skips there. On Ubuntu checkout-converge reads `5 pass, 1 skip`, the skip the case spelling, so all five checks passed through the link spelling. That settles the plan's inference that Linux behaves as macOS for links. None of main's known failures showed. The seat's wrong-code pairs on 29b83bf4 (macOS, Node 22.22.2, both spellings): 12 of 14 red (record `seat-mutate-output-29b83bf4.txt`, commit d7bc8a46). All eight of the brief's wrong versions are red, and so are the seat's four more. P13, a normal install rewriting its own healthy entry, also turns three settings-merge checks red. P5 (a dangling newly managed link relinked) and P10 (the predicate owning an entry whose target is missing) survived, as declared: no check plants either shape. In the seat's probe the lane's own code handles both right, as main does. The independent check ran on 29b83bf4 and HOLDS over 12 probes with no MUST (details under seat items). The trace reads 1,585 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 345 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/checkout-spelling-converge-2026-10-20/seat-verify-29b83bf4.md`, with the Windows log and the independent check beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. Its MUST: the new file skips every check on win32, and the runner fails a file that passes nothing with no declared skip. So the Windows leg, which blocks release, would have gone red; a platform-keyed entry in the runner's skip map fixes it. It narrowed the rows: "another spelling" reads "another letter case or a link to it", and the two guards name only another copy of the repository. It dropped one wrong-code entry as a duplicate and a win32 guard the predicate already makes needless. It measured two new limits: a checkout moved with no link left still gives four foreign link rows on main and the lane, and forced win32 through a link keeps all six hook entries in the link spelling, so the README must not claim Windows converges a junction. The planner's likeliest wrong claim was that the linked spelling is honest variation in scope: R-2202 is red on main only through it, and on Linux R-2200 and R-2203 rest on it alone. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker (one build): 8d6af832. The seat merged main c7f582ad at 29b83bf4. Claude Opus 5.5 (the final reviewer, through review-ledger) and pi gpt-6-astra (the land reviewer, through pi-review; the owner moved the land review back to astra on 2026-10-20) review it.

**Carried to HANDOFF.**
- checkout-spelling-converge: three setup-checkout-spelling residuals go: uninstall leaving an earlier-spelling knowledge entry, install leaving four skill links in the old spelling, and the settings merge's older lookup with no comment (the build added the comment).
- checkout-spelling-converge: new lines, one each, for the independent check's duplicate knowledge entry; a relative skill link under a home reached through a link, or through the owner's own link, rewritten to the absolute spelling on disk; the knowledge rewrite reported as added, dry run included; Windows unchanged; a moved checkout with no link left; the untested dry runs; a failed strict lookup reading a case spelling as another copy; the repeated test helpers; and the P5 and P10 survivors.

**Seat items from other lanes.**
- checkout-spelling-converge, independent check: it ran on 29b83bf4 and HOLDS over 12 probes, plus the must-meet file (5 PASS) and four neighbouring files at 0 failing. A home installed through a linked or a letter-case spelling converged all 20 links and the knowledge entry at the next install through the on-disk path, where main, on the linked spelling, kept 4 links and the entry in the old spelling. After the case convergence a second install changed nothing, and forced win32 settings and install rows were identical to main's. Its known bug, a respelled duplicate beside the exact entry rewritten into a second identical entry (`created added`; uninstall removes both), and its out-of-scope note, a relative newly managed link under a home reached through a link rewritten to the absolute on-disk path, are HANDOFF residuals.
- checkout-spelling-converge, builder: the builder ran the locked full suite (181 files, 0 failing), which the brief did not ask for; it said to run single test files only. That is the builder's own run, not a seat check.
- checkout-spelling-converge, chain: the planner's notes ask for a merge of main once realpath-strict-fallback has landed, before the final review, because that lane adds a setup README bullet right after the one this lane rewrites. realpath-strict-fallback lands first in the chain, so the seat's chain merge covers it; the notes also ask for a pairs re-run on the merged tip and the macOS install check before the land.
- checkout-spelling-converge, main moved: main is c203f485, after the notify-lifecycle-pins, mutation-runner-takeover-race and handoff-cap-surrogate lands. None touches a nana-setup file or the runner's skip map, and a trial merge conflicts only in the generated code map.

## 2026-10-20 — in pi, a plain rm in or of a bare git repository asks again

Spend served the current priority's pi-seat line: nana working in terminal mode for pi with nana-pi on macOS and Windows; the gate is one of that seat's extensions. It closes a gap that gate-rm-workdir (2026-10-19) opened and declared. That lane's exemption lets a plain recursive or forced rm of literal targets inside the working folder run unasked, and it spots a git repository only by a `.git` entry directly under a target. A bare repository has no `.git` entry. The plan's probe on main 6f0098a8's module: with bare repositories under the cwd, `rm -rf vault`, `vault/objects`, `vault/refs/heads` and `repos/mirror.git/objects` ran unasked headless with 0 dialogs; from a `git clone --bare` folder and the folders inside it, `rm -rf objects`, `refs`, `heads`, `topic` and `wt` did too. Before gate-rm-workdir, the gate asked for all nine.

**The change.** In `packages/nana-pack/lib/gate-rm-inside.ts`, `isBareShape` tests git's is_git_directory shape with lstat only: a `HEAD` file and `objects` and `refs` folders. A new step walks up from the cwd's real path to the filesystem root. At each level a `.git` entry of any type ends the walk first, and only then does a bare shape make `rmInsideCwd` return false, so the gate keeps its ask. Step 5, which already lstats each existing part from the cwd down to the target, now also returns false for a folder part with the bare shape. `lstatOrNull` returns null for a missing entry and rethrows anything else into the module's catch, which returns false. The shape's four names are contract consts beside `PLAIN_RM`. `nana-gate.ts` and `real-path.mjs` are unchanged. Four rows in FEAT-010, R-2240 to R-2243, each with one check in `gate-rm-inside.test.mjs` through the real registered handler, on fixtures git builds: a bare repository or a path inside one asks (R-2240); an rm from a bare repository's folder or a folder inside it asks (R-2241); an rm in a worktree that git added inside a bare repository's folder still runs unasked (R-2242); a folder holding only part of the shape still runs unasked, as target and as cwd (R-2243). The pack README's exemption paragraph adds the guard and swaps its two bare-repository known limits for the plan's. CAP-03 moves from 153 to 157 rows and FEAT-010 from 63 to 67.

**Evidence.** CI run 39414069623 on draft pull request #127 at cc81b85a (build 1 plus the seat's merge of main c203f485): macOS 181 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 183 files (7,929 checks pass); Ubuntu 180 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,868); Windows 166 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,860 pass, 110 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On Windows gate-rm-inside passes 11 with no skip, so Git for Windows makes the same shapes; that was the plan's claim most likely wrong. The seat's wrong-code pairs on cc81b85a: 22 of 22 red, 0 survived, each exactly on its named titles (record `seat-mutate-output-cc81b85a.txt`); M1, main's behaviour, turns R-2240 and R-2241 red. The seat's probe through the real `tool_call` handler: 68 cases matched the plan and none threw; across 176 folder and command pairs, the only differences from main are bare-repository targets. The independent check on cc81b85a HOLDS over 11 probes, with no MUST. The trace reads 1,590 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 347 modules, 0 problems; requirements-moved lists R-2240 to R-2243 new and implemented in FEAT-010. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/gate-rm-bare-repo-2026-10-20/seat-verify-cc81b85a.md`, with the Windows log and the independent check beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. Four wrong versions passed every draft check, and each now fails an amended case: W13 (the shape tested before `.git` at each level) fails R-2242's new `both` case, a `git init` folder whose top also holds the shape; W14 (the walk capped at two levels) fails R-2241's new `refs/heads` case; W15 (step 5's test on the first part only) fails R-2240's new `repos/mirror.git/objects` case; W16 (the walk testing `HEAD` alone) fails R-2243, which now uses each look-alike as the cwd too. A Claude Sonnet 5 subagent built it (one build): 35dfff59, from main a112fd30. cc81b85a merges main c203f485, the handoff-cap-surrogate land. Claude Opus 5.5 (the final reviewer, through review-ledger) and pi gpt-6-astra (the land reviewer, through pi-review; the owner moved the land review from Fable to astra on 2026-10-20) review it. Main has since moved to 0305bc8d (realpath-strict-fallback and post-edit-values-named landed), so the land needs a merge of main and a confirmation round on the merged tip.

**Known limits.** A bare repository below a target still runs unasked, even one level down; the owner's nested-repository trade also leaves a normal repository two levels down unasked. A linked `HEAD`, `objects` or `refs` is not recognised (W12, which accepts links, survives and only asks more). A plain folder inside a bare repository that is not a worktree over-asks. So do a path from outside through a bare repository into its worktree and, on a case-folding disk, a folder with a lowercase `head` file and `objects` and `refs` folders (the seat's probe). Any `.git` entry ends the walk, valid or not, as git also stops there. The walk ignores ceiling folders, filesystem boundaries and `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, so it can only over-ask, and it always runs to the filesystem root. Mount points and inner links keep their existing residuals. The pairs and the independent check ran on macOS only; CI is the Linux and native Windows evidence.

**Carried to HANDOFF.**
- gate-rm-bare-repo: the gate-rm-workdir line "the gate does not recognise a bare repository" goes, because both its halves now ask. The line before it, on the one-level git check, stays as is: it names no bare-repository case, and a bare repository below a target gets its own line.
- gate-rm-bare-repo: HANDOFF held none of the README's four declared limits, so each gets a line: a bare repository below a target runs unasked; a linked head file is not recognised; a plain folder inside a bare repository over-asks, with the other two over-asks; every rm walks to the filesystem root, with its cost.

**Seat items from other lanes.**
- gate-rm-bare-repo (independent check, cc81b85a): HOLDS over 11 probes. Its sweep of 202 cwd and target cases found no case where the lane allows what main asked; all 140 new asks sit under a bare repository. On this machine's real repositories, read-only, nothing over-asks. Cost: 2000 calls from a cwd 30 folders deep took 454 ms on main and 3422 ms on the lane, about 1.5 ms more per rm.
## 2026-10-20 — install holds its settings lock through the PATH entries, so a second install stops at the lock instead of racing the first on a link

Spend served the current priority's installation line: nana working in terminal mode on macOS and Windows, installation and every hook included. `install()` in `packages/nana-setup/lib/steps.mjs` took the settings lock (`.settings.json.nana-setup.lock`) before its first write and released it right after the settings step. Shared memory, pi config, subagent config, the reviewer agent, the knowledge build and the PATH entries all ran unlocked. A second install started in that gap went ahead, and the two runs collided on a link: install-honest-report's independent check saw 1 of 40 staggered pairs crash with EEXIST at the pi-review link, and HANDOFF carried it as that lane's residual, ending "No test pins how long the lock is held". The plan adversary reproduced it on a clone of main 6f0098a8 with a real second process: the lock was gone at the first install's look at `<bin>/pi-review`, the second install exited 0, and the first threw EEXIST (symlink). With win32 forced, main did not crash, but both runs wrote the shims and the last writer won (the seat's probe).

**The change.** `install()` now runs shared memory, pi config, subagent config, the reviewer agent and PATH inside the one `withSettingsLock` call, after the settings step, in main's order; the closure is renamed from `claudeSteps` to `fileSteps`. The knowledge build moves after PATH, outside the lock; the desk step and pi registration follow as before. The lock is taken once and released once, and the dry run runs the same closure with no lock (R-926). A second install started during any file step meets the lock and exits 2 with the existing message (R-1590, R-2080). One visible change in output: the knowledge index row now prints after the four PATH rows, in real and dry runs. The row sets match main on macOS (38 rows) and forced win32 (33). `lib/fsops.mjs` is unchanged. The steps header's `@outputs` and `@errors` and the setup README's lock paragraph name the new span, and the README states two known limits. R-2260 to R-2263 join FEAT-013 SUB-014 after R-1590: a second install started at the first's PATH step exits 2 naming the held lock (R-2260); the first finishes with no PROBLEM row and all four PATH rows created (R-2261); the lock, taken once, is present at every look of the ten later targets (R-2262); the lock is gone when the knowledge build starts (R-2263). Their checks sit in the new `packages/nana-setup/tests/install-overlap.test.mjs`, which starts a real second `nana-setup install --home <same home>` through `spawnSync` at the first install's first lstat of `<bin>/pi-review`. CAP-04 moves from 474 to 478 rows, FEAT-013 from 180 to 184 and SUB-014 from 37 to 41.

**Evidence.** CI run 39413207254 on draft pull request #126 at f52a33fe (build 1 plus the seat's merge of main c203f485; GitHub's merge commit 7afbe8cc merges f52a33fe into its own ancestor, so the tree tested is f52a33fe's): macOS 182 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 184 files (7,929 checks pass); Ubuntu 181 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,868); Windows 167 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,860 pass, 110 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On Windows install-overlap passes 4 in 0.6 s, R-2260 to R-2263 each by title, on the shim branch. None of main's known failures showed. The seat's wrong-code pairs on macOS: 9 of 9 red, each exactly on its named titles, 0 survived (record `seat-mutate-output-f52a33fe.txt`, commit fba92156). ## 2026-10-20 — doctor, the session-start line, uninstall's preview and the land and review tools work where Node's native path lookup fails

Spend served the current priority's installation-and-hooks line on Windows ("installation and every hook included"): it closes setup-checkout-spelling's final-review finding. That lane moved nana-setup's compares against the checkout to `realPath(x, { mustExist: true })`, which rethrew any native error other than a missing file. Node's `realpathSync.native` fails on some Windows virtual volumes where `fs.realpathSync` works; vite's `optimizeSafeRealPathSync` documents EISDIR from ImDisk virtual and RAM disks and switches lookups there. The plan's probes on a clone of main 773de493, macOS, with the native lookup patched to throw: realPath under mustExist threw EISDIR, EIO, EPERM and UNKNOWN; doctor flipped 21 of 48 POSIX rows and 6 of 44 forced-win32 rows from ✓ to ✗; the stale-copy producer printed `INSTALL STALE` on both forms; `uninstall --dry-run` exited 1 with `✗ uninstall preflight problem EISDIR: injected native failure`. The independent check added three more on its base clone aa5f25cd: review-round's key threw EISDIR, nana-land refused a land with the injected error, and install exited 1 on a home holding a legacy `context-size-check.sh` link.

**The change.** One change, in `realPath`'s catch in `packages/nana-pack/lib/real-path.mjs`: on a native error other than ENOENT/ENOTDIR it tries `fs.realpathSync(abs)` first, with or without `mustExist`; only when that also throws does it rethrow the native error under `mustExist`, or return `abs` without it. The missing-file branch is unchanged. This restores the win-review-paths design (its plan-design §1) for strict calls; that lane's SHOULD 5 asked only that `mustExist` stop returning an unresolved path, which R-2181 now pins. No nana-setup code changed: `linkOk`, `skillLinkState`, `knowledgeHookHealthy`, the win32 hook-health rows, uninstall's `repoReal`, preflight and per-link targets, install's retired-hook compares, nana-land, review-round and pi-review all inherit it, and the gate's loose calls do not change. The fallback returns the typed spelling, so on such a volume the spelling guarantees do not hold; the pack README and the setup README say so, and the setup README gains a tests-table row. `packages/nana-pack/tests/real-path.test.mjs` patches `fs.realpathSync.native` in-process and restores it in `finally`. `packages/nana-setup/tests/realpath-fallback.test.mjs` writes a preload into its temp root at run time that throws EISDIR for every native result inside the checkout, starts each failing CLI with `--import`, and fails, never skips, when the preload does not fire, install fails under it or the control doctor shows no ✓ hook, settings or skill row. Rows R-2180 to R-2183 sit in FEAT-015 and R-2184 in FEAT-018 beside R-1985: CAP-04 moves from 474 to 479 rows, FEAT-015 from 124 to 128 and FEAT-018 from 22 to 23.

**Evidence.** CI run 39408045115 on draft pull request #123 at 6cbd0f76 (build 2 plus the seat's merge of main c7f582ad): macOS 180 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 182 files (7,919 checks pass); Ubuntu 179 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,858); Windows 165 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,849 pass, 111 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. ## 2026-10-20 — the pi seat's injected compaction summary ends before a split surrogate pair, not on its first half

Spend served the current priority's pi-seat line: nana working in terminal mode for pi with nana-pi on macOS and Windows. The compaction handoff, `nana-handoff`, is one of that seat's extensions. Its `before_agent_start` handler injected `h.body.slice(0, INJECT_CAP)`, so a summary whose unit 7999 is a high surrogate lost the pair's low half, and the prompt ended on a lone 0xD83D (for an emoji) or 0xD840 (for rare CJK). The store holds only well-formed text, so only the cut created the half. pi-ai's own sanitizer strips a lone half from the system prompt on most providers, but the plan read in the pi 1.0.2 bundle that the openai-codex provider sends `instructions` unsanitized, and every roster model on this Mac ran on it when the plan was written. How that server treats a lone `\ud83d` escape is unmeasured, so the user harm is inferred. handoff-pins had recorded two residuals: this split, and `INJECT_CAP` carrying no provenance note.

**The change.** In `packages/nana-pack/extensions/nana-handoff.ts` the injected body is now `head(h.body, INJECT_CAP)`. `head` is the pack's existing pair-safe cut in `lib/display.mjs`, already used by `objective.ts` and `displayText`: the first n units, minus one when unit n−1 is a high surrogate. `INJECT_CAP` is now exported under `// contract (R-189): the injected compaction summary body cap, in UTF-16 code units.`, following the file's existing `UNADDRESSABLE_MARK` export. R-2160 joins FEAT-002 SUB-003 under R-189: if the cap falls between the two halves of a surrogate pair, the injected body ends immediately before that pair. Its check in `handoff-artifact.test.mjs` compacts `"x" × (CAP − 1)`, then U+1F600, then `tail`, reading the cap by name, and needs the cut to equal exactly CAP − 1 units of `x`. R-189's check stays the cap's one seal with the literal 8000, and build 2 folded an 8500-unit ASCII body into it. R-189's text, the module header, the pack README's "capped at 8000 UTF-16 code units", `POINTER_CAP`, `DAY_MS` and the stalePointer trim are unchanged. CAP-01 moves from 192 to 193 rows, FEAT-002 from 62 to 63 and SUB-003 from 10 to 11.

**Evidence.** CI run 39406330980 on draft pull request #121 at e58bd585 (build 2 plus the seat's merge of main aa5f25cd; GitHub's merge commit bb72716b merges e58bd585 into its own ancestor, so the tree tested is e58bd585's): macOS 177 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 179 files (7,909 checks pass); Ubuntu 176 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,848); Windows 162 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,845 pass, 107 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On Windows handoff-artifact passes 9, one more than in handoff-pins' run, and both cap checks print PASS by title. None of main's known failures showed. The run on build 1 (4f686738) was cancelled as superseded. The seat's probe through real pi 1.0.2 with an offline faux provider: main injects 8000 units ending on 0xD83D, which is not well-formed; the lane injects 7999 ending on `x`; ASCII, CJK, low-surrogate-cut and short emoji bodies give system prompts byte-identical to main's once timestamps are masked. The seat's wrong-code pairs on macOS: on 4f686738, 9 of 10 red, with M4 surviving (record `seat-mutate-output-4f686738.txt`, commit becab95a). M4 retunes `INJECT_CAP` from 8000 to 8001; the R-189 seal's emoji body has a high surrogate at unit 8000, so `head(body, 8001)` steps back to 8000 and the seal passed, where main's raw slice had caught it. On 4011c0a3, 10 of 10 red, each exactly on its named titles (record `seat-mutate-output-4011c0a3.txt`, commit d9494ee6). The independent check ran on build 1 and HOLDS over 11 probes, with no MUST. The trace reads 1,575 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 342 modules, 0 problems; requirements-moved lists R-2160 new and implemented in FEAT-002. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/handoff-cap-surrogate-2026-10-20/seat-verify-e58bd585.md`, with the Windows log and the independent check beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it five ways. Adding R-2160 also needs `npm run req:tree`, or requirements-tree.test.mjs fails 5 R-291 checks. The red-before was misleading: on unchanged code the new check fails only because `INJECT_CAP` is not exported, so the brief does the export first and shows the real red, a cut of 8000 units ending on 0xD83D. The row says "immediately before that pair", matching the check's exact CAP − 1. The user harm is stated as inferred: pi-ai already strips the half on the six other providers the adversary read, and the codex server's reaction is unmeasured. The seat runs the macOS install check before the land, since its real pi turn loads this extension. ## 2026-10-20 — only one waiter at a time takes over a dead shard's mutation-runner lock

Spend served the current priority's Windows line through the seat's own tools. The mutation runner judges the seat's wrong-code pairs, and HANDOFF recorded that its contention check had never taken over a dead holder's lock on Windows. The lane also restores R-1776, an implemented no-overlap row known broken in the single-kill takeover case. `withWorktreeOperationLock` in `packages/nana-pack/lib/mutation-runner.mjs` removed a dead owner's lock by reading it, comparing it and unlinking it (`removeWorktreeLockIfOwned`). Waiter B read dead content X and stalled just before its unlink; A removed X, published its own lock and entered; B then unlinked A's fresh lock, published its own and entered too. The mutation-runner-lock-overlap planner found it. This lane's planner probed a temp copy of main 773de493: two child waiters, with B paused in the existing `unlinkSync` seam, recorded `B found A` for a dead pid and for an old unreadable lock, 3 runs of 3. Pausing B after its first read stayed green, because the removal re-read before it unlinked. The race needs a shard killed while it holds the lock plus two live waiters; git then fails loudly and the shard is refused, so a pair goes missing rather than a verdict going wrong.

**The change.** `lib/mutation-runner-config.mjs` adds `WORKTREE_LOCK_BREAKER_SUFFIX = ".break"`, `chosen` and naming R-2170, listed in `@outputs`, not sealed and not overridable (precedent `LOCK_SUFFIX`). `lib/mutation-runner.mjs` extracts two helpers that the lock and the breaker share: `judgeWorktreeLock` (read, parse, pid alive, unreadable age) and `releaseWorktreeLock` (R-1960's bounded release loop). A waiter that judges the lock dead publishes `<lock>.break` with `publishWorktreeLock`, the lock's own temp-plus-link step, so one waiter at a time holds it. Holding it, the waiter judges the lock again and removes it, compare-then-remove, only if it is still dead with the same content; a `finally` releases the breaker before any wait or throw, and the waiter then waits on a transient result or retries at once. A live breaker holder makes the others wait through `waitOrFail`. A dead or old unreadable breaker is removed by compare-then-remove, and the waiter retries at once. Every breaker file operation goes through the injected `io`. R-1775 and R-1777 to R-1779 and R-1960 keep their values and behaviour. The module's `@effects` names the breaker files, and the pack README's Limits paragraph gains two sentences: only one waiter at a time removes a dead shard's lock, and a waiter killed while removing one, plus a later dead shard, can still let two in. The new `packages/nana-pack/tests/mutation-runner-takeover.test.mjs` pins R-2170 (two child waiters, B paused after its read or before its removal, each with a dead pid and an old unreadable lock; A must signal before B's release), R-2171 (a child that kills itself at its first unlink of the lock; an in-process waiter then enters before the wait limit on a fake clock) and R-2172 (a per-call sweep: one EPERM, EACCES or EBUSY at each read, stat, link or unlink of a fault-free takeover, then a plain takeover of the next dead lock). The rows sit in FEAT-031 after R-1960; FEAT-031 moves from 92 to 95 rows and CAP-07 from 307 to 310. The code map and the tree are regenerated.

**Evidence.** CI run 39406441608 on draft pull request #122 at e943d4de (GitHub's merge commit 6a6d6cdc merges e943d4de into its own ancestor aa5f25cd, so the tree tested is e943d4de's): macOS 178 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 180 files (7,911 checks pass); Ubuntu 177 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,850); Windows 163 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,847 pass, 107 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. None of main's known failures showed. The new file reads 3 pass on macOS (1.4 s), Ubuntu (0.5 s) and Windows (1.3 s), and on Windows R-2170, R-2171 and R-2172 each print PASS by title. So the plan's known limit that Windows behaviour stays inferred until the seat reads the Windows leg is closed. mutation-runner.test.mjs reads 39 pass on macOS (57.1 s) and Ubuntu (20.9 s) and `37 pass, 3 skip  86.3s` on Windows, as on main, with the contention check, the transient lock error check and R-1960 printing PASS there. The builder showed R-2170 red before the change, `B found A` for both before-removal dead-lock kinds. Per its report, W1 (today's code), W2 (no re-check under the breaker) and W3 (a breaker written by plain overwrite) each turn R-2170 red; W4 (a dead breaker never removed) turns R-2171 red; W5 to W8 (the breaker kept after a transient lock removal error, a transient breaker release given up, the breaker never released, the breaker kept after a transient re-read error) each turn R-2172 red. The new file ran green three times on macOS (0.99 s, 0.98 s, 0.95 s). The seat re-ran none. The plan's prototype passed R-2170 in 5 runs of 5 and the sweep's 63 cases in 0.4 s; today's code passed R-2171 and the sweep's 45 cases. No seat pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. `requirements-moved --base main` lists R-2170, R-2171 and R-2172 as new (implemented) in FEAT-031. The trace reads 1,577 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 343 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/mutation-runner-takeover-race-2026-10-20/seat-verify-e943d4de.md`, with the Windows log beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. R-2172 became a per-call fault sweep, because the first plan's first-call-per-path faults missed W8: the re-read under the breaker never got the first-call fault. R-2171 was narrowed to "just before it removes", the only kill point the test reaches. R-2170's pass now needs A's signal before B's release, so a slow Windows runner cannot pass today's code by never reaching the schedule. Fake clocks start at `Date.now()`, since a clock at 0 judged an old unreadable lock fresh. The adversary also corrected a known-limits claim: R-1778's check already takes over a dead-pid lock and an old unreadable lock in-process on every leg, Windows included. The plan rejected rename-to-unique, an inode compare, OS file locks, epoch files and parent-only takeover. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker in one build (cbfb4869); e943d4de merges main aa5f25cd, the mutation-runner-lock-overlap land. Claude Opus 5.5 (the final reviewer) reviews it through review-ledger.

**Known limits.** A dead breaker is still removed by compare-then-remove, so a waiter killed while holding the breaker, a second dead lock and two live waiters with a stall can bring the race back; this is reasoned, not probed, and no check covers it. A live but stalled breaker holder makes the others wait, and their wait-limit line may name the dead lock's pid rather than the breaker holder's. Breaker release reuses the 300000 ms release limit, so on a Windows path that stays delete-pending a waiter can outlast the 30000 ms acquisition limit, the same trade as R-1960. The user gain is small: today the race fails loudly as a missing pair.

**Carried to HANDOFF.**
- mutation-runner-takeover-race: the mutation-runner-lock-overlap plan's takeover-race residual is removed, since this lane closes the single-kill case.
- mutation-runner-takeover-race: mutation-runner-win-lock's Windows takeover residual now says a planner confirmed the race, this lane fixed it, and its two-waiter check passes on Windows.
- mutation-runner-takeover-race: the plan's three known limits, one line each: the dead breaker's compare-then-remove; the wait-limit line's pid; breaker release outlasting acquisition on Windows.

**Seat items from other lanes.**
- mutation-runner-takeover-race build: the builder reported the rulings unavailable on this machine. HANDOFF already says this Mac lacks both rulings copies until the owner's install run, so no line was added and no action follows.
- mutation-runner-takeover-race base: the lane began on 54433844 and its base is aa5f25cd, main when the seat merged it into cbfb4869 at e943d4de. The lane's own change is `git diff aa5f25cd..e943d4de`. Main has since moved to c7f582ad (post-edit-timeout-name and pi-launch-followups landed), which the lane has not merged.
- mutation-runner-takeover-race plan: HANDOFF held none of the plan's three known limits, so each became a residual line.
## 2026-10-20 — tests pin the pi seat's system notices and its session journal lines, and the missing compaction reasons read violated

Spend served the current priority's pi-seat line: nana working in terminal mode for pi with nana-pi on macOS and Windows. `nana-notify` and `nana-lifecycle` are two of that seat's extensions. Five FEAT-023 rows read untested. R-166 (a working OS notifier is used when the agent settles): the 2026-10-04 EARS batch commented out every notifier call and both of the row's cites stayed green, since they checked only that no fallback fired. R-173 (headless notices are opt-in) and R-818 (no escape code without a UI): every notify-fallback fixture set `notify.headless` true, and astra's r1 removed the headless guard with the suite green. R-162 (the five lifecycle journal lines) had no test, and its text was wider than the code: pi 1.0.2 gives each compaction event a reason (manual, threshold or overflow), and nana-lifecycle journals none. In the plan's probes, 12 of 18 wrong versions left notify-fallback, lifecycle-reload and config-handlers-malformed green, and five more turned them red by exit code only, with no FAIL line.

**The change.** Tests, rows, the tree and the code map; nana-pack's extensions, lib and scripts are byte-unchanged. The new `lifecycle-journal.test.mjs` fires the five real handlers once each with a distinct pi reason (start resume, before_compact threshold, compact manual, compact_failed overflow, shutdown fork), reads a temp `journal.path` and filters lines by event name: R-162 needs exactly one line per event with the session cwd and `process.pid`, and R-2140 needs the start and shutdown reasons. The new `notify-dispatch.test.mjs` replaces `node:child_process` `execFile` with a recording stand-in through `createRequire` and `syncBuiltinESMExports` before it imports the extension, fakes `process.platform`, and swaps stdout only around each handler call. R-166 needs one `osascript` call on darwin and one `powershell.exe` toast on win32, each with the Ready for input body, for both a tui and an rpc context, and on Linux one OSC 777 sequence and no process. R-173 needs no call, no stdout and no `notify_fallback` line in print and json mode unless `notify.headless` is true, and then one call on macOS and Windows. R-818 needs no ESC or BEL byte without a UI, even when the stand-in fails with code 1. The no-UI contexts keep a `ui` object, as pi 1.0.2 binds a no-op UI there. R-162 narrows to the lines with cwd and pid, R-2140 takes the start and shutdown reasons, and R-2141 records the compaction reasons as violated. R-166, R-173 and R-818 keep their text and cite the new checks in place of their old notes. product-report's R-1655 check now compares against a violated count its own fixture sets (below). FEAT-023 moves from 45 to 47 rows and CAP-05 from 103 to 105.

**Evidence.** CI run 39404468320 on draft pull request #119 at b5bcf664 (GitHub's merge commit 00097125 merges b5bcf664 into its own ancestor 54433844, so the tree tested is b5bcf664's): macOS 179 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 181 files (7,915 checks pass); Ubuntu 178 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,854); Windows 164 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,851 pass, 107 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On Windows all seven new checks print PASS by title, so the plan adversary's likeliest wrong claim, that the `execFile` stand-in and the faked platform work on the Windows leg, held. None of main's known failures showed. Per the plan, each of W1 to W18 turned its named check red in a temp clone of main 31b95f34; the builder's own tries are in its two reports, and the seat re-ran none. The trace reads 1,576 rows (1,339 implemented, 6 violated), 0 off form; map:check reads 344 modules, 0 problems; requirements-moved lists six FEAT-023 rows. No seat pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/notify-lifecycle-pins-2026-10-20/seat-verify-b5bcf664.md`, with the Windows log beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. W16 (the headless guard keyed on `ctx.mode !== "tui"`) silently dropped OS notices for desk sessions and passed every planned check, so the macOS and Windows R-166 checks now run an rpc context too. W17 (the guard keyed on `!ctx.ui`) passed with the plan's `ui: undefined` fixture, so the no-UI fixtures now carry pi's no-op UI object. W18 (no Linux OSC branch) was red only by exit code, so a third R-166 check covers the Linux TUI. R-173's journal assertion narrowed to no `notify_fallback` line. The adversary also found that a sibling planner's mutation-runner edits had reached its temp clone; they touched only the mutation runner and its test, and the mutation-runner-lock-overlap entry records that planner's re-run. ## 2026-10-20 — on macOS and Linux, killing install ends its pi launch too, and pi output up to 1 MiB a stream reads as pi's own result again

Spend served the current priority's installation line: nana working in terminal mode for both seats on macOS and Windows, installation included. pi-probe-timeout put a relay behind every pi launch of install, its dry run and doctor, and its plan, independent check and review left five known bugs on it. This lane fixes three. First, the relay ran in install's process group while pi ran in its own session, so a supervisor's SIGKILL of install's group (or `timeout -s KILL`) ended install and the relay but not pi. In the plan's probe on a hung fake pi that ignores SIGINT, sh, the fake pi and its child were all alive at the 2.2 s bound, and nothing would ever end them. A SIGKILL of install alone left the orphaned relay waiting out the launch's limit (12 s in the probe). Second, the relay wrote pi's stdout and stderr as one JSON line, which the outer spawnSync capped at its default 1 MiB, so a `pi install` that wrote just under 1 MiB of control characters to each stream read `failed: spawnSync <node> ENOBUFS` on exit 0 and on exit 1. Third, on Windows the launch test file printed PASS after each skipped check, so its summary counted nine passes where seven checks ran.

**The change.** In `packages/nana-setup/lib/bounded-spawn.mjs`, the outer spawnSync that starts the relay passes `detached: true` on POSIX only, so the relay leads its own session and a kill of install's group misses it; the win32 options are unchanged. The launch spec gains `parentPid: process.pid`, and the outer call takes `maxBuffer: RELAY_RESULT_MAX_BYTES`. Two constants join the relay's others, both chosen 2026-10-20 and unsealed: `RELAY_PARENT_POLL_MS = 200` (pi gets its signal within 0.2 s of install's death) and `RELAY_RESULT_MAX_BYTES = 16 MiB` (two streams at main's 1 MiB need 12 MiB after JSON's worst six-byte escape). In `lib/bounded-spawn-child.mjs`, POSIX only, an unref'd interval compares `process.ppid` with `spec.parentPid`; on a change it clears itself and calls `terminate("SIGINT")` only when no termination is in progress, so pi gets SIGINT, the 1 s grace, then the group kill. The deadline, Windows and signal-handler paths are unchanged. nana-setup registers no signal listener, so Ctrl-C now reaches pi through this watch: install dies on the signal, and the relay sees it. Rows R-2130 to R-2133 sit in FEAT-013 SUB-013 after R-2027: CAP-04 moves from 464 to 468 rows, FEAT-013 from 172 to 176 and SUB-013 from 41 to 45. `pi-launch-limits.test.mjs` gains a SIGINT-ignoring switch for the hang fixture, a cleanup delay for the lock fixture, a large-output fixture and the four checks; every win32 skip now returns a skip result, so the runner prints one SKIP line and no PASS. The setup README gains one Idempotence sentence and extends the pi-launch-limits row.

**Evidence.** CI run 39399980825 on draft pull request #120 at 86240f7f (GitHub's merge commit 0825db5e merges 86240f7f into its own ancestor 54f1271f, so the tree tested is 86240f7f's): macOS 176 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 178 files (7,887 checks pass); Ubuntu 175 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,826); Windows 161 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,817 pass, 109 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. pi-launch-limits passes 13 on macOS and 13 on Ubuntu, so R-2130, R-2131 and R-2133 pass on Linux, which settles the plan's most-likely-wrong claim. On Windows it prints 8 PASS and 5 SKIP lines: R-2026, R-2027's lock check, R-2130, R-2131 and R-2133 skip with their reasons, and R-2132 passes through a `pi.cmd` fake pi. The seat's wrong-code pairs ran on macOS, Node 22.22.2: 9 of 10 red (record `seat-mutate-output-86240f7f.txt`, commit 8d4783e4). The pair that drops the parent pid turns R-2026 and R-2027 red as well as the new checks, so the old signal checks now rest on the watch. Pair 6 survived, as declared: a watch for a parent of pid 1 acts the same on macOS, where an orphan's parent is pid 1; under a Linux subreaper or a container's init it could differ (inferred, not run). The independent check ran on 86240f7f and HOLDS over 7 probes with no MUST (details under seat items). The trace reads 1,572 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 340 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/pi-launch-followups-2026-10-20/seat-verify-86240f7f.md`, with the Windows log and the independent check beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. As first worded, the watch called `terminate("SIGINT")` from a repeating interval; its next tick read as a second Ctrl-C and killed at once, cutting pi's 1 s grace to one 200 ms poll, and no planned check noticed, since R-2027's fake pi cleans up at once. A watch that fires once but ignores a termination in progress (a supervisor signalling install and the relay together) did the same. The fix clears the interval and calls terminate only when none is running; the new R-2133, with a pi that takes two polls to clean up, tells it from both. The adversary also added the SIGINT-ignoring switch, since a fake pi that dies on SIGINT let a watch that never kills pass R-2130 and R-2131. It narrowed the feature sentence and the README to install, since no check kills doctor or the dry run. The plan left bug 1 unbuilt: by pi 1.0.2's source, pi takes its settings lock only inside one synchronous read and write, and a local `pi install` runs no npm, so a pi hung at its limit is outside that section. Bug 5, a stuck relay on Windows leaving cmd and pi, has no check here and stays a residual. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker (one build): 8ec28199. Claude Opus 5.5 (the final reviewer) and Claude Fable 5.1 (the land reviewer) review it, both through review-ledger.

**Carried to HANDOFF.**
- pi-launch-followups: two pi-probe-timeout residuals go: a group SIGKILL leaving a hung pi with no limit, and the Windows skip lines that printed PASS.
- pi-launch-followups: four pi-probe-timeout lines are reworded: the combined output cap (now 16 MiB, with each stream up to 1 MiB fitting), a kill of install alone (now Windows only), the deadline kill's missing grace (kept, with the plan's reading of pi's source), and the independent check never re-run on that lane's second build (this lane's check probed Ctrl-C on the merged code).
- pi-launch-followups: new lines, one each, for a second Ctrl-C no longer cutting pi's grace short; Ctrl-C reaching pi up to one poll later, as SIGINT whatever ended install; Ctrl-Z stopping only install; a kill of the relay itself leaving pi; `pi install`'s failed wording where the version checks say could not start; the pair 6 survivor; and the independent check and pairs on macOS only.

**Seat items from other lanes.**
- pi-launch-followups, independent check: it ran on 86240f7f and HOLDS over 7 probes, plus the must-meet file (13 PASS) and two neighbouring files (pi-registration 70/0, doctor-runtime 21/0). Doctor and the dry run get the same cleanup as install when killed: doctor by group, the dry run by group and the dry run alone each ended every process in 1,031 to 1,215 ms; the plan only inferred that. A control-character output of just under 1 MiB per stream that read ENOBUFS on main now reports pi's real status. It did not run Linux or real Windows; CI is the Windows evidence.

Each is a HANDOFF residual, the first two on reworded lines.

## 2026-10-20 — the post-edit check's default time limit is one named setting, and the extension reads it by name

Spend served the current priority's hook line: nana working in terminal mode for both seats on macOS and Windows, every hook included; post-edit is a pi extension in the pi seat. `extensions/nana-post-edit.ts` armed a check whose command set no `timeoutMs` at an inline `c.timeoutMs ?? 30_000`. No test pinned that default. In the plan's probe, `c.timeoutMs ?? 0` (no deadline, so a hung checker hangs the turn) left all six test files that import the extension at 0 FAIL. HANDOFF carried it as a post-edit-win-pin residual, and the coding standards forbid an inline tunable.

**The change.** The new `packages/nana-pack/lib/post-edit-config.mjs` exports `POST_EDIT_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000`, with its provenance beside it: chosen, since the pack's first release (2026-09-01) gave each check 30 seconds, not measured; a command's own `timeoutMs` overrides it, and 0 arms no deadline (R-1702). ## 2026-10-20 — the mutation runner's lock check no longer races its own starting step

Spend served the current priority's CI legs, where a false red costs a re-run and, on the Windows leg that blocks release, a release. On install-cli-pins' CI run 39386603117, attempt 1, the macOS leg failed the contention check in `packages/nana-pack/tests/mutation-runner.test.mjs` (R-1776, R-1777) with overlapText "5:0". Every other field held: 2,400 acquisitions, every worker exited 0, no lock or marker left. Attempt 2 passed, and install-cli-pins touched no mutation-runner code. The check starts with an unreadable gate lock and a gate exclusivity marker. It lifted the lock first and removed the marker second, so a worker that took the lock in that gap found the marker and recorded an overlap the lock did not cause.

**The change.** Tests only, in the contention block of that one file. The gate marker write and its removal after the lift are gone: the check's `enteredFreshUnreadable` read already fails on any worker that enters while the starting lock stands, so the marker added nothing and left an order to get wrong. In the worker script's EEXIST branch each overlap line now names what the worker found in the marker, `a:b found c:d` for a real overlap or `a:b found ?` when the holder had already left. One comment above the `enteredFreshUnreadable` read says why no marker exists during the gate. `lib/mutation-runner.mjs`, the config, the check's title and its R-1776 and R-1777 markers are unchanged; no row, tunable or tree line moved.

**Evidence.** CI run 39399273061 on draft pull request #117 at 8138f592 (GitHub's merge commit 7482a204 merges 8138f592 into its own ancestor 15703d8d, so the tree tested is 8138f592's): macOS 176 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 178 files (7,864 checks pass); Ubuntu 175 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,803); Windows 161 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,799 pass, 106 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. mutation-runner.test.mjs reads 39 pass on macOS (37.8 s) and Ubuntu (19.8 s) and `37 pass, 3 skip  116.7s` on Windows, as on main, with the contention check printing PASS there. None of main's known failures showed. The builder's wrong code in `lib/mutation-runner.mjs` turns the check red both ways: W2 (a fresh unreadable lock not treated as held) with enteredFreshUnreadable true, and W3 (1 in 100 contended publications steal the lock) with overlap text naming what each worker found (`1:1 found 2:0 …`). The plan's load probes ran a standalone copy of the check's gate and worker phase, 8 copies beside 12 busy loops on a 10-core Mac: the planner saw overlaps in 9 of 200 runs with the old order, all 10 lines a first turn that found the gate marker and one of them `5:0`; the adversary saw 2 of 40 (`5:0 found gate`, `2:0 found gate`, `2:1 found gate`) and 0 of 200 with no marker, every run reaching 2,400 acquisitions. The CI attribution stays inferred, since the failing record held no marker content; the new diagnostic makes a repeat prove the cause either way. No seat pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. The trace reads 1,563 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 340 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/mutation-runner-lock-overlap-2026-10-20/seat-verify-8138f592.md`, with the Windows log beside it.

**Plan and build.** The planner's design removed the marker before the lift, with a named pause of four lock retry intervals between the two and a new row pinning that order. One adversary amended it: the marker is redundant, so the lane deletes it, and the pause, the tunable and the row go with it, since they existed only to guard the marker's order (the row also pinned the test's own steps, not a runner promise). While probing, a sibling planner overwrote the adversary's scratch file in the seat's shared scratch folder, so its first runs used the notify-lifecycle-pins planner's temp clone for about 5 minutes; it reverted its edit there, re-ran everything in its own clone, and touched no real repository or home. A Claude Sonnet 5 subagent built it in one build (8f7a91e2); 8138f592 merges main 15703d8d, the pi-probe-timeout land. Claude Opus 5.5 (the final reviewer) reviews it through review-ledger.

**Carried to HANDOFF.**
- mutation-runner-lock-overlap: install-cli-pins' lock overlap residual now names the cause, the starting marker that raced the lift, and this lane's fix, with the cause marked inferred.
- mutation-runner-lock-overlap: a new line for the takeover race the planner found, against R-1776's no-overlap promise.
- mutation-runner-lock-overlap: a new seat-process line for the land suite's 300 s per-file limit, the first thing to fail under load.

**Seat items from other lanes.**
- mutation-runner-lock-overlap, the cause: the check lifted its unreadable starting lock before it removed its gate marker, and a worker that took the lock in that gap found the marker and recorded an overlap the lock did not cause. The planner reproduced it in 9 of 200 loaded runs, every line `found gate`. The adversary's amendment removed the redundant marker instead of reordering it, since the fresh-unreadable read already catches a worker entering early, so the lane adds no row or tunable.
- install-cli-pins, residual: its lock overlap line said the planned mutation-runner-lock-overlap lane would try to reproduce it. The cause is now known and fixed in this lane, inferred for the one CI record.
- mutation-runner-lock-overlap, plan: a separate, real gap stays open against R-1776. When two waiters take over the same dead owner's lock, the takeover reads and then unlinks it, so the slower waiter can unlink the faster one's fresh lock and both enter. The planner's in-process injection probe confirmed it on main. It needs a shard killed while it holds the lock plus two live waiters, and git then fails loudly, so the shard is refused rather than giving a wrong verdict. The planned mutation-runner-takeover-race lane addresses it.
- mutation-runner-lock-overlap, seat process: the planner's 12-way CPU stress probe ran on the seat's machine. The project-check-quiet land then failed on three 300 s file time-outs (review-ledger, templates-render, templates-update; 0 failing checks) at a load average near 158. The install-check-review-launch land failed on two (review-ledger, templates-update; 0 failing checks) at near 67, with a planner looping mutation-runner.test.mjs. The land suite's 300 s per-file limit is the first thing to fail under load; the seat now lands only when the five-minute load is under 20 and no stress probe runs.

## 2026-10-20 — a leftover install lock prints removal commands that run as printed, and doctor reports the lock

Spend served the current priority's installation line ("on macOS and Windows, installation and every hook included"), through the queue's Windows fixes. When a killed run leaves `.settings.json.nana-setup.lock`, `withSettingsLock` in `packages/nana-setup/lib/steps.mjs` printed `  rm <path>` with the path unquoted, on every platform. The plan's probes on a temp clone of main 385c633f, with the fixture home ``nana lock 'q' ’ é $x `b` [1]``: /bin/sh ran main's line from a sentinel folder, deleted the sentinels `lock`, `q` and `é`, exited 1 and left the lock. Command Prompt has no `rm`, and with Git's `rm.exe` on PATH the space splits the path (inferred, not run). On portable PowerShell 7.4.6, main's unquoted path left the lock. Doctor printed no lock row and said `all good.` on an installed home holding a lock; on a fresh home its last line said to run install, which then refused. HANDOFF carried "Doctor does not report a leftover lock" from install-honest-report.

**The change.** The new `packages/nana-setup/lib/settings-lock.mjs` exports `SETTINGS_LOCK_NAME` (a contract value from the setup README's settings.json section), `settingsLockPath`, `lockHolder` (moved from steps unchanged) and `removalCommands(lock, platformName)`. Off win32 it returns one `rm` command quoted by settings.mjs's `shq`. On win32 it returns `del "<lock>"` for Command Prompt and `Remove-Item -LiteralPath '<lock>'` for PowerShell, with each of ' ‘ ’ ‚ ‛ doubled; -LiteralPath keeps `[` and `]` literal, and single quotes keep `$` and the backtick literal. `withSettingsLock` picks by `platform()`, so `NANA_SETUP_PLATFORM` forces it, and prints `  rm '<lock>'` off win32, or four lines on win32: `  in Command Prompt:`, `    del "…"`, `  in PowerShell:`, `    Remove-Item -LiteralPath '…'`. Each command stands alone on its line, and `uninstall --yes` shares the message. Doctor adds a `settings lock` row after the Claude Code settings rows, read with lstat: ✓ `no leftover lock`, or ✗ `<lock> is left over (<holder>) — install will not run until it is removed; if no nana-setup is running, remove it: <fix>`, where `<fix>` on win32 is `in Command Prompt: <del> — or in PowerShell: <Remove-Item>`. The row has no install label, so doctor's last line lists it to fix by hand. The setup README names the forms and the doctor row, and gains two known limits. R-322 is retired, superseded by R-2080. R-2080 to R-2083 sit in SUB-014 after R-322, and R-2084 and R-2085 in FEAT-015 after R-1997. CAP-04 moves from 443 to 449 rows, FEAT-013 from 165 to 169 (SUB-014 from 33 to 37) and FEAT-015 from 113 to 115. Tests: R-2080 in settings-merge, with a forced-win32 half on POSIX hosts; the new `settings-lock.test.mjs` runs each printed line whole from a sentinel folder, through /bin/sh with only `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin` (R-2081), cmd.exe (R-2082) and Windows PowerShell through `-EncodedCommand` (R-2083), and reads doctor's row (R-2084, three checks) and last line (R-2085).

**Evidence.** CI run 39388721628 on draft pull request #114 at 74606671 (GitHub's merge commit 0b7eecf3 merges 74606671 into its own ancestor 385c633f, so the tree tested is 74606671's): macOS 176 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 178 files (7,843 checks pass); Ubuntu 175 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,782); Windows 161 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,779 pass, 105 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On native Windows settings-lock passes 5 with 1 skip (R-2081, POSIX only), and R-2082 and R-2083 print PASS by title: cmd.exe and Windows PowerShell each ran the whole printed line under the awkward home and removed only the lock. settings-merge passes 212 with 7 skips there, R-2080 included. So the plan adversary's claim most likely wrong, that cmd.exe and Windows PowerShell 5.1 behave as planned, held. None of main's known failures showed. Build 3 (cdccc250) changed tests and REQUIREMENTS.md only. CI on the seat's later push, bcfb78ff, had not started when the seat wrote this: GitHub reports the draft pull request as conflicting with main, now 445f4d5b, and runs no pull request workflow then; the trial merge conflicts only in the generated tree. So build 3's two new checks have passed on macOS only. The seat's wrong-code pairs on macOS: on 74606671, 21 of 23 red, with M22 (doctor's win32 row puts each label after its command) and M23 (doctor's row drops the live-install caveat and calls the lock safe to delete) surviving (record `seat-mutate-output-74606671.txt`, commit e6cb4751). Build 3 added two R-2084 checks, and the seat re-ran M22 and M23 alone on cdccc250: 2 of 2 red (record `seat-mutate-output-cdccc250-M22-M23.txt`, commit bcfb78ff). The records' unnamed reds are mostly settings-lock's closing `FAIL summary` line, a table artifact. Declared: the pairs ran on macOS; the PowerShell line's typographic-quote doubling (the builder's wrong code 16) has no pair and is read only by R-2083 on the Windows leg. The independent check ran on 74606671 and HOLDS over 8 probes, with no MUST: a second awkward home under zsh and bash; forced-win32 output and Windows paths with an 8.3 name, `a&b^c!d` and typographic quotes, judged by their text, since that machine had no PowerShell; doctor healthy and with an empty lock; recovery through doctor's own command; `uninstall --yes` under a lock; a mode-000, folder or dangling-link lock with no crash and no fail-open; relative homes; and test hygiene. The trace reads 1,549 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 338 modules, 0 problems. The local macOS install check runs before the land. ## 2026-10-20 — tests pin that a moved agent folder never opens the stranded config file, and that post-edit and lifecycle keep working under a malformed block

Spend served the current priority's both-seats line on macOS and Windows: the pack's config reads, which pi's extensions, the gate, the roster notice and the Claude Code adoption producer share. Three rows read untested. R-193 says that when PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR points at a folder with no nana-pack.json, the default dir's file is never read; astra's r1 probe added `fs.readFileSync(stranded)` to loadConfig, discarded the text, and left agent-dir-config green, since every check judged the gate's effect, not the read. R-825 says no mismatch note or journal line fires when the active config exists or nothing is stranded; dropping `fs.existsSync(stranded)` left the suite green, because no fixture moved the active dir with the default file absent. R-781 says every other extension keeps working under a malformed config; its cites covered only the gate and the objective, never post-edit's own output. Nothing was red, because the behaviour holds: this is a pin lane.

**The change.** Tests, rows and the code map only; nana-pack's lib, bin and extensions are byte-unchanged. In `agent-dir-config.test.mjs`, a helper `opened(run)` wraps node:fs's CommonJS read entry points (openSync, readFileSync, open, readFile, createReadStream, promises.open, promises.readFile), calls `syncBuiltinESMExports` so every `import * as fs` namespace reaches the wrappers, records each path, and restores and resyncs in `finally`; stat, exists, realpath and readlink stay unwrapped. R-193's check runs loadConfig, loadUserObjective, adoptionSettings, the roster notice's session_start and one gate decision inside it. It passes only when the stranded file exists, a UI note names it, no recorded path is it, and the active file's open was recorded. R-825's check gives three sub-cases their own dir and session: a moved empty dir with the stranded file renamed away and restored in `finally`, a moved dir holding `{}`, and the variable unset; every UI note and journal mismatch-line count must be 0. In `config-handlers-malformed.test.mjs`, five unrelated malformed blocks (gate, notify, handoff, objective, journal.path), each beside a failing postEdit command on a.ts, must keep lifecycle's session_start journal line and, except under gate = 7, post-edit's `[nana-post-edit] 1 check(s) failed` feedback; in pi a stopped gate blocks the edit first. The existing objective check gains `// req: R-781`. The three rows flip to implemented with their text unchanged; R-781's Evidence opens with a declared limitation naming notify, handoff, writing and roster-notice. No row added; the R-2110 to R-2119 block stays unused.

**Evidence.** CI run 39389264752 on draft pull request #115 at 7878d8e4 (GitHub's merge commit c06d0565 merges 7878d8e4 into its own ancestor 385c633f, so the tree tested is 7878d8e4's): macOS 175 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 177 files (7,842 checks pass); Ubuntu 174 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,781); Windows 160 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,777 pass, 104 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. R-193's Windows line reads stranded-opened=false and active-opened=true. So the adversary's claim most likely wrong, that `syncBuiltinESMExports` behaves the same off macOS, held there; Ubuntu passes the file too, though the seat holds no Ubuntu log. ## 2026-10-20 — every backslash error in a nana config file gets one repair that fits it and names no config key

Spend served the current priority's Windows line ("on macOS and Windows, installation and every hook included") through the objective block both seats print at session start and doctor's pi nana-pack.json row. win-config-json-path split the config line's repair by V8's error kind, and its two reviews left four HANDOFF residuals on that split. No check reached the internal-error block's config line, where that lane's pair M18 survived.

**The change.** In `packages/nana-pack/lib/json-text.mjs`, `BACKSLASH_JSON_ERROR` also matches `Bad Unicode escape in JSON`, under a contract comment. `FORWARD_SLASH_ADVICE` now reads "Write paths in this file with forward slashes and any other backslash as two, because JSON reads a single backslash as an escape." Its provenance is chosen: it names no key, and the doubling clause is the fix for a regex. It and `MANUAL_JSON_ADVICE` ("Repair the JSON by hand.", a contract value) are exported, and `lib/objective.ts` re-exports both beside `CONFIG_FAILURE_PREFIX`. `configFailureLine` reads `o?.configFailure`, so the producer returns its internal-error marker when called without settings; both callers always pass settings, so no user sees that change. objective-golden and doctor-detail import the two sentences instead of retyping them, and two new checks pin each sentence once. Doctor's row takes the new advice through the shared sentence; the hook launcher, the producer CLI, the extension, `config.ts`, `doctor.mjs` and both READMEs are unchanged. A config that parses, or fails another way, prints what it did before.
