## 2026-10-20 — install, its dry run and doctor stop waiting for a hung pi at a named limit and end every process that launch started

Spend served the current priority's installation line: nana working in terminal mode for both seats on macOS and Windows, installation included. Install and its dry run started `pi --version` and `pi install` through `stepPiRegister` in `packages/nana-setup/lib/steps.mjs`, and doctor started `pi --version` from diagnose in `lib/doctor.mjs`, each a spawnSync with no time limit. A pi stuck on a lock, the network or a broken install hung all three with no end. In the plan's probe on a hanging fake pi, install waited 6,370 ms and the dry run 6,036 ms, and both reported created and exit 0; doctor waited 6,091 ms and printed ✓ 1.0.2. The adversary's dry run was still waiting at 4 s. HANDOFF carried it as an install-dry-run-pi-packages residual. spawnSync's own timeout is not enough: it kills only its direct child, so a sh-wrapped fake pi's node survived, and on Windows cmd.exe dies while pi's node.exe stays.

**The change.** The new `lib/bounded-spawn.mjs` exports `boundedSpawnSync`, which returns spawnSync's shape and runs the launch through a relay, the new `lib/bounded-spawn-child.mjs`. The relay starts pi detached on POSIX, so pi leads its own group, and passes `windowsVerbatimArguments` through; neither the relay nor pi gets `windowsHide`. At the deadline it kills the tree: SIGKILL to the group on POSIX, taskkill with the pack's `TASKKILL_ARGS` on win32, falling back to the child alone. It reports one JSON line and exits, so a pi in uninterruptible sleep cannot hold it; the outer spawnSync waits the limit plus `RELAY_BACKSTOP_MS` (5 s) at most. `PI_LAUNCH_LIMITS_MS = { version: 30_000, install: 120_000 }` sits above `stepPiRegister` (chosen 2026-10-20: pi 1.0.2 answered in 0.15 s, and 30 + 2 × 120 = 270 s stays under the install check's 300 s). On a timeout, install and the dry run report pi packages ✗ `pi --version gave no answer within 30 s and was stopped` (or `pi install <dir> gave no answer within 120 s and was stopped`) and exit 1; doctor's pi executable row reads the same text plus the install hint, ✗ on the real home and a note under `--home`. Build 2 changed the signal path only: on SIGINT, SIGTERM or SIGHUP the relay passes the signal to pi's group, waits up to `RELAY_SIGNAL_GRACE_MS` (1 s, chosen) for pi to close, then kills the tree; a second signal kills at once, and on win32 it waits the same grace before taskkill. The deadline kill stays SIGKILL at once. Windows still launches through ComSpec (R-1034), and a refusal (R-1035) still spawns nothing. Eight rows: R-2020 and R-2022 to R-2027 in FEAT-013 SUB-013 after R-1036, R-2021 in FEAT-015 after R-952, with R-2023 and R-2024 also applying to FEAT-015. CAP-04 moves from 443 to 451 rows, FEAT-013 from 165 to 172 and FEAT-015 from 113 to 114. Tests: the new `pi-launch-limits.test.mjs`, nine checks on a sh-wrapped (or `pi.cmd`) fake pi that starts its own child node, with a 3 s limit. The setup README gains one Idempotence sentence and a tests-table row.

**Evidence.** CI run 39384200809 on draft pull request #107 at 4d04b56e (GitHub's merge commit 8c297be0 merges 4d04b56e into its own ancestor 385c633f, so the tree tested is 4d04b56e's): macOS 176 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 178 files (7,848 checks pass); Ubuntu 175 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,787); Windows 161 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,783 pass, 106 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On native Windows pi-launch-limits prints 9 PASS and 2 SKIP lines. The SKIPs are R-2026 and R-2027's SIGINT check, which also print PASS after skipping, so seven checks ran there. R-2023 is among them: at all four stops the taskkill tree kill ended cmd.exe, pi's node and pi's child. Build 1's CI (run 39379023422 at 4dcc19b5) also read 0 failing on all three legs. The seat's wrong-code pairs on macOS: build 1 went 23 of 24 red, and B3 survived, since the tests could not tell a stop at the limit from the backstop's 5 s later (record `seat-mutate-output-4c67ba2c.txt`, commit 98a81ca7). Build 2 went 27 of 29 red (record `seat-mutate-output-4d04b56.txt`, commit cee89671): B3 is red, the new S1 to S3 are red, and S4 (a second signal during the grace is ignored) and S5 (a child that closes during the grace is reported as an ordinary result) survive, declared, since no check sends two signals or reads the relay's result after a signal. The pairs ran on macOS only; the Windows-only taskkill and verbatim-argument pairs did not run. The independent check ran on build 1, 4c67ba2c, and HOLDS over 11 probes with no MUST and two known bugs. A hung fake pi stopped the CLI dry run and doctor at 30 s with no process left; real pi 1.0.2 gave main's rows, each launch about 30 ms slower; forced win32 kept the ComSpec command line intact. Build 2 fixed its known bug 1 (R-2027). The trace reads 1,551 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 339 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/pi-probe-timeout-2026-10-20/seat-verify-4d04b56e.md`, with the Windows log and the independent check beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. R-2026 sends each of its three signals in its own run, since a relay without a SIGHUP handler left both fake processes alive after a group SIGHUP. The fake pi starts its own child node, and its sh wrapper ends with `exit $?`, so a shell that execs node cannot pass R-2023. The relay and pi drop `windowsHide`, inferred from libuv's source to take the launch off the owner's console, and the relay exits after it reports. The tree file joined the file list, R-2024 became a plain shall row, R-2022 builds its folder from `paths.mjs`'s `repoRoot`, and the arithmetic became 30 + 2 × 120 = 270 s. The simpler design, a detached spawnSync with a later group kill, works on macOS but fails on Windows, where cmd.exe is gone before taskkill can walk its tree, and Ctrl-C would no longer reach pi. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker (two builds): 4c67ba2c, then 5389875b, which fixed the independent check's known bug 1 and the pair survivor B3 (the elapsed bound now sits below the backstop). Claude Opus 5.5 (the final reviewer) and Claude Fable 5.1 (the land reviewer) review it through review-ledger.

**Carried to HANDOFF.**
- pi-probe-timeout: the install-dry-run-pi-packages residual that the dry run's pi version check has no time limit goes; install, the dry run and doctor now stop at 30 s.
- pi-probe-timeout: new lines, one each, for the combined 1 MiB output line (the independent check's known bug 2); the EACCES wording; a pi that leaves a child holding its output reading as stopped at the limit; taskkill missing or denied on Windows; Windows Ctrl-C untested; killing nana-setup alone; a hung pi costing doctor two version limits; one more node process per launch; doctor's other probes unbounded; a pi that leaves its process group; and the pair survivors S4 and S5.

**Seat items from other lanes.**
- pi-probe-timeout, independent check: it ran on build 1, 4c67ba2c, and HOLDS over 11 probes. Its known bug 1, Ctrl-C during `pi install` SIGKILLing pi at once so pi's settings lock stayed behind (pi treats it as stale after about 10 s), is fixed by build 2: the relay passes the signal to pi's group and waits `RELAY_SIGNAL_GRACE_MS` (1 s) before the kill (R-2027). Nobody re-ran the independent check on build 2. Its known bug 2 stays a residual: the relay puts pi's stdout and stderr into one JSON line that the outer launch caps at 1 MiB, so pi output over 1 MiB now reads could not start with ENOBUFS; realistic output is far below. Its most-likely-wrong claim, the Windows tree kill, is now covered by CI's native R-2023 pass. A non-executable pi now reads `spawn pi EACCES` where main said `spawnSync pi EACCES`, and a pi that prints its version but leaves a child holding stdout now reads as stopped at the limit, since the deadline covers held pipes. Added to HANDOFF.
- pi-probe-timeout, deadline kill: the deadline path still SIGKILLs a hung pi at once, with no grace, by design; only a signal gets the grace. Added to HANDOFF with the plan's settings-lock limit.
- pi-probe-timeout, builder: build 1's builder ran the full suite, though its brief said single test files only. Recorded once, on HANDOFF's existing builder-briefs line; no action.
## 2026-10-20 — the install check runs one offline review through each installed pi-review launcher

Spend served the current priority's installation line for both seats, on macOS and Windows. Both seats start their reviewers through pi-review, and install puts its launchers in the bin folder, on Windows since win-path-commands. The install check proved install, the pi session, a live pi turn, the hooks and doctor in a temporary home, but it never ran pi-review. On Windows the only evidence that pi-review starts real pi was a fake pi package in CI (HANDOFF's Roster residual): no real pi had run a review through the new launch there. On main 20debe02 the install check, its config and its test held no `pi-review` or `PI_REVIEW` text.

**The change.** `install-check-config.mjs` gains `PI_REVIEW` after `PI_TURN_POWERSHELL`, with provenance beside every value: the fixture path; its provider `nana-install-check-review-fixture` and model `nana-install-check-review`; the pi arguments built from them (`--offline -e <fixture> --provider <p> --model <m> --no-session -p`); launcher arguments `--retries 0`; one run off win32 (`pi-review`, item `install-check-review`) and two on win32 (`pi-review (cmd)` and `pi-review (sh)`, each with its own item); the `review-repo` and `review-out` folders; the fixture file, text and commit message; git options that give an identity and turn off signing; a plain-words prompt; the answer; the verdict `LAND` and its verdict line; the record; the tally path (review-round's user-home ledger); and the review standard's path. `SPAWN_TIMEOUT_MS` gains `review: 180000`, above one attempt's 75 s stall bound plus its 15 s poll. The new fixture `install-check-review.mjs` registers a faux provider. Its one response writes the system prompt, the user messages and `NANA_ROLE` to the record in the temporary home, and answers with the answer, a blank line and `VERDICT: LAND`. In `install-check.mjs`, after the turn's lines and before `hook set`, the check commits the fixture file into `review-repo` in the temporary home through git commands led by the sealed options. Per run it then removes the record, launches, reads the output, the tally, the record and the checkout's standard, and judges with the exported pure `judgePiReview`, whose FAIL detail names the first failing condition. Off win32 it starts `<home>/.local/bin/pi-review` by that path. On win32 it starts the home's `pi-review.cmd` through `winCommand` under ComSpec, then gives Git Bash one standard-input line that names `pi-review` bare with each argument single-quoted. The setup README's Install check section names the review and what fails it, and says its ledger is the temporary home's. ## 2026-10-20 — the project check prints no module type warning, using doctor's mute

Spend served the current priority's installation line for both seats, on macOS and Windows: all three adopt skills close on the project check (R-1670), so every adoption ends on its output. On main 6f07daee, `nana-setup project <dir> --check` wrote Node's four-line `[MODULE_TYPELESS_PACKAGE_JSON] Warning` naming the pack's `lib/config.ts` to stderr, in a folder with project config, one without and a bare folder. `checkProject` imports config.ts on every run, and the pack's package.json declares no module type. The seeding run and `trust` were already quiet, so doctor-quiet's HANDOFF residual was half right.

**The change.** `muteDoctorWarnings();` becomes the first statement of runProject's `if (opts.check)` branch in `packages/nana-setup/bin/nana-setup.mjs`. It reuses the sealed `DOCTOR_MUTED_WARNINGS` (the SQLite ExperimentalWarning and any MODULE_TYPELESS_PACKAGE_JSON warning) with no new tunable, filter change or rename; the list's provenance comment now names R-2050, and the CLI header's @outputs and @effects name project --check beside doctor. Three rows in SUB-021 after R-1670, each with one check in `packages/nana-setup/tests/doctor-quiet.test.mjs`: R-2050 (a folder with `.pi/nana-pack.json`, so the check reaches project trust), R-2052 (a folder without it) and R-2051 (a preload's unmuted ExperimentalWarning and coded warning still print). The setup README gains one sentence, and its Tests row names project --check. CAP-04 moves from 443 to 446 rows, FEAT-014 from 91 to 94 and SUB-021 from 7 to 10.

**Evidence.** CI run 39384750051 on draft pull request #109 at a7ae9920 (GitHub's merge commit d1497ed3 merges a7ae9920 into its own ancestor 385c633f, so the tree tested is a7ae9920's): macOS 175 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 177 files (7,842 checks pass); Ubuntu 174 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,781); Windows 160 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,777 pass, 104 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. doctor-quiet prints 7 PASS lines on every leg, its six checks and its summary; on native Windows (Node 22.23.3) the three new checks pass by title. That R-2050 and R-2052 fail there before the fix is inferred from the plan's reading of an earlier Windows log; this run's log also carries 13 such warnings from other tests' loads of .ts modules. Per the builder's report, R-2050 and R-2052 failed before the fix on macOS, each with the warning naming config.ts as its first stderr line, and M1 to M6 each turned red exactly their named checks. No seat pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. The seat helper's re-run at the notes commit: doctor-quiet 7 PASS on macOS Node 22.22.2; the trace reads 1,546 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 336 modules, 0 problems; readme:check reads 718 claims, 0 problems. The seat's local macOS install check runs before nana-land. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/project-check-quiet-2026-10-20/seat-verify-a7ae9920.md`, with the Windows log beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it four ways. It added R-2052: Node delivers the warning only after the check's async chain ends, so a mute gated on project config (M6) or installed on the trust branch (M7) still passed the with-config check, and only a without-config check turns red. It made the CLI header name project --check, fixed a typo and named the builder. ## 2026-10-20 — a hung CI test leg stops after 90 minutes, not six hours

Spend served the current priority's CI legs on macOS and Windows, which block release. The root CI suite job set no `timeout-minutes`, so GitHub's 360-minute default applied. A hung leg (a stuck npm, uv, pnpm or pi install, or a runner the 5-minute per-file kill does not end) could hold release for six hours. HANDOFF carried that as a win-leg-flip residual.

**The change.** The suite job now sets `timeout-minutes: 90` at job level, directly after `runs-on`, under a five-line `chosen` comment that gives the measured provenance. One value covers all three legs; a per-OS expression did not earn its complexity. The release and install-check jobs and the README are unchanged. `ci-workflow.test.mjs` gains a contract constant for GitHub's 360-minute default and two checks: R-2060 (exactly one job-level limit, a plain integer above 0 and below the default) and R-2061 (sealed at 90, with `chosen` in the comment lines directly above it). Both rows sit in FEAT-019 after R-1531; CAP-04 moves from 443 to 445 rows and FEAT-019 from 23 to 25.

**Evidence.** CI run 39384632077 on draft pull request #108 at 155ef222 (GitHub's merge commit 7862106b merges 155ef222 into its own ancestor 385c633f, so the tree tested is 155ef222's): macOS 175 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 177 files (7,841 checks pass); Ubuntu 174 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,780); Windows 160 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,776 pass, 104 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. `ci-workflow.test.mjs` passes 15 on every leg (13 on main). Job wall times on that run: Windows suite 32.0 min, macOS 16.1, Ubuntu 11.2. pi-probe-timeout's run 39384200809, the same hour, took 34.7 min on the Windows suite. By the builder's report, nine wrong versions in a temp copy of ci.yml each turned their named check red and left the 13 older checks green: no limit, 360, a per-OS expression, a limit on the Windows test step only and a limit on the release job only turn both checks; 60, "picked" and a blank line above the limit turn R-2061; two limit lines turn R-2060. No seat pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. The trace reads 1,545 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 336 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/ci-suite-timeout-2026-10-20/seat-verify-155ef222.md`, with the Windows log beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it three ways. It raised the value from 60 to 90 after measuring 409 CI runs through the GitHub jobs API, 2026-10-16 to 10-20. The slowest finished legs took 34.5 min on Windows, 18.7 on macOS and 14.5 on Linux. After the Windows leg turned green, its successful runs still grew about 4.8 min a day, as test files on main went from 174 to 196. At 60 that left about 5 days of headroom; at 90, 2.6 times the slowest leg, about 10, and the hang window still shrinks 75%. No hang occurred in 400 suite runs, while a false time-out stops release. It also narrowed R-2061 to the word `chosen`, which a test can check, and dropped a README sentence that restated CI configuration and traced to no row. A Claude Sonnet 5 subagent built it in one build (10dc31bf, 6d87702a); 155ef222 merges main 385c633f. Claude Opus 5.5 (the final reviewer) reviews it through review-ledger.

**Carried to HANDOFF.**
- ci-suite-timeout: win-leg-flip's residual that the suite job has no time limit goes.
- ci-suite-timeout: new lines, one each, for a Linux leg stopped at the limit likely stopping release (inferred, as at 360); the release job keeping GitHub's 360-minute default; the install-check job's 20-minute limit with no provenance comment; and the two new checks reading the suite job as all text between the suite and release jobs.

**Seat items.**
- ci-suite-timeout: the limit changes only what happens when a leg hangs; it has no effect on a healthy run. Its first real test is the next hung leg. Added to HANDOFF.
- ci-suite-timeout: the adversary's 409-run measurement showed Windows legs growing about 5 minutes a day on 10-19 and 10-20, so 90 leaves about 10 days of headroom at that rate. Added to HANDOFF: re-measure the Windows suite's wall time when it passes 60 minutes, because a false time-out cancels a blocking leg and stops release.

## 2026-10-20 — the session-start hooks stop a hung producer after 1.5 seconds, and the verifier hook survives a closed error output

Spend served the current priority's hook line: nana working in terminal mode for both seats on macOS and Windows, every hook included. The Claude Code objective and adoption hooks run their producers through `runHookProducer` in `packages/nana-setup/lib/hook-producer.mjs`, a spawnSync with no time limit. In the plan's probes on a temp clone with stub producers (macOS), a producer that never exited left the hook printing nothing and still running when the probe killed it at 8 s. Claude Code's own 5 s hook limit, the installer's `timeout: 5`, would drop that hook's whole output, the adoption hook's stale-copy line included. Separately, `verifier-pipe.mjs` guarded only its stdout: with stderr closed it exited 1 in 4 of 4 runs on malformed input. The knowledge pull hook had no closed-output check. HANDOFF carried all three as residuals of win-stale-copies and hook-output-fail-open.

**The change.** `runHookProducer` passes `timeout: HOOK_PRODUCER_TIMEOUT_MS` (1500 ms, chosen 2026-10-20 with measured provenance, sealed by R-2013) to its spawnSync. Past the limit spawnSync signals the child and returns ETIMEDOUT, and the existing failure branch prints that producer's unavailable marker, never its partial stdout. The adoption hook's two producers, each limited, end inside the installer's 5 s. `verifier-pipe.mjs` adds a stderr error listener beside its stdout one. The knowledge CLI, `nana-adoption.mjs` and `nana-objective.mjs` are unchanged; a new check pins that the knowledge pull hook already exits 0 with a closed stdout. One setup README sentence names the limit and the wait it does not stop. Six rows, R-2010 to R-2015, sit in SUB-012 after R-1944: CAP-04 moves from 416 to 422 rows and FEAT-013 from 156 to 162. Tests: the new `hook-deadlines.test.mjs` (three checks, hung stub producers in a copied tree, expected text from the checkout's own markers and installed limit) and one new check each in `hook-producers.test.mjs` and `hooks-closed-output.test.mjs`.

**Evidence.** CI run 39377281189 on draft pull request #105 at fe12b848 (GitHub's merge commit afde470b merges fe12b848 into its own ancestor cdf4b098, so the tree tested is fe12b848's): macOS 173 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 175 files (7,788 checks pass); Ubuntu 173 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP (7,733); Windows 158 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,724 pass, 103 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On native Windows hook-deadlines passes 3, hook-producers 11 and hooks-closed-output 7, each new check by title. The seat's wrong-code pairs on macOS went 5 of 5 red, 0 survived (record `seat-mutate-output-fe12b848.txt`, commit 52008906): no limit (main's form), a 3000 ms limit, the partial stdout printed, no stderr listener (main's form), and the knowledge CLI ending by setting the exit code. Declared: the pairs ran on macOS only; the knowledge pair changes a file the lane did not; no check covers a stopped producer's leftover children. The independent check ran on fe12b848 and HOLDS over 12 probes, with no MUST and no known bug. A producer whose detached grandchild holds stdout now gives the unavailable marker at the limit instead of hanging the hook; no current producer starts a child, so real output is never lost in practice. A healthy producer that prints after 1.3 s still prints, and one after 1.7 s gives the marker, the plan's declared limit. Both adoption producers hung under CPU load ended in 3.057 s against 5 s. The real producers, healthy, took at most 120 ms under load. The verifier hook exits 0 with closed pipes where the base exits 1, and its ask decision is unchanged. The install check passed 15 of 15. It did not observe the real Windows kill or a cold first run after a reboot, the plan's known limits. The trace reads 1,518 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 334 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/hook-deadlines-2026-10-20/seat-verify-fe12b848.md`, with the Windows log and the independent check beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. R-2014 and R-2015 narrowed to the closed-reader case their tests exercise. R-2013 names all three producers, and its seal loops over them. The threat model no longer promises to end a hung file read, since spawnSync's timeout signals the child and then waits for it. The README tests-table row was dropped, the hang checks' spawns got a temp HOME, and a seventh wrong code (the limit for the adoption producer only) joined the brief. It chose no async launcher, which would rewrite the R-687 test seam, no per-hook budgets and no SIGKILL. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker in one build (fe12b848). Claude Opus 5.5 (the final reviewer) and Claude Fable 5.1 (the land reviewer) review it through review-ledger.

- hook-deadlines: the stale-copies residual on the adoption producer's missing time limit now says the launcher stops each producer at 1.5 s, and keeps the part left open: a wait no signal ends.
- hook-deadlines: two hook-output-fail-open residuals go, the verifier's unguarded error output (R-2014) and the knowledge pull hook with no closed-output check (R-2015).

## 2026-10-20 — the install check's live pi turn proves post-edit, and on Windows the gate on pi's PowerShell tool

Spend served the current priority's installation-and-every-hook line for the pi seat, on macOS and Windows. The install check's live pi turn, added by pi-seat-live-turn, proved the objective, rulings, writing and knowledge injections and nana-gate's block on a scripted bash call. No real pi turn had run nana-post-edit on any platform, and none had run pi's powershell tool under nana-gate. pi-seat-live-turn's known limits said so: the turn ran only the bash tool and the gate's headless path. pi 1.0.2 keeps the powershell tool off by default (cli.md, windows.md), so without `--tools` the turn cannot call it. On main cdf4b098 nothing in the install check wrote a post-edit command, the win32 turn carried no `--tools`, and neither a `pi post-edit` nor a `pi powershell gate` line existed.

**The change.** `install-check-config.mjs` gains two sealed exports after `PI_TURN`, with provenance beside every value. `PI_TURN_EDIT`: item `pi post-edit`, tool `write`, file `install-check-post-edit.txt`, match `install-check-post-edit\.txt$`, placeholder `{file}`, failure lead `- check `, exit code 3 (chosen: cmd's 1 and a shell's 126, 127 and 9009 read as could-not-run), and a source and an `exited 3` text both built from that code. `PI_TURN_POWERSHELL`: item `pi powershell gate`, tool `powershell`, platforms `["win32"]`, tool list `["--tools","read,bash,edit,write,powershell"]`. `PI_TURN` stays byte-equal. In `install-check.mjs`, after install exits 0, the check reads the temporary home's `nana-pack.json`. Only when it parses as a plain object does it set `postEdit.commands` to one command, `"<node>" -e "<source>" {file}`, keeping every other top-level key and replacing a non-object `postEdit`. A missing or unparseable file stays as install left it. On win32 the tool list goes before the turn's sealed arguments. Two pure judges, `judgePiPostEdit` and `judgePiPowerShell`, name the first missing condition. After `pi turn` the check prints `pi post-edit` on every platform, then `pi powershell gate` on win32 only; a timed-out turn reports `timeout` on all three. The fixture `install-check-turn.mjs` answers request 1 with one assistant message: the bash hit, a write of the sealed file whose content is the user message holding the prompt and run token, and, only when pi's active tools hold powershell, a powershell call with the PowerShell verifier sample. The setup README's install-check paragraph says so and drops "does not cover the PowerShell tool"; the UI confirmation exclusion stays. Five checks and a pin go into `install-check.test.mjs` after the `pin R-1790 to R-1796` check. R-2000's check runs the composed command the way nana-post-edit does on the host, through real cmd.exe with `"%NANA_PI_FILE%"` on Windows. Rows R-2000 to R-2004 sit in FEAT-015 after R-1796; CAP-04 moves from 416 to 421 rows and FEAT-015 from 96 to 101.

**Evidence.** CI run 39377875753 on draft pull request #106 at c21273a8 (GitHub's merge commit 6ef519b8 merges c21273a8 into its own ancestor cdf4b098, so the tree tested is c21273a8's): macOS 172 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 174 files (7,788 checks pass); Ubuntu 172 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP (7,733); Windows 157 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,724 pass, 103 skip), its skips main's declared ones. install-check.test.mjs passes 121 on every leg (115 on main), and on Windows each new check prints PASS by title. Both install-check jobs succeed. Windows prints 18 items, all PASS, with `PASS pi turn`, `PASS pi post-edit` and `PASS pi powershell gate`, pi being the npm `pi.cmd` shim. macOS prints 16, all PASS, with `PASS pi turn` and `PASS pi post-edit`; the PowerShell line is Windows-only by design. So the plan adversary's claim most likely wrong, that pi 1.0.2 on native Windows enables the powershell tool from `--tools` through the `pi.cmd` shim, held. None of main's known failures showed. The seat's local macOS install check on c21273a8, before review, passed all 16 items, exit 0 in 22 s. By the builder's report, each new check turned red on wrong code: dropping the placeholder (R-2000), an unconditional config write (R-2001), accepting a missing token (R-2002), omitting the Windows tool list (R-2003), accepting a non-error PowerShell result (R-2004). The relay names one try per row, fewer than the brief listed. No seat pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. The trace reads 1,517 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 333 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/install-check-turn-edit-powershell-2026-10-20/seat-verify-c21273a8.md`, with both install-check logs beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which rebuilt the design in a temp clone of main and ran a real offline turn on macOS with the installed packages and pi-subagents 0.75.0. Bash came back blocked, the write carried `- check "<node>" -e "..." "<abs>" exited 3: <prompt> <token>`, and powershell came back blocked; doctor then read `✓ pi nana-pack.json`. Its subtraction try, turning powershell on from inside the fixture with `pi.setActiveTools` at session start, hung the turn, so `--tools` stayed. It amended the plan eight ways. R-2000's check runs the placeholder as nana-post-edit does on the host, so the Windows suite leg, which blocks release, runs the cmd.exe path. The source sets `process.exitCode` instead of calling `process.exit`, since an early exit can drop piped output on macOS. The exit text is built from the sealed code, and the placeholder is sealed. R-2003 and R-2004 carry the no-other-platform negative: a macOS `pi powershell gate` line would fail every real macOS run. A non-object `postEdit` is replaced. New exports and checks sit apart from the edits other planned lanes make to the same files. The base moved to cdf4b098. ## 2026-10-20 — both seats and doctor name a nana config file that does not parse, with its place and repair, never its text

Spend served the current priority's Windows line ("on macOS and Windows, installation and every hook included") through the queue's Windows fixes: the objective that both seats print at session start, and the Windows checklist's objective step. HANDOFF carried readme-windows-checklist's review note: step 7 sets `objective.path` in `nana-pack.json`, says nothing about backslashes in JSON, and so a pasted Windows path gives a parse error. The plan's probe on main 2cd533d9 with a temp home showed what followed. The Claude Code hook printed the default `nana-objective.md` block with no notice, since `loadUserObjective` read an unparseable file as no blocks. pi printed the same block, and `loadConfig` set the gate stop, so every gated tool stopped. Doctor read `✗ pi nana-pack.json missing or unparseable: <path>`. V8 on Node 22.22.2 and 24.22.0 alike reports `Bad escaped character in JSON at position 49 (line 2 column 48)`; an unquoted path gives `Unexpected token 'C', …` with a slice of the file, so no new surface may show the parser's message.

**The change.** `lib/json-text.mjs` gains `jsonErrorPlace`, which reads only V8's trailing `(line L column C)` through the contract constant `JSON_ERROR_PLACE`, `jsonParseFailure`, which classifies a bad escaped character or bad control character error as `forward-slash` and every other parse error as `manual`, `configFailureDetail`, the one renderer, and `CONFIG_FAILURE_PREFIX` (`NANA-PACK CONFIG UNPARSEABLE: `). `lib/config.ts`'s `readConfigFile` records that classification from its one existing read, after the BOM strip; `loadConfig` and `loadUserObjective` add `configFailure` (file, place when known, repair kind) to the objective settings, so the hook and the extension are untouched and R-018's byte identity holds by construction. `lib/objective.ts` puts one line right after the heading: the prefix, the file, ` at line L, column C` when known, then "Write objective.path with forward slashes because JSON reads a single backslash as an escape." or "Repair the JSON by hand.", then "Tell the user before spending." It shows beside an UNAVAILABLE marker and in the internal-error block too. A broken file that spells `enabled: false` still prints the objective, since only a parsed user setting turns it off. `lib/doctor.mjs` reads the file once through a local `piPackConfigState`: an unparseable file's ✗ row carries the same detail without the last sentence, and a missing or unreadable file keeps main's `missing or unparseable: <path>`. The root README's Windows step 7 stays one line and adds the forward-slash sentence, `~/` as the user folder, and the example `{ "objective": { "projectFile": "OBJECTIVE.md", "path": "~/umbrella/OBJECTIVE.md" } }`; the pack README's objective contract gains one bullet. Rows R-1970, R-1971 and R-1973 sit at the end of FEAT-001, R-1972 and R-1974 after R-1586 in FEAT-015. CAP-01 moves from 185 to 188 rows and FEAT-001 from 32 to 35; CAP-04 from 416 to 418 and FEAT-015 from 96 to 98.

**Evidence.** CI run 39375725358 on draft pull request #104 at d519cc09 (GitHub's merge commit 11cbedae merges d519cc09 into its own ancestor cdf4b098, so the tree tested is d519cc09's): macOS 172 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 174 files (7,811 checks pass); Ubuntu 172 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP (7,756); Windows 157 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,747 pass, 103 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On Windows objective-golden reads 1008 pass and 6 skip, doctor-detail 66 pass and 1 skip (the macOS-only desk service), fresh-machine 9 pass, and the lane's five checks and six new byte-identity checks pass by title. Build 1's run (82ed4542) was cancelled when build 2 superseded it. ## 2026-10-20 — nana-setup treats every spelling of its checkout folder as that folder

Spend served the current priority's installation-and-hooks line on macOS and Windows ("installation and every hook included"), through its queue line for the Windows fixes, the Windows install shape part. nana-setup took its checkout root from the path node was started through, and judged links and hook targets with Node's JS `realpathSync`, which keeps a typed letter case, a lowercase drive letter or an 8.3 short name. A hosted Windows runner's temp folder is itself spelled `C:\Users\RUNNER~1\…`. The plan's probes on main 6bc2f727 (temp clones, temp homes, macOS APFS, and forced win32): doctor run through the other letter case gave 21 differing rows (22 ✗ against 1), 10 under forced win32; install exited 1 and called the spec, py-lint, py-review and py-test links foreign; install relinked 16 links and rewrote settings.json (forced win32: 6 hook entries and 8 launcher files, each with a backup); the stale-copy line told the user to move nana's own skills. A home written through another spelling read 26 differing rows and an install exit 1; in a git clone, doctor marked `pi package source` ✗ and advised `pi remove` of the owner's own entry; `uninstall --dry-run` exited 1 with "inventory links do not resolve inside".

**The change.** `packages/nana-setup/lib/paths.mjs` computes `pkgRoot` through the pack's `realPath` (`packages/nana-pack/lib/real-path.mjs`, from win-review-paths), the folder's spelling on disk; repoRoot, sources, hook commands, launchers and pi entries follow from it. Each compare that judges a path against the checkout moves from `fs.realpathSync` to `realPath(x, { mustExist: true })`: `linkOk` (stale-copies, shared with the pi rulings extension), `skillLinkState` (doctor), `linkSkill`'s foreign-link test, the two retired-hook compares and `pathVisibleNode`'s candidate (steps), the win32 hook settings rows (hook-health), `knowledgeHookHealthy` (settings), and uninstall's `repoReal`, preflight targets and per-link removal target. `realpathSafe` becomes `realPath(p)`, so pi identity, package source and the PATH row follow. `linkFile`, `linkSkill`'s plain relink and `mergeRepoHooks` stay, and converge an earlier spelling once. The setup README gains a Known-limits bullet for that one-time change and uninstall's leftover knowledge entry, and a tests-table row. Build 2 declares the new test file's skip in `scripts/test.mjs` through `checkoutSpellingSkipReason` in `test-runner-config.mjs`, the test's own case-swap probe, never the platform name, so a case-insensitive Linux volume still runs it; and pi-registration's linked-worktree check compares through `realPath`. Rows R-1980 to R-1985 sit in FEAT-013 (SUB-013 and SUB-014), FEAT-015 and FEAT-018, and R-1986 in FEAT-031. CAP-04 moves from 416 to 422 rows, FEAT-013 from 156 to 159, FEAT-015 from 96 to 98 and FEAT-018 from 21 to 22; CAP-07 from 306 to 307 and FEAT-031 from 91 to 92.

**Evidence.** CI run 39375927343 on draft pull request #103 at 06e9b65d (GitHub's merge commit a5a5bcaf merges 06e9b65d into its own ancestor cdf4b098): macOS 173 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 175 files (7,789 checks pass); Ubuntu 172 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 SKIP (7,728), checkout-spelling skipping as declared; Windows 158 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,724 pass, 104 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. R-1986 passes on all three legs, and pi-registration passes 73 checks with 2 skips on Windows. Build 1's run 39372424797 (6bf3c850) failed Ubuntu's checkout-spelling (`0 pass, 7 skip — no check passed and no skip was declared`) and Windows's pi-registration linked-worktree check, besides main's mutation-runner lock check, since fixed. The seat's wrong-code pairs on 6bf3c850 (macOS, POSIX form and forced win32): 10 of 14 red, each by exactly its named titles; M5, M6, M7 and M11 survived, each declared; M14, main's code at every site, turns all six red, the only pair for R-1981 and R-1983. The independent check on 6bf3c850 HOLDS over ten probes with no MUST. ## 2026-10-19 — doctor's last line names install only for the ✗ rows install would repair

Spend served the current priority's installation line, for both seats on macOS and Windows: doctor is the check a seat reads after install, and its last line sent every ✗ row to install. On a temp copy of main 531ff1ec (the plan's probes, macOS), runDoctor printed `<n> missing — run: nana-setup install` whatever the ✗ rows were. An `install --home` without pi-subagents read `1 missing — run: …`, though install never installs pi-subagents. A foreign folder at `skills/spec` plus two removed rules read `3 missing — run: …`, though install refuses spec. A settings.json of `{"hooks": {},}` read `34 missing — run: …`, though install aborts before any write. A forced-win32 real home printed the same beside ✗ `PATH resolves pi-review`, and install cannot put the bin folder on the Path. No test pinned the line.

**The change.** In `packages/nana-setup/bin/nana-setup.mjs`, runDoctor, only when some row is ✗, calls `refuseCwdRelativePiHome(layout, "this install")` and then `install(layout, { dryRun: true })`. A ✗ row is install's when a dry-run row labelled `row.installLabel ?? row.label` reads created or updated; every other ✗ row is a fix by hand. The last line takes one of four forms: all install's, byte-identical to main's line; none, `<n> missing — fix by hand as each ✗ line says: <labels>`; mixed, the same labels then `; then run: nana-setup install for the other <m>`; and a SetupError refusal, `<n> missing — nana-setup install will not run until this is fixed: <first refusal line>`. Labels print once each, in row order, and every form keeps main's ` (and see the <w> ! above)` suffix. Build 2 added a fifth: any other dry-run error prints `could not predict nana-setup install repairs (<code>) — <n> missing — run: nana-setup install`, after every row, with exit 1 and no stack. In `lib/doctor.mjs`, diagnose's `add` takes an optional install label, stored only when given; three rows pass one, because install names them differently: the private rule, the shared memory index and the pi objective file. Rows with no install step (the node floor, pi executable, pi-subagents, mcp.json, the project file, PATH resolves pi-review, pi package source, the desk service) are by hand by construction. The `all good` line, the `!`-only line, every row and every exit code stay as they were. The setup README gains one paragraph and a tests-table row. The new `tests/doctor-fix-line.test.mjs` holds eight checks, R-1990 to R-1997, which sit in FEAT-015 after R-1766; CAP-04 moves from 416 to 424 rows and FEAT-015 from 96 to 104.

**Evidence.** CI run 39373655522 on draft pull request #102 at b6c5007e (GitHub's merge commit dda56575 merges b6c5007e into its own ancestor bdc65eba, so the tree tested is b6c5007e's): macOS 172 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 174 files (7,786 checks pass); Ubuntu 172 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP (7,731); Windows 157 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,722 pass, 103 skip), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. doctor-fix-line passes 9 on every leg, its eight checks and its closing tally line, and on Windows each of R-1990 to R-1997 prints PASS by title. So the plan adversary's claim most likely wrong, that native Windows behaves as forced win32 does on macOS, held: there `install --home` leaves only pi-subagents ✗, and install refuses the foreign spec folder. Build 1's run 39371938014 at 87efebe6 read 0 failing on macOS and Ubuntu; its Windows leg was cancelled when build 2 superseded it. The seat's wrong-code pairs on macOS went 15 of 18 red on 87efebe6, with D1, D2 and D3 surviving (record `seat-mutate-output-87efebe6.txt`, commit 1d107467). Build 2 killed D2, and the re-run on f44248c7 went 18 of 20 red (record `seat-mutate-output-f44248c.txt`, commit 76a4ea5d): D2 and the new D4 and D5 turn R-1997 red. D1 (the dry run skips the relative agent folder refusal) and D3 (a by-hand label printed twice) stay declared survivors. Every red pair also shows one unnamed red, the file's closing `FAIL summary: <n> failures` tally line. The independent check ran on build 1, 87efebe6, and HOLDS over 13 probes with no MUST. Its known bug A: on the forced-win32 copy path an unreadable rule copy made the dry run throw EACCES, a non-SetupError, and doctor printed a stack trace where main printed a summary. Build 2 fixed it with R-1997. Its known bug B, a linked `.claude` whose refused skills the line sends to ✗ lines that say to run install, stays a residual; main's line was no better. Its probe 2 measured doctor at 0.54 s on the lane against 0.35 s on main, the extra `pi --version` through the dry run. The trace reads 1,515 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 332 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/doctor-fix-line-2026-10-20/seat-verify-b6c5007e.md`, with the Windows log and the independent check beside it.

## 2026-10-19 — the review tools' tests wait for a reviewer's whole launch record

Spend served the current priority's Windows line through CI's Windows leg, which blocks release, and through the macOS suite every land runs. The review tools' tests failed in both with no real defect. immutable-review's fake reviewer wrote its launch record in two steps, the header and then the argv, and the test took the file's existence as a whole record: on a loaded Mac at install-pins' land it read the header alone, and two checks failed with `observed prompt text empty` and `standardOccurrences 0`. Every watched wait was a fixed 5 s: Windows run 39348142806 failed reclaim with `killedCheckout ""`, because the killed review had not started its reviewer in time. In the same run the final `rmSync` threw an uncaught EBUSY on the test's temporary folder, and the runner's FAIL-only excerpt never showed it. review-ledger's signal section waited the same 5 s, then read the pid file whole; a read between the file's creation and its write gives pid 0, and `process.kill(0, 0)` always succeeds.

**The change.** Tests only. A new helper, `packages/nana-pack/tests/launch-wait.mjs`, exports `awaitLaunchRecord(file, { closed, isComplete, limitMs, pollMs })` and two chosen tunables: `LAUNCH_RECORD_WAIT_MS` 60 s (twelve times the old wait, above the slowest whole check seen, 34.7 s on Windows) and `LAUNCH_RECORD_POLL_MS` 25 ms. It reads first, returns the text once the caller's completeness test holds, and returns null only after the launcher has closed or the limit has passed. immutable-review now runs one Node fake body on every platform, through a sh wrapper that execs it on POSIX so pi-review's pid stays; the body writes the pid file, then the whole record to `<OBSERVE>.part` in one write, and renames it onto OBSERVE. Its dirty-state, signal and reclaim launches wait through the helper with each launcher's close promise, and the signal check signals only a running launcher. The final `rmSync` retries 5 times 100 ms apart (both chosen), then prints one FAIL line, `the test's temporary folder is removed at the end`, with the error code and path. The stall check stays as win-watchdog-cpu-read left it; the stale "until W7c" comments now describe today's code. review-ledger's section 7 makes its exit promise at spawn, waits for `/^\d+\n$/` in the pid file, signals only a running launcher, and fails by name on a missing record. The new `review-test-waits.test.mjs` holds the five checks. R-1923 and R-1924 call the helper directly. Then one child run of immutable-review, under a runtime-written CommonJS preload, starts every watched pi-review 10 s late, sleeps 1 s before each write or rename onto the record, and makes the final recursive `rmSync` throw EBUSY. R-1920 and R-1921 read the child's PASS lines; R-1922 needs one named FAIL line, no stack line and an exit status equal to the FAIL count. A rename of immutable-review's environment names or watched titles fails the meta check by name. Rows R-1920 to R-1924 sit in FEAT-031; CAP-07 moves from 300 to 305 rows and FEAT-031 from 85 to 90.

**Evidence.** CI run 39369629052 on draft pull request #101 at f53cf02e (main and the merge-base are both 6bc2f727): macOS 172 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 174 files (7,781 checks pass); Ubuntu the same file counts (7,726); Windows 156 PASS, 1 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,716 pass, 1 fail); install-check passes on macOS and Windows. The one Windows failure is main's mutation runner lock check (35 pass, 1 fail), which mutation-runner-win-lock fixes; this lane touches no mutation runner file. The lane's files: immutable-review 18 pass on macOS and Ubuntu, 16 pass and 2 skip on Windows; review-ledger 165 pass, and 163 with 2 skips on Windows; review-test-waits 5 pass on all three, in 45.2 s on macOS, 37.7 s on Ubuntu and 50.4 s on Windows, where the plan estimated up to 200 s. The plan's probes on a temp clone of main d4423eff (macOS): today's immutable-review under the preload gave 13 PASS, 5 FAIL and an uncaught EBUSY; the fix under the preload gave 18 PASS and the named cleanup FAIL, exit 1, in 46 s; a plain run gave 18 of 18 in 13 s. The builder turned each new check red on wrong code: a fixed five-second wait (R-1920), a split fake write (R-1921), an uncaught cleanup (R-1922), returning on existence (R-1923), ignoring closure and the limit (R-1924). No seat pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. No suite check re-runs review-ledger under load or with a late launch; the plan's probe E (section-7 launches 6 s late crashed on an uncaught ENOENT after 67 passes on main) and R-1923's pin of the wait are its evidence. The trace reads 1,511 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 333 modules, 0 problems. ## 2026-10-19 — the mutation runner's lock check passes on Windows, and a lock release gets its own five-minute limit

Spend served the current priority's Windows line through the queue's urgent-first item: CI's Windows leg blocks release, and main failed the mutation runner's lock contention check there in most runs. mutation-runner-win-prune (landed 32d6aa8b) added a cross-process worktree-operation lock; its contention check R-1776 runs 8 child workers for 300 acquisitions each and needs zero overlap, complete ownership and nothing left on disk. The seat's scratch run 39357872086 (draft PR #92, main d4423eff) failed it in 11 of 12 hosted Windows runs. It did not fail every run: hook-output-fail-open, install-dry-run-pi-packages, knowledge-unreadable-root and win-silent-skips each read 0 failing on Windows with the lock in their tree, and main's own CI run 39368683280 at 6bc2f727 failed it again. Build 1 followed the first brief's leading hypothesis, a release that meets Windows' delete-pending EPERM and gives up at the 30 s acquisition limit, leaving a live owner's lock behind; it made release retry with no limit. The scratch run 39364310814 at 0ad43c1d still failed 10 of 12. The seat decoded the ten failure records (table in `build-brief-2.md`): every failing run had workers SIGKILLed by the test's own 30 s per-child timer, 2 to 8 of 8 per run; every recorded overlap came in a run with kills, almost all from the highest-numbered workers, the last spawned and so the last killed; the lock and marker left on disk follow from the kills. The 12 runs reached 1,927 to 2,350 of 2,400 acquisitions in 30 s, so the timer killed workers mid-section, and a surviving worker then took over the dead pid's lock, found its marker and recorded an "overlap". That reading is the seat's, inferred from the records; build 2's passing runs fit it.

**The change.** In the test, for R-1776 only: the contention block gets `CONTENTION_BUDGET_MS` = 120 s, defined once with its measured provenance (four times the 30 s window the 12 runs measured). At the budget the test writes a stop file each worker checks between acquisitions, never inside the critical section, waits `CONTENTION_STOP_GRACE_MS` (5 s, chosen), then SIGKILLs only the workers still running; the per-child safety timer becomes budget + grace + 5 s. The failure record gains `timedOut` and `killedWorkers`, a timed-out run fails as a timeout, and its overlaps print marked as recorded after a kill, not as a lock failure. The strength is unchanged: 8 workers, 2,400 acquisitions, zero overlap, complete ownership, the observer. In the product: `withWorktreeOperationLock`'s release no longer stops at the 30 s acquisition limit, which left a live owner's lock behind for every waiter. It retries until the lock is gone or the new sealed `WORKTREE_LOCK_RELEASE_WAIT_LIMIT_MS` passes (300,000 ms in `mutation-runner-config.mjs`; chosen: ten acquisition windows for Windows' delete-pending state, still bounded), then reports one line naming the lock and the limit and throws. New row R-1960 pins both halves through the lock's injection hooks: a release that meets EPERM past the acquisition limit still releases, and a child whose release always fails stops at an injected limit with one report and an error, its lock left. R-1778 now covers acquisition only, and its check drops the release half; the sealed-values check pins the new limit. CAP-07 moves from 300 to 301 rows and FEAT-031 from 85 to 86.

**Evidence.** The seat's scratch run 39368865667 (draft PR #99) at the merged tip 0a449584 passed the file in 12 of 12 hosted Windows runs, 37 pass and 3 skip each, 97.9 to 133.4 s per run. Draft PR #98, 3a564fc4 alone, never ran: it conflicted with main. CI run 39368972192 on draft pull request #100 at 0a449584 (GitHub's merge commit c23faaa8 merges 0a449584 into its own ancestor 6bc2f727): macOS 171 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 173 files (7,777 checks pass); Ubuntu 171 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP (7,722); Windows 156 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,713 pass, 103 skip), its skips main's declared ones, mutation-runner 37 pass and 3 skip in 117.2 s; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. The cleanup check, which failed once in 44 Windows runs before mutation-runner-win-prune, passed in all 36 of the seat's scratch Windows runs and in CI. The builder reports each new or changed check red on wrong code: the old 30 s kill with no stop file (the build-1 record: 10 of 12 Windows runs failed), release with no limit, release cut at the acquisition limit, and an unsealed release limit; only the first is in the review folder. No seat wrong-code pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. The trace reads 1,507 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 331 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/mutation-runner-win-lock-2026-10-19/seat-verify-0a449584.md`, with the CI Windows log and both 12-run logs beside it.

**Plan and build.** An urgent fix that ships alone, hardened later: no plan file and no adversary; the two build briefs are the plan of record, rows R-1960 to R-1969 reserved and R-1960 used. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker in two builds: build 1 (0ad43c1d, release retries with no limit) and build 2 (3a564fc4, the test's budget and stop file, and the release limit). The branch took main once (0a449584). Claude Opus 5.5, the final reviewer, reviews it through review-ledger.

## 2026-10-19 — on Windows, pi-review and pi-worker no longer stop a busy pi as stalled under a decimal-comma format

Spend served the current priority's Windows line for both seats: pi-review and pi-worker run every review and every build in either seat, and HANDOFF's queue puts the Windows fixes first. On Windows the watchdog read pi's CPU time as `(Get-Process -Id N).CPU`, a double that Windows PowerShell prints with the user's decimal mark. Under a decimal-comma format such as German, `Number("0,6416708")` is NaN, which the watchdog turned into 0. Every poll then read `cpu=0s`, the CPU looked flat, and the watchdog stopped a busy pi as stalled: every review and build on such a machine. The seat's probe P1 confirmed it on a hosted Windows runner (scratch run 39355882657, main 27fb9d3c's code, an en-US runner): Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100 printed `1,5` and the CPU value `0,90625` under an in-process de-DE thread culture; after `Set-Culture de-DE`, a new PowerShell printed CPU `0,5`; and main's win32-launch check `Windows: a busy pi is not stopped as stalled` failed, with `cpu=0s` lines and STALL. Two smaller faults sat beside it on every system. A failed read returned 0 and main's ps parse turned `n/a` into NaN, so a broken reader read either as a flat zero or as never flat. And a pi that exited during a read could end as STALL when the read repeated the last time, which the plan's adversary reproduced; Linux keeps an exited child's time until its parent reaps it.

**The change.** `pi-watchdog.mjs` exports `parseCpuSample(raw, platform)`, which returns seconds or null: on win32 a trimmed whole count of 100-nanosecond ticks divided by the new sealed `WIN_CPU_TICKS_PER_SECOND` (10,000,000 in `worker-config.mjs`, its contract .NET's TimeSpan.TicksPerSecond); elsewhere a trimmed ps time `[dd-][hh:]mm:ss[.fraction]`; any other text null. The win32 read now runs `(Get-Process -Id N).TotalProcessorTime.Ticks`, an Int64 that prints as digits in every format; both reads stay `execSync` by name through the shell, and a thrown read gives null. After each read the poll loop yields once with setImmediate; if pi has exited, the attempt ends by pi's own exit with no cpu line. Otherwise a null sample prints `cpu=unreadable flat=n/m`, counts as flat and keeps the last readable time. The plan ruled that an unreadable sample counts as flat: "no sample" would let a dead reader switch stall detection off, and pi-review has no wall-clock ceiling. The pack README's review-runner paragraph says both in two sentences. The new `watchdog-cpu.test.mjs` holds the five checks: R-1890 (Windows only) wraps the real Windows PowerShell 5.1 in a `powershell.cmd` that sets a de-DE thread culture, proves the wrapper prints `1,5` for `[double]1.5`, and needs a busy pi to print four cpu lines, each with positive seconds, then exit 0 with no STALL; R-1891 and its seal pin the parser; R-1892 cycles a fake reader through good, `n/a`, good and failing reads; R-1893 releases pi during a read that then fails or repeats the last time. The fold, in the immutable-review test only: its stall check's fake pi lives a chosen 180 s and its guard waits a chosen 120 s, both 30 s before, since run 39348142806's Windows leg printed one cpu line in 30 s. Rows R-1890 to R-1893 sit in FEAT-007's SUB-008 after R-1395, shared with FEAT-006; CAP-02 moves from 180 to 184 rows, FEAT-007 from 121 to 125 and SUB-008 from 34 to 38.

**Evidence.** CI run 39362479464 on draft pull request #93 at 3e998bf2 (GitHub's merge commit 4b859b60 merges 3e998bf2 into its own ancestor 8a9ff9a3, so the tree tested is 3e998bf2's): macOS 167 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 169 files (7,735 checks pass); Ubuntu the same file counts (7,680); Windows 151 PASS, 1 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,675 pass, 1 fail), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. The one Windows failure is main's mutation runner lock check, which the urgent mutation-runner-win-lock lane fixes; this lane touches no mutation runner file. On that en-US runner watchdog-cpu passes 5 and printed `PASS R-1890: on Windows a busy pi runs to its exit when PowerShell writes a decimal comma`; win32-launch passes 15 and printed `PASS Windows: a busy pi is not stopped as stalled`; immutable-review passes 16 with 2 skips, the fold's stall check included. So the seat promotes R-1890: its test marker becomes a `req:` marker, and the row reads implemented with that CI run as evidence. Build 1's CI run 39358574887 failed R-1890 on its own precondition: on the en-US runner the control printed `1.5`, while the run itself rose from 1.44 to 6.11 s of CPU and succeeded with no STALL. Build 2 runs the control through cmd's shell, so its PATH lookup reaches the wrapper, as the product's own read already did. The seat's P1 re-run on build 1's tip under a de-DE user culture (scratch run 39358595464) passed win32-launch 15, the busy-pi check included, and watchdog-cpu 5; build 2 changed only the test file. R-1893 passed natively on all three Windows runs, so the adversary's next likeliest wrong claim, that one setImmediate yield sees a Windows exit, held there. The seat's wrong-code pairs on macOS went 17 of 18 red on 62627846: H1, a ps parser that ignores the hours field, survived, since every ps case then carried a zero hours field. Build 2 added `01:00:00` and `1-02:03:04`, and the re-run on 3e998bf2 went 18 of 18 red, each by exactly its named titles, 0 survived. No pair ran on Windows, so no run has shown R-1890 red on the old `.CPU` read; the Windows evidence is CI and the two P1 runs. The independent check ran on build 1, 62627846, on macOS only, and held over eleven probes with no MUST: the parser reads real ps output and refuses comma, point and empty Windows text; macOS ps prints a point under German and French locales; busy and idle fake pis succeed and stall with the real ps; a forced-on Windows tick read behind a fake `powershell` sends the tick expression and behaves the same; and a pi that exits during a real ps read ends by its own status. ## 2026-10-19 — on Windows, install repairs Claude Code hook settings carried from a Mac or Linux home

Spend served the current priority's installation-and-hooks line on Windows ("installation and every hook included"), through its queue line for the Windows fixes, the Windows install shape part. A settings.json copied or synced from a Mac or Linux home is the likeliest Windows starting point. The plan's probe on main 32d6aa8b, a temp clone, a temp home and forced win32: install printed `already wired` for the objective, adoption, shared-memory and roster notice hooks, though all four named a missing `<home>/.claude/hooks/…`; the `Bash` verifier stayed and install appended a second one under `Bash|PowerShell`; the first installer's unquoted knowledge entry, `NODE_NO_WARNINGS=1 node <path> hook`, also read `already wired`. Doctor then read ✗ on 5 settings rows, and a second install changed nothing. The causes, in `lib/settings.mjs`: win32 `installerTarget` had no shell form for the five Node hooks and no unquoted knowledge form, `mergeRepoHooks` scanned only the wanted matcher, and `hasHook` read the dead shell form as present. HANDOFF's stale-copies-hooks residual carried the doubled verifier.

**The change.** `installerTarget` gains two win32-only forms. `posix-shell` takes the five Node hooks with no `args` when the command tokenizes to exactly `node` and a win32-absolute target ending in the POSIX installer's default hooks folder and script (`POSIX_INSTALLER_HOOKS_SUFFIX`, a contract value) and equals `node ${shq(target)}` byte for byte. `posix-unquoted` takes the knowledge hook when its argv is `NODE_NO_WARNINGS=1`, `node`, a win32-absolute target with the knowledge suffix, and `hook`, and the command matches byte for byte. Both reuse the existing in-place replacement, which keeps an entry's other keys. For the verifier, `mergeRepoHooks` on win32 also scans the PreToolUse groups whose matcher is `POSIX_VERIFIER_MATCHER` (now named in `desiredHooks` too), splices each timeout-5 installer-shaped verifier, drops a group only when its own splice emptied it, never rewrites a matcher, appends `{...moved, command, args}` under `WIN_VERIFIER_MATCHER` only when no installer-shaped verifier is already there, and counts the move as a repair, so a move-only repair is written. The header rules comment records this one exception to "foreign matcher-scoped groups are left alone"; POSIX output is unchanged. The setup README's hooks row and "Install never removes or reorders" bullet name the repair and the move, and its tests table lists the new `tests/win32-hook-repair.test.mjs`, eleven checks run under forced win32 on every leg with no skip. Rows R-1880 to R-1889 sit at the end of FEAT-013's SUB-013, after R-1208; R-316's evidence names the win32 exception and R-653 gains the look-alike pin. CAP-04 moves from 387 to 397 rows, FEAT-013 from 130 to 140 and SUB-013 from 22 to 32.

## 2026-10-19 — on Windows, eight test files print a skip line naming each check they run only off Windows

Spend served the current priority's Windows line: nana working consistently on macOS and Windows, with CI's Windows leg, which blocks release, as the standing proof. Eight test files guarded 21 checks with `process.platform !== "win32"` and no else. On Windows, 20 of them vanished without a word, so the leg read green with less covered than it showed; the 21st, test-locked's second guard, printed `SKIP signal process-group wait on native Windows`, which did not name its check. In the plan's evidence run (CI run 39344916736), each of these files read fewer pass plus skip on Windows than on macOS.

**The change.** Each guard keeps its line and gains the branch Windows takes, printing one `SKIP <check title> — <reason>` per guarded check: no mkfifo, file symlinks needing Developer Mode, NTFS ACLs, no /tmp alias, pgrep and POSIX process groups, a POSIX-shell git stand-in, no hook files in a Windows install, and Windows killing a child outright. test-locked's existing SKIP is reworded to name its check. A new `packages/nana-pack/tests/windows-skip-lines.test.mjs` reads the eight files as text and fails a file when any guarded check lacks its own SKIP literal, starting `SKIP <title> — ` with a reason, on the Windows branch. Rows R-1950 to R-1957 sit at the end of FEAT-031. Tests and the ledger only; no product code changed.

**Evidence.** CI run 39361973111 on draft pull request #96 at a7ed70b2 (GitHub's merge commit onto 8a9ff9a3, its own ancestor): macOS 167 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 169 files (7,739 checks pass); Ubuntu the same file counts (7,684); Windows 152 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,679 pass, 98 skip), its skipped files main's declared ones. install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On Windows the touched files print their SKIP lines: adoption-producer 1, adoption-reader 2, bounded-read 2, post-edit-hardening 6, requirements-lift 2, requirements-regroup 4, test-locked 4, win32-degrade 1; windows-skip-lines passes 8 there and on the other two legs. Each file's pass plus skip on Windows now equals its macOS count, and Windows skips rose from 78 to 98. Lines from the log include `SKIP b: no orphaned grandchild survives the kill — pgrep and POSIX process groups`, `SKIP SIGINT releases the full-run lock — Windows kills a child outright, so its signal handler never runs` and `SKIP seam does not leak: a normal run links the hooks — a Windows install links no hook files`. The plan's win32-degrade counts (92 on Windows, 93 on macOS) came from main before win-hook-rows changed that file; at the lane's base it reads 86 on Windows, and now 86 plus 1 against 87 on macOS.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. It found an eighth file, test-locked, by comparing every file's Windows and macOS counts, and added R-1957. It tightened the match: a SKIP must start with `SKIP <title> — `, carry a reason and serve one title, so one line naming three checks no longer passes. It moved the inverted-guard wrong version to its two-line form and recorded the one-line form as a known limit. A Claude Sonnet 5 subagent built it in one pass (3dae9f8c), and the seat merged main 8a9ff9a3 cleanly (a7ed70b2). An ordinary lane: no wrong-code pairs and no independent check. Claude Opus 5.5, the final reviewer, reviews through review-ledger. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/win-silent-skips-2026-10-19/seat-verify-a7ed70b2.md`, with the Windows log beside it.

## 2026-10-19 — on Windows, Claude Code gets the spec, py-lint, py-review and py-test skills as managed copies

Spend served the current priority's queue line for the Windows fixes, its Windows install shape part: the priority asks for both seats on Windows, installation included. On Windows install skipped spec, py-lint, py-review and py-test (`– skill spec skipped (win32)`), so a Windows Claude Code seat had none of /spec, /py-lint, /py-review and /py-test, which macOS and pi have. Doctor printed each as a `·` note, and the session-start stale-copy checker skipped them. The skip was a written design choice of 2026-10-07 (R-665, R-666), made before anyone tested Windows; the plan searched the rulings, memory and review records and found no owner ruling behind it. The plan's probes on a clone of main 32d6aa8b, under a temp HOME with win32 forced: an owner's folder at spec or py-test got install, its dry run and doctor exit 0; a captured legacy spec copy stayed in place with no backup; a regular file at requirements read "copy is stale … re-run", and a link at spec read `·`.

**The change.** In `packages/nana-setup/lib/steps.mjs`, stepSkills' win32 skip of the four and the unused `skip` helper are deleted, so install mirrors all five skills through requirements' copy path, each with the `.nana-setup-copy` marker; linkSkill's doc comment follows. The retired step's win32 skip of the four is replaced (build 2) by a branch that leaves a link or a directory holding the marker at those names silent, on every platform; any other folder there keeps its row. ## 2026-10-19 — four Claude Code hooks exit 0 when the reader of their output closes it first

Spend served the current priority's hook line: the priority names installation and every hook, for both seats, on macOS and Windows. On main d4423eff, the objective, adoption, shared-memory and verifier hooks exited 1 with an unhandled `write EPIPE` when the reader of their output closed it before they wrote. The adoption hook did so only when it had something to write; in a bare home it prints nothing and exits 0. The objective, adoption and shared-memory headers promised a successful exit. HANDOFF's tranche-5 residual carried the shared-memory half. The roster notice hook already exited 0, because its `process.exit(0)` runs before the stream's error event fires. Nobody has seen Claude Code close a hook's output early; the one concrete early reader is a person piping a hook into `head`. The adversary called the lane's user value modest and the change worth building, since it makes the hooks' written promise true.

**The change.** Each of `nana-objective.mjs`, `nana-adoption.mjs`, `nana-shared-memory.mjs` and `verifier-pipe.mjs` in `packages/nana-setup/claude/hooks/` adds one `process.stdout.on("error", () => {})` after its imports, with a comment naming its row. The listener ignores every stdout error, not only EPIPE, since Windows may report a closed reader as EPIPE or as EOF. Each `@errors` header line gains the closed-output clause. `lib/hook-producer.mjs`, `lib/verifier-hook.mjs` and `nana-roster-notice.mjs` are unchanged: no process-wide handler, no new module, no tunable, no README change. The new `packages/nana-setup/tests/hooks-closed-output.test.mjs` runs each of the five real hooks twice in a throwaway home: once reading its output, which must exit 0 and print, and once with the parent's read end destroyed right after spawn, which must exit 0 with empty stderr. So no check can pass on a hook with nothing to write. Rows R-1940 to R-1944 sit at the end of FEAT-013's SUB-012, after R-1207; R-1943, the roster notice, is a pin. CAP-04 moves from 387 to 392 rows, FEAT-013 from 130 to 135 and SUB-012 from 28 to 33.

**Evidence.** Before the fix, the builder's run of the new file on main's hooks gave four FAIL lines, each `open=0 closed=1 stderr="node:events:497"`. On native Windows all five checks pass by title. That shows each hook prints with its output open and exits 0 with empty stderr after its reader closes. It cannot show which error Windows raised, or that it raised one: no run of main's hooks and no pair ran on Windows. The seat's five wrong-code pairs on macOS went 5 of 5 red, each by exactly its named title, 0 survived: M1 to M4 each delete one hook's listener (main's form), and M5 deletes the roster notice's `process.exit(0)`. Declared without a pair: macOS only, the ignore-every-error choice, and the header comments. The independent check held over eleven probes with no MUST. Main's hooks piped into a reader that exits at once exited 1 on every run, and the lane's exited 0 with empty stderr. A 1.28 MB shared memory index piped into a reader that closes after 10 bytes exited 1 on main five times of five and 0 on the lane; a slow full reader still got every byte. Its three known bugs are HANDOFF residuals: K1, nothing pins the ignore-every-error choice, since an EPIPE-only listener passes the file on macOS but exits 1 on the EOF error; K2, the rows drop the plan's "write nothing to stderr" and the test titles differ from the plan's; K3, the knowledge hook, a closed stderr, the work done after the close and the adoption producers' missing time limit. ## 2026-10-19 — the knowledge build keeps the search entries of a root or folder it cannot list

Spend served the current priority's "every hook included" line for both seats on macOS and Windows: the knowledge build, which the Claude Code knowledge hook and pi's knowledge extension start in the background once the index is over an hour old. walk() in `lib/build.ts` swallowed every listing failure, and the caller counted the walked root as scanned, so the purge loop deleted every row under it. The plan's adversary probed main 32d6aa8b in a temp clone with a temp HOME on macOS: four files indexed, one deleted, root a and folder b/locked at mode 000. The build printed `removed 3`, status printed `ok` for a, and no line named either path. So a root that was briefly unlistable (a permission change, a macOS privacy-protected folder read without access, a Windows deny rule) emptied its part of the index until a later build.

**The change.** Build 1 (802c8478): walk() records every directory it cannot stat or list, the root or a folder under it, whatever the error code, in a new `BuildStats.unreadable`. The purge loop keeps a configured root's row, counted as preserved, when its root was not scanned or its path lies inside an unreadable path; it reuses `contains` from `lib/sources.ts`, now exported, so the path-boundary check stays one copy. The build command prints `  <path>  UNREADABLE (skipped, rows kept)` per path after the MISSING lines and before the summary, which setup still reads as the last line holding "files ". status marks a root MISS when stat fails, UNREADABLE when `fs.opendirSync` refuses a directory, and ok otherwise. Build 2 (08ba23e3): status opens only a directory, pinned by new row R-1914 (a readable single-file root reads ok); under `--rebuild` the MISSING and UNREADABLE lines say "(skipped; the rebuild dropped its rows)", pinned by new row R-1915; R-1913's and R-883's checks test the status line's prefix instead of the absence of "ok"; walk's root-level `return false` and the caller's `if (!walk(...)) continue` are gone, since the purge loop already keeps every row under a recorded root. The README's missing-root paragraph now covers unlistable roots and folders. One new test file, `unreadable-root.test.mjs`, denies its folders by chmod 000, or on Windows by `icacls <dir> /deny *S-1-1-0:(RD)`, and prints a named SKIP for each denial check unless stat works and readdir fails on every denied folder. incremental-build gains six `req:` markers only (R-883 and R-207, three each). Rows R-1910 to R-1915 are new in FEAT-003; R-207 and R-883 flip from untested to implemented with their text unchanged. CAP-01 moves from 179 to 185 rows and FEAT-003 from 35 to 41.

**Evidence.** CI run 39360453683 on draft pull request #89 at 08ba23e3: macOS 167 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 169 files (7,732 checks pass); Ubuntu the same file counts (7,677); Windows 152 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,667), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. unreadable-root passes 7 on macOS and Ubuntu. On Windows it passes 2 and skips 5: R-883 and R-1914 pass, and R-1910 to R-1913 and R-1915 skip by their named reason, "readdir unexpectedly succeeded", since the hosted runner's account lists a folder behind the deny. So the unreadable-folder behaviour is proven on macOS and Linux only. Build 1's run 39357305834 (fd0e7ab5) was also 0 failing on all three legs (Windows 152 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP). The seat's wrong-code pairs went 14 of 19 red on fd0e7ab5, with M19, M3, M8, M4 and M5 surviving, and 14 of 16 red on 08ba23e3, each by exactly its named titles. M19 now turns R-1914 red; M3, M6, M7 and M8 are deleted with the code they changed; M4 and M5 survive because each needs a race. The independent check ran on build 1 and held over eleven probes with no MUST: main purged the unlistable root and folder, the lane keeps them and still purges the deleted file, a macOS privacy-protected folder behaves like the chmod case, and the hook's detached build keeps the rows main purged. Its two known bugs, A and B, are fixed in build 2. The trace reads 1,465 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 327 modules, 0 problems. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/knowledge-unreadable-root-2026-10-19/seat-verify-08ba23e3.md`, both Windows logs beside it.

## 2026-10-19 — the README gains a Windows checklist for Command Prompt, and the Windows install check runs it as written

Spend served the current priority's installation line on Windows: the priority names installation on macOS and Windows, and HANDOFF's queue named this lane, waiting on pi-seat-live-turn because both edit the install check. The root README carried a numbered fresh-machine checklist for macOS only. A Windows reader got one prose paragraph, which said the documented pack and project commands run in PowerShell or cmd "(untested there)". The install check rehearsed only the macOS list (`platforms: ["darwin"]`), so on win32 it printed no `readme checklist` line. The plan's probes on main f0d6da24, in a temporary clone and HOME: `parseChecklist(readme, "win32")` ignored the platform and returned the macOS step 8. `runReadmeRehearsal({platformName: "win32"})` spawned nothing for a Windows-only README; with both headings it spawned `/bin/zsh` with no verbatim flag, and its environment held `Path=C:\caller` beside `PATH=<home>/.npm-global/bin:`. Node documents that on Windows the first key in sorted order wins, so the caller's Path would have been lost.

**The change.** The root README gains `### Fresh machine (Windows), in order` after the macOS list, with the same HTML comment and eight steps written for Command Prompt: Node ≥ 22.19 and Git for Windows; the CI-pinned `npm i -g --ignore-scripts …@1.0.2 --before=…` and `pi --version`; `git clone … "%USERPROFILE%\nana-pi"`; `cd /d` to the clone and `node packages/nana-setup/bin/nana-setup.mjs install`; `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin` on the user Path in Environment Variables, doctor's own remedy words, with no Run span; pi-subagents at doctor's floor; the objective files under `%USERPROFILE%\.pi\agent`; and `cd /d`, `nana-setup trust "%USERPROFILE%\nana-pi"` and `nana-setup doctor`, expecting `all good`. Prerequisites links both lists, and "(untested there)" now covers only commands outside the Windows list. In `install-check-config.mjs`, `README_REHEARSAL.platforms` becomes darwin and win32; `heading`, `shell` and `shellArgs` become per-platform maps, the win32 shell the bare `cmd.exe` that step 1 opens, with `/d /v:off /s /c`; new `npmPrefixBin` (`bin` on darwin, the prefix itself on win32) and `binOnPath` (win32 only), each with its provenance. `parseChecklist` and `runReadmeRehearsal` take the platform as an argument, never `process.platform`, since the Windows suite leg runs the darwin cases too. On win32 each step gets exactly one Path key, in the caller's letter case, holding the home npm prefix, the home's bin folder and the caller's Path joined with a literal `;`, and runs as one verbatim `cmd.exe /d /v:off /s /c "<joined>"` call. The bin folder stands in for step 5, which writes the registry and cannot run in a temporary process environment. darwin's call and environment are unchanged. `install-check.mjs` passes the platform through, one line. The setup README's install-check section covers both shells, the prefix layout, the step-5 stand-in and Linux printing no checklist line. R-951, R-1111 and R-1546 now say "the macOS fresh-machine checklist", R-1542 gains "WHERE the platform is darwin,", and R-1540's test loops darwin and linux under a new title.
