## 2026-10-19 — tests pin how review-ledger run refuses a broken, linked, folder-shaped or unwritable review ledger before a review starts

Spend served the current priority's both-seats line on macOS and Windows: the review ledger, which every review in either seat passes through and whose round count nana-land trusts. A broken ledger that slipped through could grant a free review or end in a stack trace. The 2026-10-04 gap audit left seven SUB-010 rows untested: R-843 and R-855 (a malformed or incomplete tally line refused, naming file and line), R-852 (a lock path that is a folder), R-723 ("every ledger path shall be a regular file", false for the reservation folder, a folder by design), R-844 ("a symlink" with no subject, its unwritable half skipped on Windows), R-725 (an untracked in-tree --out warns, runs and stays out of its snapshot) and R-845 (an outside or ignored --out stays silent). Nothing was red, because the behaviour works: this is a pin lane.

**The change.** Tests, rows, the tree and the code map only; `review-round.mjs`, `review-ledger.mjs` and `pi-review.mjs` are byte-unchanged. In `review-ledger.test.mjs`, R-843 marks the corrupted-line check, and R-855's new check plants a valid line first, so its diagnostic must read `rounds.jsonl:2:`. Section 10 declares `AT_ONCE_MS = 3000` (chosen: a fifth of the ledger's 15 s lock wait, under the 5 s orphan takeover). R-1841 folds the three per-file symlink checks into one over all four ledger files, the lock added; each case must be refused under AT_ONCE_MS with no "admitted" note, the target untouched and no stack. R-1840 is new: the tally, the audit and the previous audit, each made a folder, are refused before the review, naming the path. R-1842 replaces the POSIX-only read-only check and its Windows SKIP: chmod 0o555, or an icacls deny on Windows, then a probe write that must fail before the refusal is judged. R-852 drops "(<3s)" from its title and reads AT_ONCE_MS. R-725's check seeds `new-out.md` with the exact text the review prints, as GP2 does for R-1351, and now requires a verified round on the bare HEAD sha. R-845 marks the ignored and outside checks. R-723 retires, superseded by R-1840; R-844 retires, superseded by R-1841 and R-1842. The header's @effects names icacls.

**Evidence.** Build 1's CI run 39345850466 (d023b052) was red on Windows only: review-ledger.test.mjs 163 pass, 1 fail, 1 skip. R-1842's fixture, a deny for the Everyone SID, left the ledger folder writable on the hosted runner, so the probe write succeeded and the check failed on its own fixture, as the plan's known limits said it would. Build 2 (9dae968c) strips inherited entries, denies the current user's own SID, and prints a named SKIP only if the probe write still succeeds. CI run 39349939721 on the final tip c2b2b4e0 (build 2 plus a merge of main 4656f646): macOS 166 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 168 files (7,704 checks pass); Ubuntu 166 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP (7,649); Windows 151 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,644), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. review-ledger.test.mjs passes 165 on macOS and Ubuntu, and 163 with 2 skips on Windows. On Windows every new check passes by title except R-1842, which SKIPs by its named reason: "the hosted runner's account still writes through a per-user deny after /inheritance:r". So R-1842's refusal is proven on macOS and Linux only; R-1841's lock case, unverified until then, passes on Windows. Per the builder's saved macOS outputs, which the seat read and did not re-run, each of the 13 wrong-code tries turned red the checks the plan named: W1 and W1b R-843 and R-855; W2 R-855; W3 R-852 and R-1841 (the lock symlink taken over at 5,221 ms); W4 and W5 R-1840 and R-1841; W7 and W8 R-1842; W9b R-725; W10 R-725 and the ..draft.md check; W11 and W12 R-845's outside and ignored checks. W9 reds R-725, the ..draft.md check and one existing check the plan did not name, "a review whose --out lands inside the reviewed tree is stable". The control W5a stays green, 165 of 165. The trace reads 1,438 rows, 0 off form; map:check reads 0 problems. No seat pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/review-records-pin-2026-10-19/seat-verify-c2b2b4e0.md`.

On win32 both build the shell form by joining the exec-form entry through `shq`. In `agent-dir-parity.test.mjs`, R-827 runs `install --home <h> --pi-home <p>` with the ambient value set and needs `<p>/nana-pack.json` present, `<h>/.pi/agent/nana-pack.json` absent and the ambient folder absent. In `relative-agent-dir.test.mjs`, R-346 installs into a fresh home, then runs `doctor --claude-home <home>/.claude` twice under a pi stub: with the absolute agent folder it must exit 0, say all good and print no ✗ or ! line; with the relative one it must print the same ✓ rows, exactly one ! line naming the cwd, no all good, and a non-zero exit. The six existing rows flip to implemented with their text unchanged. R-1870 and R-1871 are new and say "a directory that nana-setup did not make"; R-304 and R-382 are retired as superseded by R-1870, and R-381 by R-1871.

**Evidence.** CI run 39348980616 on draft pull request #86 at 8b7a25eb (GitHub's merge commit onto main f0d6da24, the lane's base): macOS 165 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 167 files (7,674 checks pass); Ubuntu the same file counts (7,619); Windows 150 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,614), the skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On Windows all eight new checks pass by title and none skips; each Windows skip in those files is an older check with its printed reason. So the adversary's claim most likely wrong, that the `--claude-home` control reads all good on native Windows, held. Per the builder's report, each of its eight wrong-code tries turned the predicted check red: W1 (tokenize keeps operators) R-922 and R-318; W2 (doctor's POSIX settings row by script-name substring) R-922 only; W4 (the settings read after the skills step) R-924; W6 (no rm line) R-322; W7 (dry run "would link" over an occupied path) R-1871 only; W7d (a backup copied beside an untouched owner folder) R-1870 only; W8 (`--home` before `--pi-home`) R-827; W11 (doctor's exit ignores ! rows) R-346 only. ## 2026-10-19 — the mutation runner removes a scratch worktree git refuses to remove, and its parts take turns at git

Spend served the current priority's queue line for the Windows fixes, its maintainer-tools part, and CI's Windows leg, which now blocks release. The shared mutation runner ended each scratch worktree with `git worktree remove --force`, ignored a failure and deleted the folder, so git kept the entry as "prunable". Windows CI run 39305895780 (win-pi-registration-test's first run) failed R-1231's three-shard cleanup check this way; the check had passed in 43 of 44 saved Windows runs. Cause, inferred by the plan: git refuses before it deletes anything, or fails on the entry's own files under `.git/worktrees/`, and another shard's git call reading them is the likely Windows trigger.

**The change.** Build 1 (4aaafdc1): one helper serves a shard's own end and the parent's dead-shard cleanup. The first remove is the old call by full path. On failure, up to `WORKTREE_REMOVE_RETRIES` (3) times, it stops when `git worktree list --porcelain` no longer lists the entry, waits `WORKTREE_REMOVE_RETRY_MS` (250 ms) synchronously, deletes the `wt` folder, and removes again by git's unique suffix `<scratch folder base name>/wt`. An entry still listed after the last retry gets one line, `seat-mutate: git worktree entry <wt> is still listed after 3 retries`: in the shard's part file, or in the parent's notes for a dead shard, so it reaches the output once and the record once. The runner never runs `git worktree prune`, which acts on every entry, and never passes `--force` twice, so a lock someone set stays and is named. The KEEP paths are unchanged. Build 2 (bd3df051): CI's Windows leg on build 1 failed R-1230's existing scratch check, because shard 2's `git worktree add` read shard 1's half-removed admin folder (`failed to read .git/worktrees/wt/commondir`). One operation lock, a file in the run's parts folder, now covers each worktree add and each whole removal with its retries; a waiter tries again every `WORKTREE_LOCK_WAIT_MS` (25 ms) and replaces a lock whose holder process is gone. Six rows in FEAT-031, R-1770 to R-1775, each with one check in `mutation-runner.test.mjs`; the fixture gains the `drop-dotgit` and `lock-self` probe modes, each with a `-selfkill` form, and T4 counts removes in a `GIT_TRACE` file. The pack README names the new record line, the retry order, the suffix and the no-prune trade.

**Evidence.** CI run 39336290907 on draft pull request #75 at 9a5aa8c5 (build 2 with main cb1a5b57 merged): macOS 164 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 166 files (7,662 checks pass); Ubuntu the same file counts (7,607); Windows 149 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,603), its skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On Windows the six new checks and R-1231's cleanup check pass by title, and the test file passes 32 with its 3 existing skips; macOS and Ubuntu pass 34. T1 passing on Windows means Git for Windows found the entry by its forward-slash suffix, which the plan's adversary had not tried there. Build 1's run 39330086149 (4aaafdc1) failed only that one Windows file, 30 pass and 1 fail. Per the builder's reports, each wrong-code try turned its named check red: no retry (T1, T2, T4, T5), every retry run whatever the listing (T4), `prune --expire now` (T3), one retry or no wait (T4), the dead-shard line to the console only (T5), the helper under KEEP (R-1231), a raw full-path list compare (T1); R-1775's check was red before build 2 and with the lock bypassed, and the test file passed three runs in a row. One green Windows run is weak evidence against a failure seen once in 44 runs. As an ordinary lane it had no seat wrong-code pairs and no independent check. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/mutation-runner-win-prune-2026-10-19/seat-verify-9a5aa8c.md`, both Windows logs beside it.

Build 3 (79f6b0b9) published the lock by hard-linking a complete temp file, removed a lock only while it still held the content read, and made R-1775 drive a real shard's add and removal; R-1776 runs 8 children for 2,400 acquisitions. ## 2026-10-19 — on Windows, install and doctor list each Claude Code hook only by its settings row

## 2026-10-19 — the install check runs one real pi turn in a fresh install, on macOS and Windows

Spend served the current priority's installation line, for the pi seat: the priority names both seats, installation and every hook, on macOS and Windows. The install check proved install, pi registration, a pi RPC session and each Claude Code hook in a temporary home, but by its own README it did not invoke pi's prompt-time hooks. So a fresh install whose pi turn lost the objective, the rulings, the writing rule or the knowledge pull, or whose gate let the verifier sample through, still passed. The plan's stub run of main also gave PASS `pi session` for an `extension_error` line, an `objective unavailable:` notice and a roster-unavailable widget, and PASS `hook adoption` for a stale line and a stale-check-unavailable line.

**The change.** A new pi extension fixture, `packages/nana-setup/lib/install-check-turn.mjs` (six-tag header, default export), loads only through `-e` and sits in no pi package manifest (R-1796). It imports the faux provider from the ROOT `@earendil-works/pi-ai`, which pi aliases for every extension, and registers a sealed provider and model. Its first answer records the system prompt and the user messages, then calls bash with the verifier hit sample; its second records each tool result and writes the record as JSON into the temporary home. `install-check.mjs` runs the turn after `pi session` through `piRun`, so win32 goes through findPi and winCommand: from the fixture project, with the base environment (no `NANA_ROLE`, so the 2026-10-09 knowledge ruling holds), `--offline -e <fixture> --provider <p> --model <m> --no-session -p "<prompt> <token>"` and a sealed 60 s timeout. The exported pure judge `judgePiTurn` fails `pi turn` on a non-zero exit (naming the last stderr line), a missing record, an `Extension error (` stderr line, a stale or stale-check-unavailable line in the system prompt, or a system prompt missing the run token, `## Standing rulings (nana)` or `<nana-writing>`. It also fails when no user message starts with `[nana:knowledge]` and names the note file, or no errored bash result starts with `nana-gate: verifier pipe blocked (headless fail-closed):`. The pi session's spawn keeps 100,000 characters of output, and the exported reader `readPiSessionEvents` fails the line on an `extension_error` event or an extension notice, widget or status holding "unavailable" in any letter case, skipping non-JSON lines. The `hook adoption` line also fails on a line that starts with either stale prefix, imported from `stale-copies-config.mjs`. `PI_TURN`, `PI_SESSION` and `SPAWN_TIMEOUT_MS.turn` are sealed with provenance, and the note's file name has one sealed name. The setup README's install-check section names the turn, its local test model (no network, no real model), the new session and adoption failures, and what the turn does not cover: the PowerShell tool and the approval dialog. Rows: R-1790 to R-1796 new in FEAT-015; R-1070's evidence gains the R-1791 check. The trace reads 1,432 rows with 0 problems.

**Evidence.** CI run 39344704047 on draft pull request #83 (ee2cb4c9, GitHub's merge onto c827fcf8): macOS 165 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 167 files (7,670 checks pass); Ubuntu the same file counts (7,615 pass); Windows 150 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,610 pass), the skips main's declared ones. install-check.test.mjs passes 114 on every leg. Both CI install-check jobs pass and print `PASS pi turn`, with PASS install, pi session and doctor, and on macOS the readme checklist: 15 items each, all PASS. That is the first CI proof that one real pi print-mode turn in a fresh install shows the objective, rulings, writing and knowledge injections in the model request and the gate's block on a scripted bash call, on macOS and on Windows. The turn line follows the session line by 0.77 s on macOS and 1.66 s on Windows. So the adversary's claim most likely wrong, that pi resolves the fixture's root pi-ai import on CI's Windows leg, held. The seat's real install check on macOS on build 1 printed `FAIL pi turn — exit: exit status 1`, every other item PASS. By hand in the retained home, the seat found the prompt and the run token reaching pi as two positional arguments: pi took the second as another message, the fixture had no third answer queued (`No more faux responses queued`), and pi exited 1. One joined argument exits 0. On build 2 the same check printed all 15 items PASS. The setup README dropped the sentence that a Windows green result does not replace the owner's Windows machine rehearsal; the final reviewer judges it. No seat pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/pi-seat-live-turn-2026-10-19/seat-verify-ee2cb4c.md`, with the two Windows logs beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it: R-1790 narrowed to what its test checks and covers win32 through winCommand; the brief named the R-1079 win32 test whose `/--mode/` filter also matches `--model`; R-1794's test asserts the session cap; new wrong-code tries joined (rulings judged by the `<nana-rulings>` tag, an ignored exit status, an ignored tool name, no session cap, win32 without winCommand); the tool name is sealed; and the claim most likely wrong moved to the root pi-ai import on Windows. ## 2026-10-19 — the pipe check reads PowerShell comments and quotes as PowerShell's own tokenizer does

Spend served the current priority's every-hook line for both seats on macOS and Windows: the verifier-pipe guard, the predicate the Claude Code verifier hook and pi's nana-gate run on every PowerShell command. It is the hardening step HANDOFF's Claude Code hooks on Windows residual named, after reviewers found the PowerShell lexer's gaps one character at a time over three rounds. The planner's probe on pwsh 7.4.6, 1,500 generated commands (seed 1): main's lexer disagreed with PowerShell's tokens on 890 of 1,155 parse-clean commands. On main, a `#` or `<#` after a mid-word quoted part, `]`, `$`, a word's own `$( … )`, an escaped space or `${…}` opened a comment and hid a later `npm test | Select-Object -Last 1; git commit -m x`, where PowerShell asks. A `#` right after a here-string or block-comment close opened none, so a quote in it hid the pipe. ‘ ’ ‚ ‛ “ ” „ were not quotes, and a line comment ran past a lone CR. Main also asked for `Write-Output “a | b”; git commit -m x`, where PowerShell sees no pipe.

**The change.** One token-start scanner in `packages/nana-pack/lib/pipe-guard.mjs` replaces `startsPowerShellComment`, `matchPowerShellParen` and `liveTextPowerShell`. `#`, `<#`, `@'` and `@"` open a comment or here-string only at a token start; inside a bare word they stay literal. One pass yields the live text and the new named export `powerShellSpans(command)`. Typographic quotes act as their ASCII kind, and a line comment ends at CR or LF. The bash path and `verifierPipeReason`'s signature are unchanged. Quote sets, token enders, oracle shells, corpus seed and size and the oracle timeout live in the new `lib/pwsh-lexer-config.mjs`. The new helper `tests/pwsh-oracle.mjs` generates a seeded corpus and reads PowerShell's own tokens through `Parser.ParseInput`: pwsh wherever it is on PATH, and Windows PowerShell on win32, where it is required. `tests/pipe-guard-pwsh-oracle.test.mjs` compares comment and string spans and holds the deterministic tables. Rows R-1730 to R-1736 in FEAT-010. One existing case flips, as the plan named: `$#` is one bare word. The pack README's verifier-pipe paragraph names the comparison and the known gaps. Build 2 (7ca1eafb) makes a backtick escape that is not a line continuation clear the token start, and adds a corpus word that starts with an escape.

**Evidence.** CI run 39338404113 on draft pull request #70 (c78c6e79, build 2 plus a merge of main f497c5ad): macOS 165 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 167 files (7,684 checks pass); Ubuntu the same file counts (7,629 checks pass); Windows 150 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,625 checks pass); install-check passes on macOS and Windows. The oracle lines pass for pwsh on every leg and for Windows PowerShell on Windows, so the adversary's claim most likely wrong, that 5.1 tokenizes the corpus differently, did not come true. Build 1's run 39332622508 also passed on all legs. The seat's wrong-code pairs on macOS went 21 of 21 red on 983da287 and 22 of 22 red on c78c6e79, 19 by exactly their named titles and 3 with an extra unnamed red, 0 survived. Pair M22 puts back build 1's escape rule and turns both new leading-escape cases red. Three oracle-only pairs ran in place on a scratch CI branch (run 39338540305, never merged): 3 of 3 red on Ubuntu under pwsh 7.6.6, and 3 of 3 on Windows under pwsh 7.6.6 and Windows PowerShell 5.1. The independent check ran 12 probes on build 1 (983da287) against pwsh 7.6.7's tokens, with main's guard side by side: FINDINGS, no MUST. Over 6,000 wider generated commands, missed asks fell from main's 24 to the lane's 15, and false asks from 17 to 9; its 569 span disagreements all sit in the documented gaps (member access, operators, `@(`). F1, a fail-open the lane introduced, was a backtick escape at a word's start that kept the token start, so ``Add-Content CHANGELOG.md `## 1.2.0; npm test | Select-Object -Last 3; git commit -am v1.2.0`` went unasked where main asks. Build 2 fixed it; its two new cases were red before the fix, and the new corpus word occurs 115 times in 110 of the 1,500 commands. F2, about 5,000 nested parentheses overflowing the scanner's stack, is a carried residual: the Claude Code hook abstains with a stderr note, and pi's nana-gate blocks. The plan's known limits are HANDOFF residuals: a comment glued to an operator, a statement-start value, a redirection, a parameter colon or a member access; nested strings inside a string's `$( … )`; and a comparison bounded by its alphabet. So are the wrong forms no check catches, among them a line continuation that clears the token start. The local macOS install check runs before the land. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/pwsh-lexer-oracle-2026-10-19/seat-verify-c78c6e7.md`.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it: R-1736 split out of R-1733, a parse-clean ninth case with its `@"` twin and `a'b'<#c`, a fail-open case for double quotes, the requirements tree regenerated, the `$#` case retitled, and known limit 3 widened to member access without an index. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker in two builds: build 1 (f742f2cc and 1afceda9, docs 5150888f and 983da287) and build 2 (7ca1eafb), which fixed the independent check's F1. Claude Opus 5.5, the final reviewer, and Claude Fable 5.1, the land reviewer, review it through review-ledger.

Round 1 and build 3 (added before the final round). The final reviewers' first round found four misses that main flags: a here-string glued to an assignment (`$msg=@'` with an apostrophe in the body) hid a later pipe (Opus, a MUST), and a comment glued to a second index, an array subexpression or a method call with arguments, before an odd quote, did too (Fable, a SHOULD). A third pi gpt-5.6-sol build fixed those shapes with expected values from pwsh 7.6.7's tokens (new rows R-1737 to R-1739, each new case red before), and the corpus gained one command per class. The seat's macOS pairs on the merged tip read 25 of 25 red (M23 to M25 for the new rows), and CI run 39345087464 read 0 failing of 168 files on all three legs, the oracle passing for pwsh everywhere and for Windows PowerShell 5.1 on Windows. Spend served the current priority's every-hook line for both seats on macOS and Windows: the knowledge pull, which runs on every prompt. pi sets no time limit on a `before_agent_start` handler, so the handler's own fail-open is the only guard. The stdin cap guards the Claude Code seat, where a large paste reaches the hook unsliced (inferred from the hook JSON; pi slices to 8 KB first). knowledge-refresh-pin had cut these four rows into a residual. Nothing was red, because the behaviour works: this is a pin lane.

**The change.** Tests, rows and the code map only; the pi extension, `lib/hook.ts` and the CLI are byte-unchanged. ## 2026-10-19 — on Windows, install puts pi-review, pi-worker, nana-land and nana-setup on the path as launchers

Spend served the current priority's queue line for the Windows fixes, its Windows install shape part: the priority names installation on Windows. Install skipped all four PATH commands on Windows. The plan's probe on main (forced win32, a temp home) printed four `skipped (win32)` PATH rows and created no bin folder; doctor printed the four rows as `· skipped (win32)`. So a Windows seat typed `node "$HOME/nana-pi/packages/nana-pack/bin/pi-worker.mjs"` and its pi-review twin, and the adopt skills ran nana-setup through node.

**The change.** A new `packages/nana-setup/lib/win-shims.mjs` (effects none) renders two launchers per command. The sh one, LF, is `#!/bin/sh` then `exec node '<absolute bin>' "$@"`; `exec` replaces the shell, so a rewrite of the running launcher is never read, and Git Bash runs it by bare name. The `.cmd` one, CRLF, follows npm cmd-shim 7.0.0's layout with two changes: `SET "dp0=%~dp0"` quoted, and the program is the `node.exe` that `%%~$PATH:I` finds, with a stderr message and exit 1 when PATH has none. Its bin is `"%dp0%\<relative path>"` on the same drive, else absolute, with every `%` doubled; the endLocal-goto line ends batch reading before node starts. Suffixes, line ends and the layout are sealed contract values. On win32, stepPath writes both launchers for the four commands through `writeBytesWithBackup`, factored out of linkFile's copy branch: a byte-equal file stays, a differing regular file goes to `<name>.bak-<date>` first, a symlink is replaced and never written through. Doctor on win32 marks each `PATH <command>` row ✓ only when both launchers equal this checkout's rendering, else ✗ and exit 1. A new `PATH resolves pi-review` row, the win32 twin of R-953, reads ✓ only when PATH and PATHEXT find the bin folder's `.cmd` first: ✗ on the real home, a note under `--home`, with the user Path in Environment Variables as the remedy. install-check puts the bin folder first on win32 too, and the doctor note list and the state manifest follow. No `.ps1` launcher: under PowerShell's default Restricted policy it would refuse to run rather than fall back to the `.cmd`. The Support line in AGENTS.md, README.md and the setup README now says the pack's PATH commands are shims that need the bin folder on PATH; the setup README carries the known limits, and the pack README keeps the node forms as the fallback. Rows: R-1610 to R-1618 new in FEAT-013, R-995 retired, R-589, R-1072 and R-684 reworded; R-937's evidence drops the four PATH rows, and R-1081's notes its updated note-list pin. The trace reads 1,405 rows with 0 problems.

**Evidence.** CI run 39332394588 on draft pull request #71 (03f1faf3): macOS 165 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 167 files (7,647 checks pass); Ubuntu the same (7,592); Windows 150 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,585 pass), the skips main's declared ones; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On Windows win-shims passes 7 with no skip and win32-degrade passes 92. So the plan's claim most likely wrong, that Windows PowerShell 5.1 keeps `& ^ ( ) %` literal inside a spaced argument, held. The seat's 19 wrong-code pairs on macOS all turn a check red on build 1 and again on build 3, 0 survived; product code has not changed since build 2. On CI's Windows leg, through a scratch job never merged, the seat's 5 Windows-only pairs each turn their named check red on the tip: unquoted `dp0`, a bare `node`, no endLocal-goto line, an undoubled `%` and the absolute bin path. No pair ran for the `PATH resolves pi-review` match, though the macOS table declared one for the Windows leg. The independent check ran on build 1 and holds over nine probes with no MUST. POSIX install, doctor and uninstall output match main's; the sh launcher round-trips 12 hostile arguments under `/bin/sh`; forced win32 never writes through a symlink and backs up a directory in the way; doctor catches a launcher rendered for another checkout and a CRLF one. Its two known bugs, the no-node message's escaped redirect and an unchanged row naming writes, were fixed in build 2. Its other findings are HANDOFF residuals: the dry-run copy wording, and doctor red on Windows until the bin folder is on the Path. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/win-path-commands-2026-10-19/seat-verify-03f1faf.md`, with the Windows log beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. Nothing pinned the sh launcher's `exec`: without it a self-rewriting bin ran 192 leftover lines, so R-1617 joined. R-1611, R-1612, R-1613 and R-1615 narrowed to what their tests check. The fixture's folders carry a space, `&`, `%`, parentheses, `'`, `$a` and `é`, and callers never pass arguments on bash.exe's own command line. ## 2026-10-19 — the trust command names a missing pi trust store in one line, not a stack trace

Spend served the current priority's installation line: `nana-setup trust <dir>` is the fresh-machine checklist's trust step on every system. The readme-install lane left a HANDOFF residual: trust crashed when npm's global folder held no pi. The plan's probes on main 22ccd849 under a temp HOME: an empty `npm_config_prefix` gave exit 1, `Error [ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND]` and 10 stack lines; a planted trust module holding `module.exports = {};` gave exit 1, `TypeError: trustModule.ProjectTrustStore is not a constructor` and 2 stack lines.

**The change.** `runTrust` in `packages/nana-setup/bin/nana-setup.mjs` binds the trust module's path to one const and wraps its dynamic import: a failed import throws a `SetupError` naming the path, `err.code ?? err.name` and the shared `PI_INSTALL_HINT`. A module with no `ProjectTrustStore` function gets the same kind of refusal. The existing handler prints one `nana-setup:` line and exits 2, as trust's other refusals do. By design the command does not search PATH: under the README's npm route pi sits under `npm root -g` (inferred for nvm and fnm), and a PATH lookup needs a branch per installer and platform. So the lane's name, "finds pi", overstates it. Rows R-1800 and R-1801 in FEAT-009 each get one check in `trust.test.mjs`, which runs the CLI against a fresh npm prefix after deleting every case variant of `npm_config_prefix`. The CLI header's `@errors` and the setup README's trust paragraph say the same. HANDOFF loses the readme-install residual.

**Evidence.** CI run 39334379474 on draft pull request #79 (0c08bcd): macOS 164 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 166 files (7,652 checks pass); Ubuntu the same file counts (7,597 pass); Windows 149 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,591 pass), the skips main's; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. trust.test.mjs passes 13 of 13 on each leg, so the adversary's claim most likely wrong, that the prefix override would not move `npm.cmd root -g` on Windows, did not hold. Per the builder's report, both new checks failed on the old code and pass on the new; four of its five wrong-code tries turned a check red. The fifth, catching the failed import and going on with an empty module, passes both checks, because the store guard then refuses; it is a HANDOFF residual. No seat pairs and no independent check: an ordinary lane. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/trust-finds-pi-2026-10-19/seat-verify-0c08bcd.md`, with the Windows log beside it.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it: delete every case variant of the prefix variable, put the HANDOFF edits before the final round, run the local macOS install check before the land, print `err.code ?? err.name`, and name the refusal in `@errors`. A Claude Sonnet 5 subagent built it in one pass (0c08bcd). Claude Opus 5.5, the final reviewer, reviews it through review-ledger. Known limits, all HANDOFF residuals: a pi from pnpm, bun, volta or yarn is not found; a mixed Node setup is told to install a second pi; a trust module's own missing dependency reports against the trust module's path; npm itself missing keeps the older line with no install command.

## 2026-10-19 — a Python post-edit check's non-ASCII output reaches the model as printed

Spend served the current priority's queue line for the Windows fixes, here the pi seat's post-edit feedback, one of the hooks the priority names. Post-edit decodes every check's output as UTF-8, while a Python check writes in its host's default encoding: the ANSI code page on a Windows pipe, or a Latin-1 locale's encoding on macOS or Linux. The name and value sit beside MAX_OUTPUT as a contract value (PEP 540). UTF-8 mode, not `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8`, because only the mode puts both sides of a Python check that reads its Python child as text on UTF-8. At main 713d6aa, where the plan probed, rows R-107, R-097 and R-243 were `untested` and no assertion reached killTree's win32 branch. The hardening file's survivor probe was `pgrep`, so its kill checks passed on Windows without looking: 15 pass in 1.6–1.8 s on five Windows CI logs. The pack README said the branch "has not been exercised on real Windows".

**The change.** Tests, rows, the tree and one README sentence; `nana-post-edit.ts` did not change. Four must-meet checks, rows R-1700 to R-1703 in FEAT-030. R-1700 (hardening file, every platform): a two-level checker tree, started as `"<process.execPath>" "<tree.cjs>" "<dir>"`, writes its pids; the test sees both alive before the 2.5 s deadline and both gone within 2 s after the run settles, through `process.kill(pid, 0)` only. R-1701 (hardening file, Windows only, a SKIP line with its reason elsewhere): with PATH cut to node's folder, so taskkill cannot start, the shell is killed and the run settles as timed out; the check's own processes survive, as the plan expects, and the test reaps them by pid. R-1702 (status file): a setTimeout recorder sees exactly [300] for a 300 ms control and no timer for timeoutMs 0, and the slow check shows `✓ 1 check`. R-1703 (status file): two passing checks, one printing to stdout and stderr, append nothing to the tool result. R-107, R-097 and R-243 retire as superseded. The README now says CI's Windows leg runs the win32 branch, and that with taskkill off PATH only the shell is killed. The tree's leaf total moves from 1,306 to 1,310.

**Evidence.** CI run 39305742574 on draft pull request #60, GitHub's merge of f8569bd into main 27603d4: macOS 156 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 158 files (7,569 checks pass); Ubuntu 156 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP; Windows 141 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,497 checks pass); install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On Windows the hardening file passes 17 with no skip (15 on main), so R-1700 and R-1701 both ran and passed there; the status file passes 33 and skips 1 (31 on main). On macOS and Ubuntu the hardening file passes 22 and skips 1, R-1701. Per the builder's report, each wrong-code try turned its check red on macOS: a direct kill in place of the POSIX group kill (R-1700); `||` for `??`, `>= 0` for `> 0` and the dropped timed-out guard (R-1702); an append when a pass printed output (R-1703). The plan's Windows mutant run (taskkill without `/T`, both fallbacks made no-ops) did not happen, so no run shows R-1700 or R-1701 failing against broken kill code on Windows. That stays a residual, with the plan's other known limits: a taskkill that starts and fails is untested, abort on Windows has no tree check, and the 30 s default stays an inline literal.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it. R-1700's row narrowed to what the test sees, since on POSIX sh execs the command and leaves no separate shell level. The checker runs node by its full path, so a shim adds no level. R-1701's wrong-code claim was corrected: Node emits `close` after a spawn ENOENT as well as `error`, so making one fallback a no-op still passes. R-1702 dropped its one-second checker. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker in one pass (af00ffd), and the seat merged main 27603d4 (f8569bd). Claude Opus 5.5, the final reviewer, reviews it through review-ledger. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/post-edit-win-pin-2026-10-19/seat-verify-f8569bd.md`.

## 2026-10-19 — a review whose verdict cannot be read fails at the review, not at the land

Spend served the current priority's consistency for both seats: the review and land loop every lane runs through, in terminal mode. When a completed review's verdict could not be read under R-1010 (a bold first verdict line above a plain final one, a malformed final line, a quoted first line, a later line that mentions "verdict"), the ledger recorded an empty verdict and spent the round, while `review-ledger run` printed "verdict recorded" and `pi-review` printed SUCCESS, both with exit 0. nana-land refuses any revision with a non-LAND record, an empty one included, and a re-run cannot clear it; only a new commit can. So the seat learned of it only at the land. The plan's probe on main f29827a: the four planned shapes and a first line with no label each exited 0 with a null tally verdict. An owner project already carries nine such records.

**The change.** review-round's reader keeps what it accepts byte for byte and now also returns the selected line (its 1-based physical number and its text without CR), the condition that emptied it, and the first trigger line. `complete()` reads the output once, records as before, and returns `unread`, a one-line notice; its rounds-left count comes from `decide()` after the append, with no second counting rule. Both launchers print `<prefix>: <notice>` on stderr in place of their success line and exit 1. The round stays consumed, `--out` is kept, and the watchdog adds no retry. The notice quotes the selected line and any trigger line as JSON, names the condition from a new frozen table in `lib/verdict-config.mjs` (wording chosen), says nana-land will refuse the revision for good, and counts the rounds left. Rows R-1620 to R-1623 in FEAT-007; R-1390 now covers only review-shaped output whose recorded verdict is not empty. Six existing checks that ran a review with an empty verdict now expect exit 1 or print a readable verdict, each keeping its meaning. The pack README's exit, run, round and Limits text say the same.

**Evidence.** CI run 39301089336 on b42b541: macOS 155 of 157 files pass, 0 fail, 2 skip (7,557 checks); Ubuntu 155 pass, 0 fail, 2 skip (7,502 checks); Windows 140 pass, 0 fail, 17 skip (6,484 checks), with review-ledger at 161 pass and 2 skip and all four new checks passing on win32. install-check passes on macOS and Windows. The seat's wrong-code pairs: 20 of 20 red, 0 survived. The independent check holds with no MUST: the old and new readers agree on 240,416 generated outputs, nana-land refuses the revision after an unread round as the notice says, and a readable review's stderr matches main's byte for byte. Its three low findings are HANDOFF residuals: 8-bit control characters and Unicode line separators reach the terminal raw; a throw in the rounds-left count would turn a recorded LAND into exit 1, which needs tampering; and a readable re-review of an unread revision still reports success while nana-land refuses it, as on main. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/unread-verdict-loud-2026-10-19/seat-verify-b42b541.md`.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan and one adversary (AMEND): R-1390 contradicted the new exit, the verdict config's header needed one purpose for both tables, and the rounds-left count had to reuse `decide()`. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker. Build 1 stalled under pi-worker's 75-second watchdog before it committed, and build 1b finished it (52e90a0); the builder reports each of its 15 wrong versions of the code red. The seat merged main 713d6aa (b42b541). Claude Opus 5.5 (final reviewer) and Claude Fable 5.1 (land reviewer) review it through review-ledger.

## 2026-10-19 — each session start names nana's stale installed copies and the command that fixes them

Spend served the current priority's queue line for the Windows fixes: the Windows install shape, where copied rules and skills "go stale silently until reinstall" (the 2026-10-11 Windows triage). On Windows, install copies the Claude rules, the requirements skill and the pi rulings file, so a pull leaves them behind. On every system, a rule or skill shipped after the last install stays missing. Only doctor said so. The adversary's probe on main 6b3e432, with a temp home: a POSIX install with one rule link removed, one pointed elsewhere and one replaced by a byte-equal file, and a win32-simulated install with changed standards, rulings and SKILL.md copies. Both times the installed adoption hook printed nothing and exited 0, doctor marked those rows ✗, and the rulings extension set no stale text.

**The change.** Doctor's `linkOk` moves verbatim into a new leaf, `packages/nana-setup/lib/stale-copies.mjs`, beside `staleNotice(labels)` and `hasAdoptionInstallRecord(layout)`; doctor imports it and is otherwise unchanged. A new CLI, `packages/nana-setup/bin/stale-copies.mjs`, walks the Claude rules with `linkOk` and the skills with doctor's `skillLinkState`, skipping on win32 the skills install skips there. It prints one line or nothing: `[nana:install] INSTALL STALE:`, doctor's labels, "tell the user; they fix it with" and `node "<checkout>/packages/nana-setup/bin/nana-setup.mjs" install`. The adoption hook runs it through a second `runHookProducer` launcher, `stale-copies`; a failed checker prints only `[nana:install] STALE-COPY CHECK UNAVAILABLE: checker failed (…)` and leaves the adoption output and the exit code alone. In pi, the nana-rulings extension sets its own `nana-install-stale` section when the injected rulings copy fails `linkOk` against the shipped file; the rulings section is unchanged. Build 2 makes the CLI silent in a home with no install record, which is nana-setup's SessionStart adoption entry in Claude Code's `settings.json`. Rows R-1600 to R-1606 in FEAT-015; R-1600 now reads "for a home that holds a nana-setup install record". Both package READMEs gain a line, and the tree's leaf total moves from 1,300 to 1,307.

**Evidence.** CI run 39304726763 on draft pull request #55 at e99866d: macOS 156 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP of 158 files (7,563 checks); Ubuntu 156 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP (7,508 checks); Windows 141 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP (6,490 checks). stale-copies passes 8 on macOS and Ubuntu and 8 with the POSIX skip on Windows; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. The run before it, 39298597308 on defdeda, failed adoption-reader's two silent-hook checks on all three legs: in that file's temp home, where nana-setup never ran, the hook listed every rule and skill as stale. Neither build 1 nor the independent check had run that file. Build 2 fixed it without editing the test; per the builder's report, all 19 test files that start the adoption hook pass, 1,242 checks. The seat's wrong-code pairs: 15 of 15 red on defdeda, then 17 of 18 on e99866d. The survivor counts any SessionStart entry as the install record; no fixture separates the two, and only nana's own hook runs the check, so it does no harm in use. The independent check ran 12 probes on defdeda and held with no MUST. A healthy install's hook output is identical to main's and about 100 ms slower. The printed command, pasted as written, fixes the copies and silences the line, also from a checkout path holding a space. Each producer's failure leaves the other's output intact. pi's own loader runs the extension, and doctor's output is unchanged. Its one known bug: a folder nana-setup did not make at a skill path gets a line naming install, which refuses that folder, so the line repeats. Its note: this Mac lacks both rulings copies, so after the land every Claude Code session here carries one true line until install runs. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/win-stale-copies-2026-10-19/seat-verify-e99866d.md`.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, who amended it: no bounded read in `linkOk`, which already refuses a non-file before reading; R-1601 also forbids the unavailable line; the unavailable prefix no longer says "install check"; R-1604 and R-1605 compare with doctor's own row; and R-1602 must find a non-empty failed set. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker. Build 1 stalled under pi-worker's 75-second watchdog before it committed, build 1b finished it, and build 2 answered the red CI run. Known limits, carried in HANDOFF: the line reaches the agent, not the screen; hooks are not checked; a non-default Claude folder gets no line (inferred); a pi pack loaded from another clone shows the line every session; pi builders and reviewers carry it. Claude Opus 5.5 (final reviewer) and Claude Fable 5.1 (land reviewer) review it through review-ledger.

## 2026-10-19 — install stops before its first write when another run holds its lock

Spend served the current priority's installation line ("installation and every hook included"). Two install reports were untrue on main f29827a. With another run's settings lock in place, install linked 5 hooks, placed 6 rules and linked 5 skills, and moved an empty `.claude/enforce` flag to the dated backups, then exited 2 saying "Nothing was written"; under forced win32 the home gained 17 entries. And a second install over a home it had set up printed four `– retired .claude/skills/<name>  skipped  unrecognized kind or provenance` rows for spec, py-lint, py-review and py-test, the links it had just made, so its skipped rows grew from 2 to 6. HANDOFF had carried the second since tranche 2.

**The change.** In `packages/nana-setup/lib/steps.mjs`, `install()` keeps the settings preflight first. Outside a dry run it then takes the settings lock once around the five Claude-home steps (retired artifacts, hooks, rules, skills, settings) and releases it when the settings step returns or throws. The later steps run after the release, as before, so a Ctrl-C during a slow `pi install` leaves no lock. `stepSettings` gains a `lockHeld` option that only `install()` passes; called alone, it still takes the lock itself. `stepRetiredArtifacts` gives no row to a link at spec, py-lint, py-review or py-test, because `stepSkills` already reports every link there that is not the pack's own as ✗ (R-665). Every other entry reports as before, including a link at a retired name install no longer links. Four rows, R-1590 to R-1593 under SUB-014, each with one check; a `req:` tag on the existing dry-run check flips R-926 to implemented. The nana-setup README's lock paragraph, its retired-artifacts row and two test-table rows say the same.

**Evidence.** CI run 39300131590 on 702b9bc: macOS and Ubuntu pass 155 of 157 files with 0 failing and 2 skipped; Windows passes 140 with 0 failing and 17 skipped, and runs the four must-meet checks natively, the junction case behind the plan's most-doubted claim included. install-check passes on macOS and Windows. The seat's wrong-code pairs turned 8 of 9 red. The survivor checks for the lock before the first write but takes it only at the settings step; it shows only when two runs race, which no test can stage, so the reviewers read `install()` for it. The independent check ran 14 probes on cc2c4ed. A held lock left a 46-entry home byte-equal, a re-run went from 6 skipped rows to 2, and 30 simultaneous install pairs never crashed, where main crashed in 2 of 12. Its one MUST: the merge of main 713d6aa left R-291's leaf total at 1,298 while the rows make 1,302, so the tree test failed at that tip; CI run 39298347456 failed exactly that check on all three legs. The seat's merge helper had staged the fix without committing it. 702b9bc commits it, and the seat fixed the helper. Its known bug is the plan's known limit, now a residual: a second run that starts after the lock is released can still crash on a link the first run is making (1 of 40 staggered pairs).

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan and one adversary (AMEND): a one-line skip by name and link kind in place of a realpath compare, R-1593 rewritten as the guard that a link at a retired name keeps its row, two rows narrowed to their tests, and the check-only lock named as a known limit. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker. Build 1 stopped under the watchdog's 75-second stall limit before it committed, and build 1b finished it. Claude Opus 5.5 (final reviewer) and Claude Fable 5.1 (land reviewer) review it through review-ledger.

## 2026-10-19 — adding a requirement row needs no edit to the tree test

Spend served the current priority's queue, which runs two lanes building at once, and its CI legs: every lane that added a requirement row had to edit one typed number in the tree test, and parallel lanes collided on it. The R-291 real-ledger check in `requirements-tree.test.mjs` compared the leaf headings' row total with a typed literal (1,298 at main 713d6aa), written three times in the file. By the lane's base, 49 commits had changed it by hand. Once, two parallel lanes each raised it to the same number, and every CI leg went red with no real defect. The check summed leaf headings only, so a row in a feature's own table above its subgroups was never counted: the adversary added one to FEAT-002 above SUB-001, and the test passed with the literal at 1,278 while the trace printed 1,279. A row ID copied under a second heading failed only on the literal, naming no ID, and the tree checker reported 0 problems.

**The change.** A test-local `treeAccounting(parsed, outline)` replaces the inline check and returns a list of problems. Per heading, the committed outline IDs must equal the heading's rows plus its descendants' rows, as before. Per row ID, it compares the ledger lines that the trace rail's own row reader, `requirementRowId`, recognises with the times the parsed tree reads that ID under any heading, leaf or not; a second read reports `<ID> is read N times by the tree`, and an unread line reports `<ID> has a requirement row line the tree does not read`. The R-291 check now requires no problems and prints them on failure; the file carries no row total. Four rows, R-1640 to R-1643 in FEAT-028, each with one check: a row added to the real ledger passes with no test edit, a row line the parser drops fails naming its ID, an ID read under two headings fails naming it, and a feature-level row above a subgroup counts once. The tree checker, its CLI, the templates and the requirements skill are unchanged. After this lands, no lane edits a leaf total again; a lane still carrying the literal takes main's tree test at its next merge and regenerates the tree.

**Evidence.** CI run 39298848848 on GitHub's merge of 32ca20b into main 713d6aa: macOS 155 of 157 files pass, 0 fail, 2 skip; Ubuntu the same; Windows 140 pass, 0 fail, 17 skip; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. The tree test passes 93 checks on each leg. At 32ca20b the trace reads 1,302 rows with 0 off form, and the code map check reads 0 problems. The adversary's prototype turned each of the plan's seven wrong versions red on its named check, plus two extra cases; the build commit records no wrong-code results of its own. Known limits stay as residuals: a row deleted from both the ledger and the outline no longer fails the test (the tree movement report lists it as removed), the accounting is test-only, and the R-288 heading count stays typed. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/leaf-total-drop-2026-10-19/seat-verify-32ca20b.md`.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan and one adversary (AMEND: stale numbers after gate-rm-workdir landed, a sharper duplicate message, two stronger assertions and a warning about a `const` declared later in the file). A Claude Sonnet 5 subagent built it in one build (f3d26f9). The seat merged main 713d6aa by hand in 32ca20b and kept the lane's tree test; main's side had only raised the literal to 1,298. Claude Opus 5.5, the final reviewer, reviews through review-ledger.

## 2026-10-19 — the requirements trace lists every test no requirement marker claims

Spend served the current priority's queue line for the rest of the owner's 2026-10-16 feedback and the continuity study: how a fresh agent reads an adopted project's tests into requirements (the seat's reading; the line names an adoption refresh, and the requirements skill's audit is where that starts). The audit drafts rows from the test suite, but nothing listed the tests no `req:` marker claims, so an auditor compared titles by hand. The plan's probes on the base: the trace command printed the same 2 lines with or without a switch, exit 0 both times; a Python fixture's pytest run printed the same output either way; neither rail, the shim nor the skill held the word "unclaimed".

**The change.** With `NANA_REQ_UNCLAIMED=1`, the TypeScript rail (`templates/typescript/template/tests/requirements-trace.ts`) and the Python rail (the template's `tests/conftest.py`) print a listing after their own output: `unclaimed: <N> test declarations carry no req: marker`, also at zero, then one `  <path>:<line> <title>` line per declaration no marker claims, ordered by the whole project-relative path with `/`, then by line. A declaration is a line the rail's own marker grammar can bind, and claimed means the rail's own binding, across decorator lines in Python. The TypeScript rail exports `UNCLAIMED_ENV`, `unclaimedLines` and `printUnclaimed`. nana-pi's trace command calls `printUnclaimed` after its problem lines and now ends by setting `process.exitCode`, with the same codes: a probe printed 40,000 lines then called `process.exit`, and only 3,841 arrived through a pipe on macOS. The rendered TypeScript project's status test calls `printUnclaimed` after `printReport`. Python's `pytest_sessionfinish`, already at ruff's complexity cap, calls one helper and leaves the exit status alone. With the switch off, the trace command and a Python project's pytest run print exactly what they printed before. The requirements skill's audit gains one paragraph between Pass 1 and Pass 2: run with the switch, and treat each listed line as a proposal, never as a row or evidence. Six rows, R-1660 to R-1665 in FEAT-028, each with one check in the new `requirements-unclaimed.test.mjs`; R-1665 seals the switch name. The tree test's leaf total moved from 1,294 to 1,300.

**Evidence.** Per the builder's report, all six checks were red on the old code and pass now, their Python legs run. Each wrong version it tried turned its named checks red: a marker two lines up claiming, folder-walk or `Path` order, a dropped zero count, decorators ignored, the listing moved into the problems or cut short, and another switch name. A path joined with the platform separator fails only on Windows, so CI is that try's check. CI run 39300678028 on the merged tip 429b706: macOS 156 of 158 files pass, 0 fail, 2 skip; Ubuntu the same; Windows 141 pass, 0 fail, 17 skip (the 16 desk files and one bench file that needs an absent extension). The new file passes 6 of 6 on all three legs. install-check passes on macOS and Windows. On nana-pi the listing holds 2,103 declarations, of the 5,070 the plan measured; the seat's run on the tip prints the same count. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/unclaimed-tests-2026-10-19/seat-verify-429b706.md`.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, who amended it. A 10-line nearby-row window held 70% of nana-pi's unclaimed declarations, but 11% in an adopted TypeScript project and 25% in an adopted Python one, so the grouping and its sealed window were dropped; the adversary names this the plan's likeliest wrong claim. The trace command's early exit, the Python hook's complexity cap and a fixture with `a-b` beside folder `a`, where whole-path order differs from folder order, came from the same attack. A pi gpt-5.6-sol builder built it through pi-worker in one brief and a finish: build 1 stalled under the launcher's 75-second watchdog before it committed, and build 1b judged and finished its uncommitted work. Claude Opus 5.5, the final reviewer, reviews through review-ledger. The plan's known limits are HANDOFF residuals.

## 2026-10-19 — the product report lists implemented rows whose tests did not pass

The Issues view listed only violated rows, so a row whose tests never passed here read implemented unless someone opened its card.

**The change.** `scripts/product-report.mjs` adds `model.notPassed`: implemented rows, in ledger order, where any unique cited file last recorded FAIL, or every cited file has a recorded run and none recorded PASS. The heading and note live inside `renderInto`, because the page runs it as source. Rows R-1650 to R-1655 in FEAT-039, six checks in one new block of product-report.test. The README gains one paragraph, and R-291's leaf total moves from 1,298 to 1,304.

## 2026-10-19 — five commands run the same through a linked checkout folder

Spend served the current priority's queue line for tree-oriented briefs and reviews, on macOS: the tree movement report every brief and land carries, and the commands a reviewer runs beside it. HANDOFF's Tree residual said both tree scripts exit without a word through a linked checkout folder. The plan found five commands with that fault: the tree movement report (`scripts/requirements-moved.mjs`), the tree checker (`scripts/requirements-tree.mjs`), the candidate rows list (`scripts/requirement-rows.mjs`), the product report (`scripts/product-report.mjs`) and the roster command (`packages/nana-pack/bin/nana-roster.mjs`). Each ran its main only when `process.argv[1]`, resolved or turned into a URL, matched `import.meta.url`. Node gives `import.meta.url` the main module's real path, so through a link the two differed and the command exited 0 with nothing printed. On macOS the OS temp folder is itself reached through a link (`/var` to `/private/var`), and pi-review's detached checkout sits there, so a reviewer who ran one of these commands by its absolute path got a silent exit 0 that read as a pass. The adversary's probe on main f29827a: by real path the five printed the movement report, the ledger refusal (exit 1), usage (1), usage (2) and six roster lines; through the link all five exited 0 and printed nothing, and so did runs from the clone's unresolved temp path.

**The change.** All five now use the guard the templates ship and `scripts/runtime-measure.mjs` uses: `process.argv[1] && existsSync(process.argv[1]) && realpathSync(process.argv[1]) === realpathSync(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url))`, with the JS `fs.realpathSync` Node's loader uses, not `.native`. The `existsSync` term keeps an import from throwing when `argv[1]` names no file; the roster-notice extension, its hook and pi-worker import the roster module. No shared helper, no argument parsing, no README edit. Six rows: R-1630 and R-1631 in FEAT-028, R-1632 in FEAT-029, R-1633 in FEAT-039, R-1634 and R-1636 in FEAT-006; R-1635 is unused. The new `linked-launch.test.mjs` runs each command by the checkout's real path and through one junction or directory symlink to it, with the same arguments, environment and working folder. It passes only when the status and stdout match and the real-path run printed something. R-1636 in `nana-roster.test.mjs` imports the roster module with `argv[1]` naming a missing file and another file, and expects only the importer's own output. One named deviation from the brief: R-917's source check bans a direct `fs.mkdtempSync` outside `tests/tmp-dir.mjs`, so the builder added `tmpDirUnmanaged` there; it makes the link's folder without registering it for exit-time removal, and the test unlinks the link, then removes the folder.

**Evidence.** The builder's five link checks went red on the old guards (every linked run exited 0 with empty stdout) and green after; R-1636 passes on both, a regression pin for the new shape. Its four wrong-code tries each turned the named check red: the resolve-form guard back in one script, no `existsSync` in the roster (the import throws), `if (true) main()` (the import prints the roster) and `if (false)` in one script (only the printed-something condition catches it). Those red runs were on macOS; no Windows run showed the five checks red on the old guards. CI run 39298256239 on the merged tip a65e53d: macOS and Ubuntu each 156 of 158 files pass, 0 fail, 2 skip; Windows 141 pass, 0 fail, 17 skip; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. linked-launch passes 5 of 5 on all three legs, so on the Windows runner the junction checks the plan only inferred pass. The plan said nothing in the suite removes a link a crash leaves behind; the suite runner does remove each file's temp folder recursively. A seat probe on macOS showed that removal deletes a directory symlink and keeps its target's files; Windows is inferred. The bench's three commands keep the old guard, and that, the import pin covering only the roster, the tree checker's silent success and the crash case are HANDOFF residuals. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/silent-main-guard-2026-10-19/seat-verify-a65e53d.md`.

## 2026-10-19 — the locked land suite runs four test files at once

Spend served the current priority's queue line for the parallel test runner, split out of the process lane because it changes the suite's gate. Every land runs the locked full suite, and it ran one file at a time: about 13 to 15 minutes on this Mac (roster-set's land suite took 814.5 s for 151 files), while every other worktree's full run waited on the machine-wide lock. The plan's measurements found four files at once clean, with review-ledger (125 to 140 s alone) setting the floor.

**The change.** `scripts/test.mjs` takes `--jobs=N`: a pool of N over the collected files in collection order, a freed slot taking the next file at once, never in batches. Each file's output and result line go out as one block when it ends; WARN lines, the `failed:` list and the self-test verdict print in collection order; cleanup kills every running tree and removes every running temp root. Four files with load-sensitive timing bounds, declared with their reasons in the new `packages/nana-pack/lib/test-runner-config.mjs`, run alone after the pool drains; no bound was loosened. A bad `--jobs` argument exits 1 before any file starts. `scripts/test-locked.mjs` passes `--jobs=4` on an unfiltered run; `NANA_SUITE_JOBS` overrides it, and 1 restores the serial run. `npm test` and CI stay serial. The runner now ends by setting its exit code rather than calling `process.exit`, so a large last block reaches a pipe whole. Rows R-1470 to R-1477 in FEAT-031; the README's Tests section gains one paragraph.

**Evidence.** On this Mac at 535bba2, with other lanes' builders and reviews running, the full locked suite at four jobs passed 154 files in 269.3 s (7,500 checks, 0 failing). CI run 39287745011 at 1932efe, the lane merged with main b8616fb: macOS 155 PASS and 0 FAIL of 157 files; Ubuntu 155 PASS, 0 FAIL; Windows 140 PASS, 0 FAIL, 17 SKIP; install-check passes on macOS and Windows. The new file passes 8 of 8 on all three legs, R-1475's stop through the IPC trigger on Windows. The seat's wrong-code pairs went 24 of 27 red on build 1 and 26 of 27 after build 2; the survivor, the pipe-holder reap race, is a declared limit. The independent check held on 13 probes with no MUST, including byte-identical serial output against main and a Ctrl-C through the real npm chain that left no process or temp root behind. Its first finding, a last block over 64 KB losing the summary through a pipe, was fixed in build 2; its second, an argument after `test:locked --` skipping the lock as on main, is a residual. The seat's own probe found that a parent holding an IPC channel to the runner now waits for it to close; only two stop tests start it that way.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan and one adversary, who amended it: a check that catches batches of N, the pipe-holder reap moved to the one fixture that holds the pipe, R-1471 made red today with overlapping fixtures, and an empty `NANA_SUITE_JOBS` read as unset. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in two passes; build 2 closed the seat's three gaps (two pair survivors and the pipe cut). ## 2026-10-19 — the lane write guard judges edits where pi writes them

Spend served the current priority's pi seat on Windows: the gate every lane builder runs under. At main b3f6ec2 the lane write guard had three faults. On every system it judged the typed path, while pi's edit and write tools expand `~`, strip a leading `@` and convert `file://` URLs; so `~/x`, `@/outside/x` and `file:///outside/x` were judged inside the lane and allowed while pi wrote outside. The policy floor already used the mirror, `resolveToolPath`; only the lane guard skipped it. On Windows it counted `/tmp` as a temp root, and there `/tmp` resolves to the gate's current drive (`D:\tmp` on CI), an extra writable root. And plain `fs.realpathSync` keeps the typed letter case and 8.3 short names, so a temp or lane target spelled unlike its variable was refused.

**The change.** In `nana-gate.ts`, `realOut` starts from `resolveToolPath`; one local `real()` takes the native real path, falling back to the plain one, for the target's nearest existing ancestor, the lane root and the temp roots; `/tmp` counts on macOS and Linux only. Four checks in lane-write-guard, rows R-1430 to R-1433, cover the tmp root, the temp and lane spelling pairs in both directions, and pi's three path forms. Each spelling check first proves a sibling folder is refused, or prints SKIP. The pack README's lane paragraph says the same. A Windows builder that writes to `/tmp` with the write tool is now refused; it should use the temp folder.

**Evidence.** The seat's six wrong-code pairs each turn their named check red on macOS, on the first build and again on the third. The independent check, run on the first build, holds with no MUST: on macOS the temp-spelling, lane-spelling and path-form checks fail on main's gate and pass on that build, four fail-opens and several over-blocks close, and eight other test files that load the gate keep their counts. CI on the first build skipped R-1430's Windows half: the test's folder helper read `D:\tmp` as inside a lane on `C:`. The second build gave that helper the other-drive guard the gate's own `within()` has. The third build merged main a0e0a69, whose Linux lane had also reworked the guard's test, and kept both lanes' fixtures. CI on the second build (merged with main b8ace0b) and on the third then ran the tmp check on the Windows runner and passed it; the guard's test file passes 13 of 13 there. CI on the third build: macOS 0 failing of 152 files; Ubuntu passes the guard's test file and fails only path-spelling, main's Linux regression, which another lane fixes; Windows fails only main's 20 desk and bench files. No Windows run has shown the tmp check red on main's gate. The builders' forced-Windows probes on macOS reverted only the lane's tmp line, through macOS's path module, so they show that line matters, not main's gate failing. The final review found that on CI's drive layout main's gate already refuses the plain tmp spelling. The fourth build therefore also checks the drive-joined spelling; a pure Windows-path probe with CI's drive layout shows main's gate allows it and the lane's refuses it. CI on the fourth build, merged with main ca27d86, reads 0 failing files on macOS, Ubuntu and Windows, and the Windows runner passes the tmp check. No CI ran on the red tests alone. The seat's record is `docs/reviews/win-gate-tmp-2026-10-19/seat-verify-5f4dcef.md`; `seat-verify-ba0c960.md` beside it is the earlier record.

Claude Sonnet 5 built it in three builds: the change, the test helper fix and a merge of main. Opus reviews as final reviewer and Fable as land reviewer.

## 2026-10-19 — a checkout git calls clean reviews and lands at its plain commit

Spend served the current priority's queue line for the Windows fixes: a review and a land from an honest Windows checkout. review-round's `workingState` hashed raw bytes and the execute bit on disk, so two honest checkouts got a `+snap` revision and nana-land refused every land from them. One is a committed 100755 file with no execute bit under `core.fileMode=false`, every Windows checkout of nana-pi (inferred: NTFS stores no execute bit). The other is a CRLF checkout under `core.autocrlf=true`, Git for Windows' default, seen on Windows CI as land.test's in-flight check recording `+snap:dabb3236…`.

**The change.** The revision is now the tree `git add -A` would record over HEAD, plus any dirty or new nested repository, with the review's own outputs reset to HEAD after the add. A scratch index is seeded with `read-tree HEAD`, never copied from the real one, and a scratch object folder borrows the repo's objects through its own `objects/info/alternates` file (forward slashes on Windows; a path holding a newline refuses). The real index is read first, so a corrupt index still refuses. Every scratch command pins `core.safecrlf=false`, `core.splitIndex=false` and an empty `core.hooksPath`, and the scratch folder goes in `finally`. pi-review, nana-land and review-ledger keep their code. Rows R-1370 to R-1378 in FEAT-007; R-708 now defines clean against git's normalized tree, and R-709 narrows to "never renders a diff", its three raw-byte collapse checks removed.

**Evidence.** CI at 77289a0: macOS 0 failing of 152 files (7,481 checks); Ubuntu fails only path-spelling GP1/GP2, main's; Windows fails only 16 desk and 4 bench files, main's. All nine must-meet checks pass on Windows, including R-1377, the plan's claim most likely wrong (Windows git reading the alternates file's drive path), and win32-launch's plain-sha check now passes on a committed executable. The seat's wrong-code pairs went 16 of 16 red on build 1, then 19 of 19 after build 2. The independent check held on 11 of 16 probes and measured about 0.34 s more per derivation.

**Plan and build.** The seat's design and one adversary, who amended it: an alternates file in place of the environment variable. The adversary also rejected the smaller "git decides clean, raw hash when dirty" alternative, because a CRLF file removed only from the index broke staging independence. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in two passes. Build 2 fixed the independent check's MUST: a clean checkout under `core.safecrlf=true` was refused, because a scratch index with no stat data makes git re-check every file. It also fixed two known bugs: a `sharedindex` file left in the real `.git` under `core.splitIndex`, and the repository's own `post-index-change` hook running during derivation. A killed derivation's leftover scratch folder and an inferred Windows antivirus hold on its removal stay residuals. Opus gives the final review and Fable the land review. Both said LAND on round 1 with one shared finding: git refreshes the timestamps of objects the scratch add re-hashes, so R-1375's promise of unchanged timestamps in the real git folder was false. Build 3 drops that clause (sizes and modes unchanged, nothing added or removed, no hook) and gives each fixture a never-edited committed file, so the check sees a refreshed object.

## 2026-10-19 — in pi, a plain recursive or forced rm inside the working folder runs without asking

Spend served the current priority's pi seat on macOS and Windows: "rm inside the working directory", one of the small fixes the queue says ride along, from the owner's 2026-10-10 ruling that rm runs freely only inside the working directory. The gate asked for every recursive or forced rm, inside the working folder or not. The plan's probe on main: 8 of 8 forms such as `rm -rf build` blocked headless and opened 8 dialogs interactively. A headless builder could not clear its own build folder.

**The change.** A new module, `packages/nana-pack/lib/gate-rm-inside.ts`, exports `rmInsideCwd`. It returns true only when the whole raw command is exactly `rm`, flags from `-r`, `-R`, `-f`, `-v`, `--recursive`, `--force` and `--verbose`, and literal relative targets from an allow-listed character set. Each target must lie strictly inside the session's working folder. No target part may be dot-named or a link or junction. No target may directly hold `.git`, and none may overlap the active pi agent folder either way. The working folder may not be home, an ancestor of home, a filesystem root or dot-named. Every path is spelled by the repo's `realPath`. `nana-gate.ts` clears the one "rm recursive/forced" danger with it, after the floor and only for the bash tool and a one-segment command; the allow, extra, protected and verifier-pipe checks run after it unchanged. Plain `rm` and `rm -f` keep passing anywhere, as before. Seven rows, R-1490 to R-1496 in FEAT-010, each with one check in the new `gate-rm-inside.test.mjs`. Existing gate-corpus, gate-survives-mutation and display-surfaces cases that used an in-folder target now use `../`, with titles and counts unchanged. The pack README gains one gate bullet with the exempt form, what still asks and the known limits.

**Evidence.** The seat's wrong-code pairs: 32 of 34 red on build 1; the two survivors (only the last target checked; home compared by its typed spelling) were test gaps, and build 2 added cases that turn both red, 34 of 34. The independent check ran 13 probes on build 1 and held with no MUST. Its three known bugs: two in-folder targets main asked for now run unasked (a folder holding a git repository deeper down, a bare repository), which build 2 added to the README's known limits and HANDOFF carries as residuals; no Windows CI yet, which the run below settles; and the missing open question on plain `rm` outside the folder, which HANDOFF now carries. CI run 39273742604 on the merged tip: Windows 134 of 155 files pass, 0 fail, 21 skip; Ubuntu 153 pass, 0 fail, 2 skip; macOS 152 pass, 1 fail, 2 skip. The new file passes 7 of 7 on macOS and Windows and 7 with 1 skip on Ubuntu, where a case-changed home spelling cannot exist. On Windows its junction cases pass, so Node reports a junction as a link there, the plan's most-likely-wrong claim. macOS's one failure is a desk-service timing check (2553 ms); the lane touches nothing there, and the seat re-ran that file alone three times on the tip, 46 pass and 0 fail each. install-check passes on macOS and Windows.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan and one adversary (AMEND): the planner's agent-folder check was one-way and case-sensitive, so `rm -rf AgentDir` and `rm -rf agentdir/extensions` ran unasked on the prototype; the fix spells paths by `realPath` and rejects overlap in both directions. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in two builds. Opus gives the final review and Fable the land review.

## 2026-10-19 — a bench study stops when it cannot write a graded run's evidence

Spend served the current priority's queue: one of the small fixes that ride along, the owner's 2026-10-10 ruling that a study stops when it cannot write its evidence. R-542 had read violated since the 2026-10-02 clause audit split it out. When a graded run's evidence folder could not be written, `executeRun` returned a grader-error record that kept the spend, and `runPlan` went on to the next paid child. The adversary's probe at 0f2e0ca, with a stub child: with t1's evidence blocked and t2 interleaved, 4 of 4 children started, 2 runs had no evidence, and no error was raised. An asset copy that failed after the child took the same catch and left no evidence folder at all, and the three-failure stop never tripped, because every decided run in between reset its streak.

A post-processing failure after a measured child with the flag unset adds `evidenceError` (the folder, the code and Node's message) to the same grader-error record, whose state, spend and wording are unchanged. `runPlan` appends that record, releases the in-flight hook, then throws, naming the run, the folder, the code, the spend and the results file; `main`'s existing catch exits 1. A grader error with its evidence on disk still lets the schedule go on, and the probe path is unchanged, now pinned by one check. The README's stop condition 6 and Evidence section, the module header, the code map and the tree say so. Five new rows, R-1500 to R-1504, each with one check in orchestration-paths' new section 8c; R-542 flips to implemented and cites nine checks, the four new ones that carry its marker and five existing ledger and results checks that gained it.

**Evidence.** Red first, by the seat on macOS against 2a96090's runner: orchestration-paths passes 87 and fails 4 (R-1500 and R-1503 at 4 spawns, R-1501 with no error, R-1502 with 4 rows); the R-1504 and probe checks pass there, as planned pins. The tip passes 91 of 91; integration 48, ops 48 and plan 57 pass unchanged. CI run 39271830210 on the lane merged with main: Windows passes all six new checks natively and reads 0 failing of 154 files (133 pass, 21 skip); there Node's message names only the blocking folder, and the stop still names the run's full folder. Ubuntu's one failing file is main's path-spelling, which linux-path-spelling fixed on main. macOS's re-run, after the runner cancelled the first attempt, reads 0 failing of 154 files (7,509 checks pass). install-check passes on macOS and Windows.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan and one adversary, who amended it: a check that strips Node's message so a fix forwarding only that message fails on macOS, the asset-copy row R-1503, the R-1504 boundary that a stop-on-every-grader-error fix fails, and the tree test's leaf total. Claude Sonnet 5 built it from one brief, the checks committed before the fix. Known limits: a resume skips the stopping run; the asset-fault run still writes no evidence; no test drives `main`'s exit 1. One final reviewer, Opus.

## 2026-10-19 — the product report lists the tests behind each requirement

Spend served the current priority's queue: the rest of the owner's 2026-10-16 feedback, here test details in the product report. A row's card showed its evidence as one monospace string. Per test, the owner could not see what it checks, open it off this machine, or tell whether it ran and passed here. The page's FEAT-039 detail had 0 test-file links and 0 list items.

**The change.** `scripts/product-report.mjs` gives every row its cited tests: each citation with `::`, a title and a repository path, TypeScript or Python. Each item shows the title and the file. When the origin remote is a GitHub repository, in any of its three spellings, the file links to its GitHub page at the report's commit, in a new tab. Beside it sits the file's label from the clone's runtime data (the last `npm run map:measure`), worded as the file's result, plus ` · changed since` when the file or any file it ran is gone or hashes differently; with no entry it reads `No recorded run`. A row citing no test says `Tests: none cited in this repository`. Links and labels are built in the model, because the renderer ships as source and may read only its arguments. Rows R-1510 to R-1515; R-1012 now also needs the origin and the runtime data unchanged.

**Evidence.** Each new check renders the page the command wrote, its renderer evaluated in a fresh `vm` context. Against the base's report code, the six new checks fail and the widened byte-identical check passes, as the plan predicted (the seat's probe). Per the builder's report, each listed mutation turned its named row red. CI run 39270074838, on the lane merged with main: product-report.test passes 63 of 63 on macOS, Ubuntu and Windows. The one failure on each leg is the tree test's leaf total, a merge artifact: the lane and main each wrote 1,248 for their own six rows. Ubuntu also fails main's path-spelling, since fixed on main. On the real page, R-1058, R-1063, R-1064, R-1330 and R-1340 are implemented, yet every test file they cite reads `SKIP · changed since`.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it twice over. A module constant inside the renderer passed every existing check but threw on the written page, so the checks now render that page. An injected data path hid an unwrapped git runner that would show `No recorded run` everywhere, so the tests write the fixture's own runtime data and also build from a linked worktree. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in one pass. Known limits: the label is per file and from this clone's platform only, CI legs and in-file SKIP lines are not read, and links go to GitHub only. One final reviewer, Opus.

## 2026-10-19 — the export rule says a pi extension's default export counts as its named entry point

So every pi extension in the pack broke the written rule. The 2026-10-09 audit (C08) filed it as wording plus a template tag: the standard was wrong, not the code.

G-005 stays `untested`, its evidence unchanged. The tree's leaf total moved from 1,242 to 1,243.
