The builder's wrong-code tries: dropping the words from each copy turns R-1480 red naming that copy, 3 of 3; changing G-005 in `REQUIREMENTS.md` alone turns R-756 red. CI run 39268120561 on b02048e, which GitHub merged onto main 4be0b79: Windows 132 PASS, 0 FAIL, 21 SKIP of 153 files, the first Windows run with 0 failing files, since main now carries win-leg-blocking's skip list; macOS 151 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 SKIP; Ubuntu 1 FAIL, path-spelling, main's Linux regression that linux-path-spelling fixes. install-check passes on macOS and Windows. win-skill-occupied's list of shipped requirements-skill versions needs no new entry: every copy installed since that lane carries its marker.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it: it cut a default-export scanner and three of its four rows, because the ruling asked for wording, the scanner's checks passed with the carve-out absent, and about 30 tests already load each extension's default export. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in one pass. ## 2026-10-19 — every new session starts with nana's standing rulings

Spend served the current priority's queue line for the rest of the owner's 2026-10-16 feedback and the continuity study: the rulings file every seat reads at startup, for both seats on macOS and Windows. Nana's standing rulings lived in the Claude Code seat's own memory and in scattered HANDOFF lines. A pi seat has no shared memory, so it never saw them, and a cold session or a builder had to rediscover them.

**The change.** One file, `packages/nana-pack/rules/nana-rulings.md`: a heading, one scope sentence telling a builder or reviewer to act only within its brief, then 19 dated rulings (2,404 bytes), naming no owner, no work project and no model. Install links it as a fifth Claude rule, and links it into pi's active agent folder too, copying it on Windows; doctor and the state manifest know both, and uninstall removes only its own links. A new pack extension, `nana-rulings`, reads the installed copy on every `session_start` reason through the bounded reader (cap 8,192 bytes, strict UTF-8) and sets its own `nana-rulings` prompt section. A missing, empty, non-regular, over-cap or non-UTF-8 copy gives one `RULINGS UNAVAILABLE:` line that names the file and tells the user to run `nana-setup install`. The shared runtime table names the new rule and extension. Rows R-1410 to R-1418 in FEAT-001.

**Evidence.** CI at 9c993df: macOS 0 failing of 154 files (7,493 checks); Ubuntu fails only lane-write-guard and path-spelling, both main's; Windows fails only 16 desk and 4 bench files. The lane's checks pass on all three legs; on Windows rulings-install passes 5 and skips its 3 POSIX link shapes with a reason. The seat's wrong-code pairs went 28 of 29 red on build 1, then 29 of 29 after build 2. The independent check held on 12 of 13 probes, including install, re-install, Windows copies over links, uninstall and the install check.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan and one adversary, who added the scope sentence row, narrowed the marker row and made the install test use a pi folder outside the home's default one. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in two passes. Build 2 fixed the independent check's MUST: the new extension had turned the runtime-table check red on all three legs. It also pinned the marker's file name and repair command, the one pair that survived, and gated three POSIX-only checks on a Windows host. Opus gives the final review and Fable the land review.

## 2026-10-19 — every bench test file runs and passes on Windows

Spend served the current priority's Windows CI leg: the test-portability line of the Windows fixes. win-leg-blocking had declared four bench files Windows skips, hiding about 210 checks that pass there. The bench README says every bench test runs on win32.

Against another lane's run on the same base, Windows gains 4 passing files and 223 passing checks. macOS reads 151 PASS, 0 FAIL; Ubuntu's one failure is path-spelling, main's Linux regression that linux-path-spelling fixes. install-check passes on macOS and Windows.

## 2026-10-19 — the folder-spelling test's letter-case checks skip on a case-sensitive volume

Spend served the current priority's CI legs. Since win-review-paths landed, CI's Ubuntu leg failed path-spelling's two case-divergence checks, GP1 and GP2 (rows R-1350 and R-1351), on every run; main's run 39251730146 on 73f39d5 read `9 pass, 2 fail, 11 skip`. Each check renames `Docs/` to `docs/` through git, then resets the index to the old commit. On a case-insensitive volume that leaves one folder whose case on disk differs from git's. On a case-sensitive volume it leaves two different folders, so the `--out` file is honestly untracked and the tools rightly admit it. The old guard (`diskName === "docs"`) held on both kinds of volume, so it never skipped.

**The change.** Each check now runs only when the old spelling `Docs` still reaches the renamed folder on disk; otherwise it prints `SKIP GP1 (case-divergence): this volume is case-sensitive, so the rename left two different folders` (and the GP2 twin) and runs no check. The guard asks the volume, not `process.platform`. Tests only; no product file and no row changed.

**Evidence.** On a case-sensitive APFS volume on macOS, the builder ran both checks without the new guard and saw them fail with the Ubuntu messages; with it, both skip. CI on the merged tip (run 39256629338): Ubuntu passes path-spelling with `9 pass, 13 skip`, the two fails now skips; its one failing file is lane-write-guard, main's, fixed on main since. macOS reads 0 failing of 152 files. Windows passes path-spelling with both case-divergence checks running; its 20 failing files are main's desk and bench. The Ubuntu log shows the skips by count only: the suite runner prints a file's SKIP lines only when no check in it passed.

**Plan and build.** The seat's must-meet plan, with no adversary file. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in one pass. One final reviewer, Opus.
## 2026-10-19 — Windows install refuses a requirements skill folder it did not make

Spend served the current priority's installation on Windows. On macOS, install refuses a real folder at the requirements skill path that it did not make and leaves it alone. On Windows, where the skill is a copy, install backed up the owner's SKILL.md inside that folder and overwrote it; the dry run exited 0, and doctor read ✓ afterwards. The test declared it a known bug.

**The change.** One predicate, `ownSkillCopy` in `packages/nana-setup/lib/steps.mjs`, decides whether a folder is nana-setup's copy: it holds the new `.nana-setup-copy` marker, it is byte-equal to the pack, or its SKILL.md (CRLF read as LF) matches one of the 13 versions the pack shipped before the marker (`lib/skill-copy-history.json`). Install and its dry run refuse any other folder before any write, with macOS's message, and doctor reads ✗ with the move fix. Install writes the marker with constant text after each copy, so a second run still changes nothing. Seven must-meet checks (rows R-1420 to R-1426): six new in the skills-and-standards test's section 4b, and one tag on the existing stale-copy refresh.

**Evidence.** CI's Windows leg ran all seven natively and passed them (the file at 55 pass, 4 skip; 20 of 152 files still fail there, all desk and bench). macOS reads 0 failing of 152 files; Ubuntu's two failing files are main's. The seat's wrong-code pairs turned 10 of 12 red: one survivor, the marker's part in the step's report, has no check, and one is equivalent at this tip. An independent check probed ten folders on 04c14e8 and found the change holds: the owner's bytes and line endings stay intact, a copy with a byte-order mark and an empty folder are refused with nothing lost, and the macOS path is unchanged. Of its two known bugs, the list going stale after tree-briefs is settled by the recompute; the other, a comment on the link handling, is a residual.

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan and one adversary, who added the seventh row: a copy nana-setup made must keep refreshing after any future SKILL.md change. After tree-briefs changed the requirements SKILL.md on main, the merge recomputed the list with one new key. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in one pass. Final review by Opus and land review by Fable.

## 2026-10-19 — pi-review and pi-worker end an attempt as soon as pi exits

Spend served the current priority's review and builder launchers, which both seats run on macOS and Windows. pi-watchdog, behind pi-review and pi-worker, noticed pi's exit only at its next poll (15 s by default), so every attempt ended up to a poll late and a run of instant failures waited a full poll each. Worse, a pi killed by a signal the watchdog did not send (SIGKILL, or the SIGABRT Node raises when it runs out of memory) hung the after-loop wait on a `close` event that had already fired. pi-review then exited 13 ("unsettled top-level await") without retrying and left its review checkout and reservation for the next launch to reclaim. pi-worker `--lane` left `nana-lane.lock` with the dead pid, and the next lane run in that worktree was refused; nothing reclaimed that lock.

**The change.** Only `runOnce` in `packages/nana-pack/bin/pi-watchdog.mjs` changed. One `ended` promise per attempt settles on pi's `exit` or `error` event; each poll races its timer, the signal wait and `ended`, then clears the timer, so pi-review does not live out the poll; the after-loop wait is `await ended`. The checks after a wake keep their order, and stall, ceiling, signal handling and retries are unchanged. The pack README's watchdog paragraph gains one sentence. Six new rows, R-1390 to R-1395, each with one check in the new `watchdog-exit.test.mjs`.

**Evidence.** The plan's probes, main against the fix: pi-review closed 4,854 ms after pi exited at `--poll 5` against 155 ms; three instant failures took 6,048 ms against 130 ms; an exit between polls ended the attempt 3,968 ms later against 6 ms. CI on the build tip: macOS 149 of 151 files pass with 0 failing; the new file passes 6 of 6 on macOS, Ubuntu and Windows, and every other failure on Ubuntu (1 file) and Windows (23 files) is main's own. The seat's eight wrong-code pairs all turn their checks red, 0 survived. The independent check holds with no MUST; its one known bug (a background child's output written after pi exits is no longer captured) is a carried residual, and the Windows leg verified its other residual (the signal checks on Windows). On Windows immutable-review took 27.9 s against about 78 s on other runs; the fix is the likely cause (inferred).

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan and one adversary, who amended it: a sixth check for the lane lock, WE1 timed from pi's own exit, and a sixth wrong-code pair that only the between-polls check catches. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in one pass. Opus reviews as the final reviewer and Fable as the land reviewer, under the power-mode lineup.

## 2026-10-19 — a Python project's readme check and code map print their whole report under any output encoding

Spend served the current priority's new projects on Windows. A Python project's `scripts/readme_check.py` and `scripts/code_map.py` printed through Python's default stdout, which raises UnicodeEncodeError at the first character the output encoding lacks. On a Windows pipe that encoding is the locale's code page, cp1252 on Western machines, so a README path or module name holding λ stopped the report partway. The adversary's probe at b3f6ec2: the readme check printed 1 of 2 problem lines in check mode and 21 of 25 lines in list mode; the code map check printed 0 of 3 problem lines; the impact view printed nothing and exited 1 where UTF-8 exits 0.

**The change.** Each script's main block now sets stdout's error handler to `backslashreplace` and keeps whatever encoding the caller asked for; an `isinstance` guard keeps mypy strict passing. A character the encoding lacks prints as its Python escape: λ prints as the six characters backslash-u-0-3-b-b. Four rows, R-1440 to R-1443, and four checks in the template render test run four commands under UTF-8, ASCII and cp1252 output. Each run must exit as the UTF-8 run does and print the UTF-8 output with each missing character escaped; the fourth check keeps UTF-8 output unescaped. CI run 39249884922, on the lane merged with main: Windows passes the four checks, 126 of 150 files pass and all 23 failures are main's; macOS reads 0 failing files; Ubuntu's one failure is main's lane-write-guard. Template acceptance passes on the tip (the seat, on macOS).

**Plan and build.** The seat's plan went to one adversary, which amended it: a JSON decode had turned the planned escape into a literal λ, which a correct fix would fail, and the comparator became one exact rule per encoding. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in one pass, the checks committed before the fix. Known limits: adopt mode and the code map's write mode stay untested here; under a cp1252 pipe, pi's post-edit shows a replacement mark for some characters; the TypeScript readme check claims only ASCII paths. One final reviewer, Opus.
## 2026-10-19 — one command changes one slot of a machine's roster

Spend served the current priority's queue: the one-command lineup change Jake queued after the Windows fixes, for both seats in terminal mode. Changing one roster slot meant a hand edit of the user-scope `nana-pack.json`: run `nana-roster --json`, paste its `roster` value into the file, keep every other key, then change the slot. Pasting the whole output beside the file's object made invalid JSON. D2 of the roster-notice change promised one command for this; the owner took it on 2026-10-16.

**The change.** `nana-roster set <slot>[:] <tokens>` takes the slot and the tokens a plain run prints, so a printed line pasted after `set` changes nothing. It checks the new roster through the existing resolver, keeps every other top-level member, copies the old file to `nana-pack.json.bak`, and renames a fully written temp file from the real target's folder over it, through a symlink. It refuses, changing no byte, on malformed input, on a file the resolver refuses, and inside a pi model shell (`PI_SESSION_ID` set). It loads only through a dynamic import, so the hook, the extension and pi-worker start as before. Rows R-1400 to R-1407; R-1123 and R-1192 now exclude `set` from their WHEN.

**Evidence.** On main, the plan's probes ran six of the eight checks red; R-1403 was red only on its `set refused:` prefix, and R-1405 only because its lib did not exist. The eight pass locally and in CI on macOS and Ubuntu; on Windows they pass with one POSIX-only case skipped, and the symlink check runs. CI's macOS leg reads 0 failing of 151 files (7,468 checks). Ubuntu and Windows fail only main's known files.

## 2026-10-18 — the review tools' tests on Windows, and the short-path bug under them

Spend served the current priority's pi seat on Windows: the review and builder launchers and the review ledger. Their three test files stopped early on Windows: two died at module load on a raw `D:\` path handed to `import()`, and the third after 10 of 52 checks.

**The bug under it.** The seat's re-triage of the nine maintainer test files found product bugs in the land and review tools. They compare a typed or temp path with git's path as plain text. Windows CI's temp folder is a short 8.3 name, `RUNNER~1`, while git prints the long name. So on Windows a tracked review output is not refused, one repository gets two ledger keys, and a dead review checkout is never reclaimed. nana-land also refuses real lands and cleanups. A probe on this Mac showed the same code failing for a wrongly cased typed path, including one fail-open: a non-LAND verdict recorded under the other spelling is invisible to nana-land, which then merges. This lane keeps the short temp path on purpose, so those checks fail on Windows and print both spellings. The next lane, a gate lane, fixes the tools.

**Plan and build.** The seat's re-triage ran one agent per failing file, then a cut and one adversary. Claude Sonnet 5 built it in two passes, each followed by read-only checks. ## 2026-10-18 — the pi seat's handoff, display and post-edit tests on Windows

Spend served the current priority's pi seat on Windows: the handoff store and artifact, the staleness pointer, the trust checks, the display surfaces, the post-edit queue and the frontier checker. Their seven test files failed on Windows, and two of them stopped before their end.

**The change.** Tests only, no new row. Journal lines are parsed as JSON before their paths are compared, since JSON escapes each Windows backslash. Expected paths use the platform separator, and the expected pointer uses `~\` where Windows writes it; pi's own resolver expands that form. Temp homes are resolved to their long names, because Windows hands out short 8.3 names and the product will not shorten a path holding `~`. The post-edit queue's log path travels through an environment variable, not script source. Hostile names NTFS refuses use a Unicode line separator and a bidi override instead. The long-home block, the tab, CR and LF names and the POSIX lock-out cases skip on Windows, each with its reason.

**Plan and build.** Claude Sonnet 5 built it in four passes against CI's Windows leg on a draft pull request. Run 1: four of seven files passed. Brief 2 blamed the display test's `\\?\` prefix on its temp home, and run 2 proved that wrong. Build 3 found the source: `fs.mkdirSync` with `recursive` returns the first folder it made, and on Windows that path comes back in the `\\?\C:\` form, which cmd.exe refuses as a working folder. Run 3 left one check, whose expected JSON locator did not escape backslashes. Run 4 passed all seven files. The reviewer then sent the lane back once: the store test skipped its dangling-link cases on Windows as "POSIX symlinks", but Windows has symlinks. Build 5 runs them there, and run 5 passed all ten. CI's Windows failing count fell from 49 of 150 files to 38. One reviewer.

Build 3 found the holder: the test had moved its own working folder into its temp home and never moved back, and Windows will not remove a process's working folder. Run 3 passed all five files. One reviewer.

**Lessons.** (1) An EBUSY that survives retries is a live holder, not a slow release; look for the process's own working folder first. (2) A test that changes its working folder must restore it before cleaning up.

## 2026-10-17 — project files on Windows

Spend served the current priority's Windows fixes (Jake moved them up on 2026-10-17); this is the first of the Windows plan's lanes.

**The change.** `CLAUDE.md` is a regular one-line file, `@AGENTS.md`, on every system: Claude Code imports a repo-relative `@path` with no prompt. nana-pi's own `CLAUDE.md` changes from a symbolic link to that file; git for Windows had checked the link out as a 9-byte text file reading `AGENTS.md`, so Claude Code on Jake's Windows clone loaded no project instructions. `nana-setup project` writes the import file for a new project, `--check` accepts it or an existing link and flags the 9-byte file, and a refresh replaces that file and never writes through a link. The AGENTS.md working-region refresh closes its handle before the rename and retries a refused rename briefly, and it compares file identity with 64-bit stats. CI's Windows suite run is verbose. Rows R-1310 to R-1313.

**Plan and build.** Claude Sonnet 5 built it in five passes, each driven by one Windows CI run on a draft pull request. Run 1: the refresh passed on Windows; the project test crashed on a bare `npm` spawn. Run 2: a raw path handed to `import()`. Run 3: 112 of 121, eight of them line-ending failures, which the line-endings lane then fixed, and one test that hard-coded a forward slash. Run 4: the project test passed 121 of 121, and the owner-swap check showed the refresh overwriting a file its owner had replaced: Windows file IDs are 64-bit, and as JavaScript numbers two different files can compare equal. Build 5 compares them as 64-bit integers; run 5 and a rerun of its Windows job passed every target. sol's final round then found one more data-loss case: the rename retry did not re-check `AGENTS.md`, so an editor's save between two attempts would be overwritten. Build 6 re-checks identity and bytes before every retry, with a test that injects one refused rename while the owner rewrites the file. Round 2 found the re-check ran before the 20 ms delay; build 7 moved the delay first, so the re-check runs just before each retry's rename. A save in the instant between that re-check and the rename can still be overwritten: Windows has no atomic compare-and-replace, so it is a known limit.

**Lessons.** (1) Verbose CI output paid for itself on its first run: every round after it named its cause. (2) An intermittent Windows check is a bug report, not noise: runs 1 and 3 passed, runs 2 and 4 failed, and the cause was a data-loss guard that silently did not work there. (3) Fix the shared cause first: the line-endings lane cleared eight of this lane's failures.

**The change.** One must-meet test, R-1330: on Windows, the installed knowledge hook command, run exactly as settings.json holds it with a prompt that matches a fixture store, prints a pull block naming the fixture file. The second run passed all seven target files; the Windows failing count fell from 62 to 56 of 150. One reviewer.
