- t3-carry `535c155` and t3-carry2 `9a28cce` — `nana-setup project` refreshes only the marker-owned AGENTS.md region (atomic, byte-exact outside the markers); doctor checks memory links. The first version made doctor fail on dangling links the memory system allows; the follow-up lane made them informational.

**Wave B.**
- t3-launcher `6002186` — `pi-worker --lane` builds the whole pi argv from one sealed roster value, refuses caller arguments, retries and duplicate options, holds a per-worktree lock, revalidates before the spawn, and the gate keeps edit/write inside the worktree (unresolvable entries fail closed).
- t3-land `c383b9e` — `nana-land` lands only a revision with a LAND round on record, runs the suite, does one ff-only merge, verifies branch, ref and files, and cleans up without force. It landed itself, then every later lane. The seat's own compare-and-swap ruling left the checkout stale; astra caught it and the next round subtracted it.
- t3-checks `1e6f032` and the seat's `.pi/nana-pack.json` — nana-pi's own post-edit checks (`node --check`; a strip-then-compile TypeScript check that never runs the file). The seat probed first: `node --check` rejects valid TypeScript.
- The canary `14a1384` — one real change through the whole loop: lane-mode build, post-edit caught a seeded syntax error in the builder's own turn, sol reviewed inside the immutable checkout, the ledger recorded the round, `nana-land` landed and cleaned up. One manual bridge: the suite-lock wrapper lives outside the repo.

**Builder comparison (Jake: "if anything use sonnet 5.5").** The land-helper lane was built twice from one brief — pi luna and a Sonnet 5.5 Claude subagent — and reviewed blind: luna 4 → 6 → 6.5, 8 blocking findings, 110 builder-minutes; Sonnet 4 → 7 → 6, 9 blocking, 126 minutes, twice the tests. A tie; luna stays. A classification of all 221 review findings from tranches 1–3 (`docs/reviews/hardening-round-causes-2026-10-07/`) put about two in three blocking findings on the builder, one in six on the seat, one in six on unnamed edge cases.

**Found on the machine.** The Codex app re-imports the legacy skills into `~/.agents/skills` each night; leaked test processes (a desk test server alive 29 hours) were killed; the objective-golden timing flake recurred under load.

**Lessons.** (1) Probe a tool's behaviour before a ruling depends on it (git `update-ref` then `merge --ff-only` leaves files stale; `node --check` rejects TypeScript). (2) A guard hook abstains on a miss; `allow` is a permission grant. (3) Subtract exemptions on a floor rather than refining them — it converged twice this tranche. (4) Proportional stopping: on low-risk lanes, rare edge cases after three rounds become declared residuals. (5) Brief one item per round when a builder stops early; "wrong code first" and "fix the class" are now in the preamble.

(5) When a measurement feeds a decision, the land reviewer's question is "same unit?" — astra's catch on t2-trial was worth its round.

Jake asked for a hardening plan with "ultracode", then: "Go, and then go with your recommendations on all, pi luna high effort worker, sol reviewer, astra land review, you as seat, fable for seat adversarial consultation only."

**Audit and plan.** Fifteen read-only Opus 5.5 auditors (ten planned lanes, five gap lanes); Fable checked the first 122 findings until its weekly limit, then — at Jake's correction ("Use pi sol... That's always been the independent reviewer we used") — gpt-5.6-sol through `pi-worker` verified all 165 blind: 156 kept, 9 dropped. Plan `docs/hardening-plan-2026-10-06.md`, corpus `docs/reviews/hardening-audit-2026-10-06/`. Five root causes: two nana generations installed side by side; no trust decision recorded (post-edit inert, objectives labelled untrusted); the builder loop not an artifact; rulings stranded where nana-pi sessions cannot read them; heavy and partly wrong instruction surfaces. Seat acts the same day: main pushed and `v0.6.3` tagged (D9), the local `git-checkpoint.ts` retired (D10), the nana-dev-kit installer guarded against writing through pack links, HANDOFF rewritten to its drop rule (3,656 → 947 words), the 09-28 rulings archived here, one dated roster in shared memory, the umbrella's appetite line added.

**Tranche 1 (six lanes, all landed and pushed; builder pi gpt-6-luna high, slice review sol, land review astra, Fable consult before launch).** Fable's consult caught four setup flaws before any builder ran (watchdog would kill lock-waiting builders; the land review would hit the round cap; the live gate would block the gate lane's own commands; conflicted merges in the live tree) — so lanes land through an integration worktree and main moves `--ff-only` between green suites.
- t1-docs `eee71f9` — fresh-clone suite green; the truncated AGENTS rule restored and guarded; runner-specific map commands; front-door text.
- t1-ledger `a968145` — line-start `VERDICT` shape; redirects into the reviewed tree refused; cap identity from `--tree`; signal cleanup that cannot hang. Astra's land round caught a child-cwd regression; one audited over-cap round.
- t1-flake `c2b5db8` — the intermittent stage-key failure was cross-talk between test instances on fixed app ports; desk app listeners now accept port 0 and report bound ports; the runner prints a red file's failing lines. Desk restarted.
- t1-setup `1252cc6` — registration coverage by manifest dirs; desk lifecycle (bootstrap then kickstart, failures exit 1, doctor needs a running service); exact-shape knowledge-hook migration. Astra caught a test that rewrote the repo's own manifests. The live `install --desk` then hit a launchd bootstrap race (error 5) and left the desk down for about a minute; the seat recovered it; the fix is in tranche 2.
- t1-session `f8c4795` — adoption counts the complete nana structure and skips temp roots (nana-pi no longer nags itself); prompt sections via `systemPromptOptions` in one canonical order (live probe: persisted in order); notify on approval prompts; OSC only in TUI; one path resolver.
- t1-gate `00db666` — anchored policy regex; a code-loading floor for pi's own config with symlinked targets and a fail-closed walk cap; one bounded reader; headless recovery lines. Twelve builder rounds. The rm-text relaxation was SUBTRACTED after sol found destructive bypasses in two straight rounds; the template-source exception was subtracted after astra showed a traversal bypass; old behaviour is preserved by composition (the pre-lane matcher, verbatim, runs first). Live probe: a headless `rm -rf` is refused with the new recovery line.

**Found on the machine.** `os.tmpdir()` held 268,018 entries: tests leaked a temp dir per case for months (one test alone 110,985 pairs) and `pi-watchdog` leaked a capture dir per attempt. A deleted-cwd test slowed from 12 s to over 300 s. The seat removed the stale test-prefixed entries; t2-tests isolates temp per test file by construction.

**Lessons.** (1) On a security floor every relaxation became a bypass under review and every tightening held: subtract relaxations; preserve old behaviour by composition, never by re-implementing it "verbatim". (2) A small builder's "mutation proof" inverts the assertion unless the brief forbids it: the mutation must break the code. (3) A lane's findings list must hold only what it closes — attached context findings are held against the lane by reviewers. (4) A test that compares against `main` compares the code with itself once it lands: pin an immutable commit. (5) Machine-wide state (temp dirs, the review ledger, the live desk) belongs in the land checklist, not only the code.

## 2026-10-05 (evening) — pi durable, Karpathy's other formats, the code map sees the tests

Jake asked how pi's durable agent helps us and where Karpathy's other output formats fit beyond the writing trial. The seat found pi-durable is a separate experimental library, not what the pi CLI runs on. Jake parked it. He held HTML report pages until the writing trial ends. He approved code-drawn diagrams, "but compare to our code map first".

**The comparison found a real defect.** The map's TypeScript reader skipped `import(new URL("../x", import.meta.url).href)`, the form 54 of 95 test files use. A blast-radius diagram drawn then would have shown most changes as untested. Jake: "Fix the map."

**Lane.** A Sonnet worker built it in `feat/map-test-links`. Links rose from 144 to 245; code modules with no test link fell from 56 to 27 of 77; `--impact` now prints the untraced-test count (13 of 95) in both templates. astra blocked three rounds (7, 6, 6). Round 1 found comments and strings making links. That bug was already on main. The seat had the worker add a lexer, then a comment-line guard; each lost real imports, and astra's rounds 2 and 3 were almost entirely about them. Both were subtracted. Fable ruled LAND 8/10.

**Lessons.** A filter on a regex reader that "can only drop matches" is exactly how it loses real edges; the seat claimed it was safe and was wrong twice. A lost edge is worse than a spurious one, so the pre-existing spurious class stays, documented, with the `ignore` list as the remedy. The seat also wasted round 1's ledger record by redirecting the review log into the reviewed tree.

Jake: a full walk-through of nana-pi, spawning real pi processes where needed, with the docs and READMEs brought up to date. Four Sonnet auditors ran in parallel: the pack in real pi sessions, setup with the hooks, knowledge and templates, the desk with stage and bench, and the docs as a whole. Each worked under one shared rules file.

- no 1.0 addendum in the landscape doc;
- AGENTS.md's "Working under nana-pi" diverged from the shared file it calls canonical;
- a wrong citation source;
- stale HANDOFF lines.

R-858 is `untested` because the suite's checks skip without the tools. The shared file is now the verified single truth; AGENTS.md holds a byte copy, pinned by R-859. sol r1 BLOCK 6 found the shared text still stated defaults as absolutes: handoff path, notify, the objective switch, two gate gaps. r2 BLOCK 7 found `map:impact --` in the generator and an over-categorical handoff sentence; the seat fixed both. r3 LAND 9. Suite 95/95, 5,780 checks.

The seat found a stray live desk session and stopped it (shared memory written). A byte-mirror test proves two texts agree, not that either is true; only the reviewer's sentence-by-sentence read against code caught that.

## 2026-10-05 — the edge desk on pi's built-in MCP; the lock-file advice

Spend served the current priority: it removed the last pi-mcp-adapter consumer and closed a `violated` row. Roles: Sonnet worker in two worktrees, gpt-6-astra through `pi-review`, the seat (Opus 5.5) briefed, verified and landed.

**Lock-file advice.** R-760 was `violated`: it forbade "move it aside" for an obstructed lock as well as a held one. The code was right. An obstructed lock path is never a running pi's lock, and pi never clears it. The seat narrowed the row and pinned it by mutation. astra r1 then found a real unsafe case behind it: a genuinely held proper-lockfile lock acquired under a restrictive umask is unreadable (mode 333), and nana called it obstructed and advised moving it. Fixed (staleness decides; an unreadable fresh folder is held). astra r2 found the new remedies claimed more than the check knows. A stale unreadable empty folder is reclaimed by pi, yet the advice said pi fails. Fixed with uncertainty-aware remedies (R-856/R-857), with the readable remedies byte-identical over 144 combinations.

**Edge desk.** The probe found the brief's argv could not work: `--no-extensions` disables pi's built-in extensions too, so a server registered from `-e` never connects. `-e builtin:mcp` (pi's own grammar) fixes it. The worker built past the brief's stop condition; the seat accepted it because it is documented pi behaviour and kept the trust posture. The app's own extension registers the server, as basketball does. Blocks now arrive at `structuredContent.structuredContent.blocks`. nana-stage reads them there and drops the raw carrier by never returning `structuredContent`; the adapter path and its overflow branch are gone. The desk accepts `builtin:<name>` extension entries (R-943/R-944). astra r1 BLOCK 7 → r2 BLOCK 8 → r3 LAND 9. Live: the quiesce-first runbook, then a real prompt staged two signed blocks with no raw carrier.

**Lessons.** (1) A wrapper log redirected into the reviewed tree voids the round: the snapshot changes mid-review (memory updated). (2) A running service that caches config at load needs a stop before the checkout changes, not a restart after: astra showed a mixed old-manifest/new-stage window. (3) `launchctl bootstrap` loaded the desk without starting it; `kickstart` started it. (4) A classification fix can make a remedy overclaim: test the advice text, not just the label.

## 2026-10-04 — pi 1.0 adopted, subagents fixed at the cause, the writing trial started

Spend served the current priority (consistent, coherent, effective nana-pi experience). Roles were declared at the start by Jake: Sonnet workers, gpt-6-astra reviews through pi-review, Fable on architecture and landing, Opus 5.5 as the seat (`docs/reviews/pi-1.0-2026-10-04/ROLES.md`).

**pi 0.87.1 → 1.0.2.** The compatibility audit found nothing in our consumers that breaks. The source-read worry, codemode scripts bypassing the gate, was disproven: nested `ctx.executeTool()` calls go through the same `tool_call`/`tool_result` hooks. Full suite 89/89 (5,492 checks) on both versions. pi-mcp-adapter was removed for pi's built-in MCP (`memory` server `exposure: direct`, `autoEnableCodemode: false`); the edge desk app loads the adapter by path and nana-stage reads its result shape, so it keeps a vendored copy in `~/.pi/agent/apps/vendor/` — no audit or ruling saw that consumer; the desk log did.

**Subagents.** Jake: one subagent at a time, and it occupies the main agent. The diagnosis worker said no real session existed; a re-scan found the one that did (basketball-geek, 09-03). The launch WAS background. The builtin `reviewer` has no shell and its prompt sends it to the parent for git/test output — three supervisor requests in six minutes made the main agent the child's shell operator. Fix: `nana-setup` seeds pi-subagents' config (`forceTopLevelAsync`, `maxSubagentDepth: 1`) and a nana-owned `reviewer.md` that shadows the builtin and runs read-only evidence commands itself; doctor owns the floor (pi-subagents 0.75.0 — background children fail on pi 1.0 below it). astra r1 BLOCK 7 → r2 BLOCK 8 → r3 LAND 9; Fable LAND. Acceptance over RPC: the gate blocked `rm -rf` inside a background child (journal pid ≠ parent), the reviewer ran `git show` itself with zero asks, three reviewers ran in parallel while the main agent answered "Four" in 1.2 s.

**Karpathy.** Jake meant the 2026-10-02 post only (outputs are now the bottleneck; write in ASD-STE100, then diagrams, HTML, video). Research in `research/karpathy-x-2026-10-04.md`. The seat proposed EARS for rows — Jake: "arent we already doing EARS?" — yes, since 10-02; the miss was not reading memory first. Gap found instead: nothing checks the EARS form, and about 200 of 507 nana-pi rows carry more than one `shall`.

**Writing trial.** One rule (BLUF + STE-80% + one fact per sentence) for text Jake reads, delivered to pi by a seventh pack extension (Fable's Amendment 1 after astra showed an agent-dir `AGENTS.md` link hides a user's `CLAUDE.md`), measured by a report-only checker. astra r1 BLOCK 6 (newline splitting made the measure depend on wrapping: 4% vs 29%), r2 BLOCK 8 (a 64 MiB rule or a FIFO could crash or hang pi), r3 BLOCK 8 at the cap; the seat re-ran r3's probes, all closed; Fable LAND. Baseline: seat reports 31% over 25 words, verdict first 6/35. Stop 2026-10-18 or 20 reports.

**Later the same day — setup bugs and the EARS-form lane.** Jake: verify the three setup bugs the acceptance reviewers flagged, add a form check, and split the rows that make more than one promise; push once at the end. The setup bugs were all real (a vanished link crashed doctor; `--home "$HOME"` touched the live machine; writes went through a symlink, and the fix exposed a desk restart on a skipped plist). astra r1 BLOCK 7 → r2 BLOCK 8, closed by execution. The EARS lane: batch 0 built the one-`shall` check in both template rails (astra three rounds: backtick runs, a Unicode-version boundary drift between Node 22 and Python 3.14, a Python IGNORECASE fold; final contract ASCII-only, swept over all 1,114,112 codepoints, 0 differences). Five split batches took 200 origins into 244 rows, off form 194 → 0. The honest-status half did not converge: worker-claimed `implemented` rows had an uncovered condition in 58.7% (A3) and 79.5% (B) of cases even with recorded mutations, because a worker picks a mutation its test catches. Fable switched B and C to promote nothing; the seat missed that trigger for B and corrected it before landing (now a shared memory). 214 rows sit `untested` with their uncovered words as Open question 6b's worklist.

**Lessons.** (0) A ruler's "what would change my mind" trigger is part of the decision; check it after every measurement. (1) A surprising negative from a worker ("no real session exists") is a claim; the per-call re-scan changed the diagnosis and the fix. (2) Removing a package needs a consumer search beyond the repo — the edge app config lived in the agent dir. (3) Mutation runs by the reviewer, then by the worker before the next round, turned "tests exist" into "tests catch the break" in both lanes.

## 2026-10-02 — requirements-first promoted into the toolkit, then the toolkit put under its own rules

Spend served the umbrella priority directly (make the nana-pi experience consistent, coherent and effective): what was proven in aml-desk on 10-01/02 now ships from the templates, the skill and the rule, and nana-pi itself lives under it.

**Promotion (two Opus lanes, disjoint ownership).** Templates: `REQUIREMENTS.md.jinja` with the shared Part G block (G-001–G-012), the `req:` comment-marker rail in both languages (one convention; `req-candidate:` in adopt mode), `scripts/code-map.mjs` + `scripts/code_map.py` (`--check`, `--impact`, `exempt`, `testRoots`/`layerExempt`, `ignore`, `runner`), `scripts/readme-check.{mjs,py}` (G-012: commands, paths, sections, flags, completeness), post-edit entries, doc seeds. Skill `requirements` (one source; nana-setup symlinks it into `~/.claude/skills`), rule `nana-standards.md`, installer + doctor + tests, cross-references in spec/scaffold/adopt.

**Review.** gpt-5.6-sol r1 (adversarial) BLOCK: CRITICAL the win32 skill mirror followed a directory symlink and wrote into its target; HIGH adopt mode shipped a false ledger with no rail; HIGH "every module" mapped only src; MEDIUM nested tests invisible, implemented without a citation, Python dynamic imports silent. All fixed by two fixers with mutation proofs (victim byte-identical; adopt rows all untested; header drop in tests/ red; nested unknown id red; evidence `—` red; non-literal import named). Friction: the reviewer wrote the `--out` file itself and the wrapper overwrote it with the final message — body recovered from the pi session jsonl; procedure memory updated (never instruct the reviewer to write the file).

**Self-hosting.** Three read-only extraction passes (pack+runner 258 rows, knowledge/stage/setup 108, desk/bench 103) → `REQUIREMENTS.md` 481 rows, 499 after the clause audit; the pack's overflow block moved to R-700+ before publication. Three header passes over 70 modules (one `*/` inside a header value broke the test runner — caught by `node --check`, now fixed); three bench modules exempt (content-pinned by a closed study; re-pinning would alter committed evidence). Markers placed mechanically on 1,130 `check(` calls (the rail had to learn mid-line calls and `check` as a call name). Wiring worker: shims over the template modules, root map config (two layers), `docs/code-map.md` 73 modules (162 once the six test roots were mapped after r2), README check over seven READMEs — 380 first-run problems, ~360 of them extraction errors (machine paths, package specifiers, README-relative paths) → classifier fixed to the spec's words, README-dir resolution, `externalPaths` declared with reasons; the remaining real README errors fixed one by one (listed in the worker report; nana-stage README written). Four repo-level tests under `npm test` (rail, map, README, and an external test of the runner itself). **Sol r2 BLOCK** (inline README commands unchecked — 443 → 506 claims; implemented rows overclaiming clauses — a three-worker clause audit of all 416 implemented rows: 136 re-cited to the check that pins the clause, 17 split; `npm test` red under an ambient `NANA_HANDOFF=off` — the runner now scrubs `NANA_*`/`PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` from children; test roots unmapped — headers on 89 test modules, map 73 → 162). Full suite 83 → 89 files, 5,174 → 5,454 checks, green.

**Lessons.** (1) A reviewer told to "write the file" loses its review to the wrapper; capture the final message. (2) A header is code: a `*/` in a value closes the comment — syntax-check after a comments-only pass. (3) A README checker's first run is mostly its own extraction errors; fix classification to the spec's words before touching READMEs, and declare the genuine externals with reasons rather than weakening the check. (4) Three disjoint lanes → one independent reviewer → one fixer per finding set held up across 200+ files; the ledger is where workers overclaim, never the code — 58 of 85 sampled desk/bench rows had an unmarked adjacent check that pinned the missing clause: marker placement, not missing tests. (5) Never edit the reviewed tree while pi-review runs — r2's verdict was voided by my own HANDOFF edit and the round was consumed.

**r3 (last round): BLOCK on one class** — clause-level overclaim persists (fresh sample 16/25 fully pinned) while every round-1/2 fix re-verified. Landed with the residual recorded in REQUIREMENTS 6b and HANDOFF; nine sampled rows split. **Open:** tag `v0.6.0` for copier consumers; migrate basketball-geek's `@pytest.mark.req` to the comment marker when touched; aml-desk keeps its R-600 block.
